Bilingual edition · 中英对照

Phase Lock

相位锁定

A manifesto on brain-computer interfaces and preserving human agency in the age of AI.
一份关于脑机接口与在人工智能时代守护人类能动性的宣言。

Saturnin Pugnet（萨图尔南·皮涅） · October 2026（2026年10月）

原文为英文，摘自作者 PDF（phaselock.satpugnet.com，169 页）。本对照版逐段提供中文译文，专有名词、人名与术语在首次出现时保留原文。图表尽量按原位置插入。
English original extracted from the author’s PDF. Each passage is followed by a Chinese translation. Proper names are kept in the original on first appearance. Figures are placed at their original positions.

phase lock /feɪz lɒk/ n.
相位锁定（phase lock）/feɪz lɒk/ 名词
The exact threshold where two independent systems collapse into a single, unified frequency.
两个相互独立的系统坍缩为单一、统一频率的确切阈值。
In physics, the moment an oscillator binds its rhythm to an external force to preserve structural stability. In electronics, a circuit anchoring itself to a master reference to eliminate drift. In neuroscience, the spontaneous alignment of firing neurons to an outside stimulus.
在物理学中，指振荡器将自身节律绑定于外部作用力、以维持结构稳定的那一刻。在电子学中，指电路锚定于一个主参考信号以消除漂移。在神经科学中，指放电神经元与外部刺激之间自发形成的同步。
Humanity and AI are now about to enter Phase Lock.
人类与人工智能即将进入相位锁定。
Executive Summary
执行摘要
Reality is bending.
现实正在弯曲。
The future, which used to arrive in decades, now arrives by the month. Every day that passes feels faster and stranger than the previous one.
过去以数十年为单位到来的未来，如今按月到来。每过去一天，都比前一天更快、更陌生。
The intuitions our species built over millennia, at the slow pace of generations, are coming apart at a dizzying speed. Our economic utility, our role in society, and our very meaning in the universe are all being challenged; and as our philosophical anchors are fracturing one by one, we drift into the unknown.
我们这个物种在数千年间、以世代更替的缓慢节奏建立起来的直觉，正以令人眩晕的速度瓦解。我们的经济效用、我们在社会中的角色，乃至我们在宇宙中的意义本身，都在受到挑战；随着我们的哲学锚点一个接一个地断裂，我们正漂向未知。
Today, AIs write, code, reason, plan, and increasingly act on our behalf; and while humans are still in the loop, it feels like our presence is fading. As AI is gaining in agency by the day, one has to wonder: can humans still control machines?
今天，人工智能（AI）在写作、编程、推理、规划，并越来越多地代表我们采取行动；尽管人类仍处于决策回路之中，但我们的存在感似乎正在消退。随着 AI 的能动性与日俱增，人们不禁要问：人类还能控制机器吗？
At the center of this upheaval lies a growing mismatch between human cognition and machine intelligence, AI capability is racing ahead faster than human biology can keep up.
这场剧变的核心，是人类认知与机器智能之间日益扩大的错配：AI 能力的前进速度，已经超过了人类生物学所能跟上的速度。
This is what I call the bandwidth problem.
这就是我所说的“带宽问题”（bandwidth problem）。
As AI becomes more capable, our ability to direct, understand, and govern it does not scale with it. We have opened Pandora’s box and we are attempting to steer what has emerged through keyboards, screens, and clicks: interfaces designed for an earlier age and unequal to the task now before them.
随着 AI 能力增强，我们引导、理解和治理它的能力并没有随之扩展。我们已经打开了潘多拉的盒子，却还在试图通过键盘、屏幕和鼠标点击来驾驭从中涌现的东西：这些界面是为更早的时代设计的，无法胜任眼前的任务。
Unless we develop scalable ways to align humans and machines, human agency will steadily recede from the systems shaping the world. Either we align AI or we get left behind.
除非我们开发出可扩展的方式来对齐人与机器，否则人类的能动性将从塑造世界的系统中逐步退场。要么我们对齐 AI，要么我们被抛在身后。
There are two ways to do that: alignment-by-control, which constrains the machine from the outside, and alignment-by-integration, which couples human values and intents to the machine directly. Control can hold for the next few years, but ultimately, only integration will last.
实现这一点有两条路径：“控制式对齐”（alignment-by-control），即从外部约束机器；以及“融合式对齐”（alignment-by-integration），即把人类的价值观与意图直接耦合到机器之中。控制在未来几年内或许还能维持，但最终，只有融合才能长久。
The brief interval in which humanity can still determine the terms of its integration with AI before it grows too powerful to steer is the Phase Lock Window. Once it shuts, the choice is gone and humans are left permanently out of the loop, with no say in a future run by machine intelligence.
在 AI 变得过于强大而无法驾驭之前，人类仍能决定自身与 AI 融合条件的这段短暂时期，就是“相位锁定窗口”（Phase Lock Window）。一旦窗口关闭，选择便不复存在，人类将被永久排除在回路之外，对一个由机器智能主导的未来毫无发言权。
This manifesto explores a breakout technology that offers us a path to alignment-by-integration:
本宣言探讨的是一项突破性技术，它为我们提供了一条通往融合式对齐的道路：
Brain-computer interface.
脑机接口（brain-computer interface, BCI）。
BCI is one of the primary levers we have for aligning AI with humans, serving three simultaneous functions: it provides an architectural blueprint for an aligned intelligence (the brain), it supplies training signal so that AI learns human intents and values directly rather than inferring them from our cognitive artifacts, and it widens the bandwidth between mind and machine, and ultimately of the mind itself, so that humans can keep pace with AI.
BCI 是我们用以使 AI 与人类对齐的主要杠杆之一，它同时发挥三项功能：它为一种对齐的智能提供了架构蓝图（即大脑）；它提供训练信号，使 AI 能够直接学习人类的意图和价值观，而不是从我们的认知产物中去推断；它还拓宽了心智与机器之间的带宽，并最终拓宽心智本身的带宽，使人类能够跟上 AI 的步伐。
Even though it remains widely dismissed, BCI is approaching the same inflection point AI crossed roughly a decade ago. The existence proof is already here: researchers can decode speech, reconstruct mental imagery, and detect intent from raw brain activity. Before the decade is over, thoughts will turn into AI prompts, imagined scenes will materialize on screens, and robots will respond directly to mental commands; and a few years later, that connection will become bidirectional, making it possible to write thoughts or emotions directly to one’s mind.
尽管 BCI 至今仍普遍不被看好，它正在接近 AI 大约十年前跨越的那个拐点。存在性证明已经摆在眼前：研究人员能够从原始脑活动中解码语言、重建心理意象并检测意图。在这个十年结束之前，思想将变成 AI 提示词，想象中的场景将呈现在屏幕上，机器人将直接响应意念指令；再过几年，这种连接将变为双向的，使人们能够将思想或情绪直接写入大脑。
The question is no longer whether BCI can work, but how it will scale. The practical bet of this manifesto is that the same scaling laws that produced modern AI (better hardware, larger datasets and bigger models) apply just as well to brain decoding. By treating the brain as a vast, noisy, largely unlabeled dataset, AI can extract structure from signals that were previously too complex to decode; the ChatGPT moment of the brain is now around the corner.
问题已不再是 BCI 能否奏效，而是它将如何规模化。本宣言务实的押注是：造就现代 AI 的规模定律（scaling laws）——更好的硬件、更大的数据集和更大的模型——同样适用于脑解码。通过将大脑视为一个庞大、嘈杂、基本无标注的数据集，AI 能够从过去复杂到无法解码的信号中提取结构；大脑的“ChatGPT 时刻”已近在眼前。
The fastest path to execute this strategy will be through non-invasive BCI. Consumer devices can reach millions of people without surgery, generating the large and diverse neural datasets the field has been lacking. Consumer demand, paired with the growing value of neural data to frontier labs and robotics companies, will then finance further development, setting a flywheel in motion.
执行这一战略的最快路径将是非侵入式 BCI。消费级设备无需手术即可触达数百万人，从而产生该领域一直缺乏的大规模、多样化神经数据集。消费需求，加上神经数据对前沿实验室和机器人公司日益增长的价值，将为后续发展提供资金，从而启动一个飞轮。
This manifesto maps that transition: how BCI will evolve over the next decade, the role it will play in the AI race, why neural data will become a critical asset for frontier labs, and how neurotechnology as a whole will help humans preserve agency and oversight over increasingly powerful machines.
本宣言描绘了这一转变：BCI 在未来十年将如何演进、它将在 AI 竞赛中扮演什么角色、为什么神经数据将成为前沿实验室的关键资产，以及神经技术作为一个整体将如何帮助人类在面对日益强大的机器时保有能动性与监督权。
BCI and AI are now on a direct collision course. AI will give BCI its scaling playbook, its models, and its capital; and in turn, BCI will give AI a new training modality and a new interface.
BCI 与 AI 如今正处于一条直接相撞的轨道上。AI 将为 BCI 提供规模化的打法、模型和资本；反过来，BCI 将为 AI 带来一种新的训练模态和一种新的交互界面。
From this mutually reinforcing dynamic will emerge a new lever for controlling and aligning AI with humanity.
从这种相互强化的动态中，将涌现出一种控制 AI 并使其与人类对齐的新杠杆。
AI will scale BCI, and BCI will scale AI.
AI 将推动 BCI 的规模化，BCI 也将推动 AI 的规模化。
How we shape their convergence over the coming decade will define the centuries to come.
我们在未来十年如何塑造二者的汇合，将决定未来数个世纪的走向。

PART I.
第一部分
The Signal
信号（The Signal）
Can the brain be read?
大脑可以被读取吗？
01 What Is BCI?
01 什么是 BCI？
“The brain is a world consisting of a number of unexplored continents and great stretches of unknown territory.” — Santiago Ramón y Cajal
“大脑是一个由许多未经探索的大陆和大片未知领土组成的世界。”——圣地亚哥·拉蒙-卡哈尔（Santiago Ramón y Cajal）
To most, ChatGPT was a surprise. Three years before it landed, AGI1 was a taboo topic, something people who read too much science fiction would bring up. Outside of a very niche subcommunity in SF and London, it was deemed absurd to think that within a few years a machine would surpass us at most tasks. The world expected decades if not centuries before it would be able to talk to an intelligent machine.
对大多数人来说，ChatGPT 是一个意外。在它问世的三年前，AGI⟦1⟧ 还是一个禁忌话题，是那些科幻小说读得太多的人才会提起的东西。除了旧金山（SF）和伦敦一个非常小众的圈子之外，认为机器会在几年内在大多数任务上超越我们，被视为荒谬之谈。世界预期，要过几十年甚至几个世纪，人类才能与一台智能机器对话。
The world was wrong.
世界错了。
After a collective miss of that magnitude, intellectual humility is the least we can do. However, the right response is not just to ask why almost everyone failed to see LLMs coming, it is to ask ourselves: what are we getting just as wrong today? What transformational technology is on a clear exponential trajectory while being dismissed as science fiction by most?
在如此规模的集体误判之后，保持智识上的谦逊是我们最起码能做的。然而，正确的反应不仅仅是追问为什么几乎所有人都没能预见大语言模型（LLM）的到来，而是要问自己：我们今天在哪些事情上同样大错特错？哪一项变革性技术正处于清晰的指数轨道上，却被大多数人当作科幻小说而不屑一顾？
Brain-computer interface is certainly a prime candidate. Too cryptic for the masses, seemingly impossible to build, and with an expected timeline of decades before anything meaningful comes out of it. All while the data is telling us a very different story.
脑机接口无疑是首要候选。对大众而言它过于晦涩，看似不可能造出来，而且人们预期要等几十年才会有任何有意义的成果。而与此同时，数据所讲述的却是一个截然不同的故事。
Indeed, BCIs are about to surprise the world, and the ramifications of the technology will extend far beyond neuroscience, reaching all the way to frontier AI. An impact which will be vastly magnified by the collective ignorance of the impending arrival of the technology. Over the next five years, the field will move from an interesting research project to the center of the technological frontier.
事实上，BCI 即将让世界大吃一惊，这项技术的影响将远远超出神经科学，一直延伸到前沿 AI。而公众对这项技术即将到来的集体无知，将极大地放大这种影响。在未来五年里，该领域将从一个有趣的研究项目走到技术前沿的中心。
BCI is now only a few years away from its ChatGPT moment.
BCI 距离它的“ChatGPT 时刻”只剩几年了。
So what exactly is BCI? A brain-computer interface (or BCI) is exactly what the name suggests: a way for the mind to communicate directly with a computer. Instead of relying on hands to type or a voice to speak, BCI systems read the natural signals produced by the brain and translate them directly into digital commands. They remove the physical barriers between our thoughts and machines.
那么，BCI 究竟是什么？脑机接口（简称 BCI）正如其名：一种让心智直接与计算机通信的方式。BCI 系统不依赖双手打字或嗓音说话，而是读取大脑产生的自然信号，并将其直接转换为数字指令。它们消除了我们的思想与机器之间的物理屏障。
1. An Artificial General Intelligence defines an AI that is better than human across a broad range of cognitive tasks.
1. 通用人工智能（Artificial General Intelligence, AGI）指在广泛的认知任务上都优于人类的 AI。
It may sound a lot like magic or telepathy (and in many regards it is), but remarkably, it is possible. We are already able to send words, images and emotions to machines via the power of thoughts only.
这听起来很像魔法或心灵感应（在很多方面它确实如此），但值得注意的是，它是可能的。我们已经能够仅凭思想的力量，把文字、图像和情绪发送给机器。
The deeper promise of this technology is a faster control of robots and digital systems and the removal of layers of compression between thought and expression; unlocking richer, higher-fidelity communication between minds, biological or artificial.
这项技术更深层的前景在于：更快速地控制机器人和数字系统，并消除思想与表达之间的层层压缩；从而在心智之间——无论是生物的还是人工的——实现更丰富、更高保真的交流。
As simple as it is to describe, building BCI is in fact a herculean task, under this simple premise lives a wealth of complexity. Humanity has been trying to crack this problem for more than 50 years and just like AI, it has been making slow, but compounding progress over the decades.
尽管描述起来如此简单，构建 BCI 实际上却是一项艰巨的任务，这个简单前提之下蕴藏着大量的复杂性。人类尝试攻克这个问题已有五十多年，而且和 AI 一样，几十年来它一直在取得缓慢但不断累积的进展。
Memory cells in a mouse brain. Somewhere in your head, a clump of cells like this is storing this exact sentence; BCI is the attempt to decode it. Source: NIH.
小鼠大脑中的记忆细胞。在你脑中的某处，一簇类似这样的细胞正储存着这句话本身；BCI 就是解码它的尝试。来源：美国国立卫生研究院（NIH）。
Three Paths
三条路径
When people say “BCI” they tend to mean three quite different, yet adjacent, technologies. The simplest way I have found to explain the difference is the stadium analogy.
当人们说“BCI”时，他们往往指的是三种截然不同却又相互毗邻的技术。我发现解释其差异最简单的方式是“体育场类比”。
Imagine you are standing outside a packed stadium during a football match. The crowd is screaming. You want to know what they are saying.
想象你站在一座座无虚席的体育场外，里面正在进行一场足球比赛。人群在呐喊。你想知道他们在喊什么。
You can stand outside the stadium and hold a microphone up to the wall. You will hear something; it will be muffled and washed out and dominated by the loudest, simplest patterns. That is non-invasive BCI. The brain equivalent is sensors sitting on the scalp and picking up electrical, magnetic, or hemodynamic activity through the skull. No surgery, and no FDA approval is required for consumer applications, it has the broadest theoretical user base, but also the noisiest data.
你可以站在体育场外，把麦克风贴在墙上。你会听到一些声音；它会是闷闷的、模糊的，并被最响亮、最简单的模式所主导。这就是非侵入式 BCI。在大脑中对应的是放置在头皮上的传感器，透过颅骨捕捉电、磁或血流动力学活动。无需手术，消费级应用也不需要美国食品药品监督管理局（FDA）批准；它在理论上拥有最广泛的用户群，但数据也最嘈杂。
Alternatively, you can drill a hole in the wall and put a microphone right against the inside surface. The signal gets dramatically cleaner, although you still cannot quite pick out individual voices. That is semi-invasive; devices placed under the skull but not through the dura mater. Comparable to outpatient procedures we already do at scale (LASIK, cochlear implants, pacemakers). This path takes regulatory work, and recovery rooms.
或者，你可以在墙上钻一个洞，把麦克风紧贴在内壁上。信号会干净得多，尽管你仍然无法完全分辨出单个人的声音。这就是半侵入式：设备置于颅骨之下，但不穿透硬脑膜。它可与我们已经大规模开展的门诊手术相提并论（如 LASIK 激光近视手术、人工耳蜗、心脏起搏器）。这条路径需要监管审批工作和术后恢复室。
Or you can walk into the stadium and put a microphone next to a specific person’s mouth. That is fully invasive, electrodes directly in brain tissue (what Neuralink is doing). The cleanest signals possible but also the longest road; involving surgery, regulations, and generational timescales for adoption.
又或者，你可以走进体育场，把麦克风放在某个特定观众的嘴边。这就是全侵入式：电极直接置入脑组织（Neuralink 正在做的事）。它能获得最干净的信号，但也是最漫长的道路；涉及手术、监管，以及以世代为尺度的普及时间。
If you track how these three paths improve over time and effort, they each form an S-curve. Progress starts slow, accelerates rapidly as the technology matures, and eventually flattens out when it hits the physical ceiling. The metric that matters most is practical bandwidth: how much useful information we can actually pull out of the head and put to use at scale.
如果追踪这三条路径随时间和投入的进步情况，它们各自都会形成一条 S 曲线。进展起初缓慢，随着技术成熟而迅速加速，最终在触及物理上限时趋于平缓。最重要的指标是实际带宽：我们究竟能从头脑中提取多少有用信息，并将其规模化地加以利用。
Non-invasive has the capacity to scale faster over the next few years and reach a viable threshold of market value because it does not require a surgeon, but its capability ceiling might arrive early because the skull blocks signals. Fully invasive has a theoretically much higher ceiling, but it must first crawl under the heavy floor of clinical regulation and surgical throughput before it can even start to scale.
非侵入式有能力在未来几年更快地规模化，并达到具备市场价值的可行门槛，因为它不需要外科医生；但由于颅骨会阻挡信号，它的能力上限可能来得较早。全侵入式在理论上有高得多的上限，但在开始规模化之前，它必须先在临床监管和手术通量这块沉重的“地板”下艰难爬行。
The important point is that nobody actually knows where the ceiling for any of these curves sits today. We have not climbed far enough to hit the head-room of the possible, until we do, the different modalities are just different ways of asking the same question: What is the fastest way to decode the mind at scale?
关键在于，今天没有人真正知道这些曲线的上限在哪里。我们还没有攀爬到足以触及可能性顶部空间的高度；在那之前，不同的模态只是在用不同方式问同一个问题：大规模解码心智的最快方式是什么？
Sidenote · 旁注
There is one more distinction worth drawing. Reading vs. writing. Reading the brain (pulling structured information out) is a fundamentally easier problem than writing to the brain (putting an intention or experience in). Sensing the magnetic, electrical, or hemodynamic shadow of activity through the skull is hard but tractable. Stimulating a specific neuron or population from outside the head, with the precision to cause a thought rather than disrupt one, is a much harder problem. It may simply not be possible non-invasively at high fidelity.
还有一个值得区分的概念：读取与写入。读取大脑（把结构化信息提取出来）从根本上说是比写入大脑（把一个意图或体验放进去）更容易的问题。透过颅骨感知神经活动的磁、电或血流动力学“影子”虽然困难，但可以做到。而要从头部外部刺激某个特定神经元或神经元群，并且精确到能够引发一个想法而非扰乱它，则是一个难得多的问题。以非侵入方式实现高保真写入，或许根本就不可能。
Wilder Penfield, 1934. One of the fathers of modern neuroscience.
怀尔德·彭菲尔德（Wilder Penfield），1934 年。现代神经科学的奠基人之一。
But philosophy does not build hardware, what matters is the data. So let’s look at what non-invasive BCI can actually do today, and establish the physical baseline of what is possible.
但哲学造不出硬件，重要的是数据。那么，让我们来看看非侵入式 BCI 今天实际能做到什么，并确立“可能性”的物理基线。
02 The Art of the Possible
02 可能性的艺术
“When a distinguished but elderly scientist states that something is possible, he is almost certainly right. When he states that something is impossible, he is very probably wrong.”
“当一位杰出但年迈的科学家说某件事是可能的，他几乎肯定是对的。当他说某件事是不可能的，他很可能是错的。”
— Arthur C. Clarke, First Law, 1962
——亚瑟·C·克拉克（Arthur C. Clarke），克拉克第一定律，1962 年
BCI capabilities are already beyond what most people expect. Here are four things that happened in recent years, which you probably did not hear about.
BCI 的能力已经超出了大多数人的预期。以下是近年来发生的四件事，你可能都没有听说过。
A team at UT Austin placed a person in an fMRI scanner, played them a podcast, and reconstructed the sentences out of brain activity, sentence by sentence; not the audio but the meaning itself. They can do this for silent inner narration too: you think the sentence, and the model writes a paraphrase of it.2
德克萨斯大学奥斯汀分校（UT Austin）的一个团队让一名受试者躺进功能性磁共振成像（fMRI）扫描仪，给他播放一段播客，然后从大脑活动中逐句重建出句子；重建的不是音频，而是意义本身。他们对无声的内心独白也能做到这一点：你在心里想一句话，模型就写出它的转述。⟦2⟧
A team at Stanford implanted microelectrode arrays in a paralyzed person’s motor cortex, asked them to imagine handwriting letters with their hand, and decoded typed text at 90 cpm. That is faster than most of us type on a phone. (A team at UCSF reached comparable rates a couple of years later, decoding intended speech directly from the cortex.)
斯坦福大学的一个团队在一名瘫痪患者的运动皮层中植入了微电极阵列，让他想象用手书写字母，并以每分钟 90 个字符（cpm）的速度解码出打字文本。这比我们大多数人在手机上打字还要快。（几年后，加州大学旧金山分校（UCSF）的一个团队直接从皮层解码意图言语，达到了相当的速率。）
A team at Princeton captured fMRI activity from someone looking at a photo and reconstructed that photo from the brain signal alone, using diffusion models trained on neural-image pairs.
普林斯顿大学的一个团队采集了一个人观看照片时的 fMRI 活动，并利用在“神经信号—图像”配对数据上训练的扩散模型，仅凭脑信号就重建出了那张照片。
2. Tang’s work stands on the shoulders of the Gallant Lab at Berkeley, who built the first cortical semantic maps a decade earlier; Huth et al. 2016, Nishimoto et al. 2011, and others laid the foundations that made it possible.
2. Tang 的工作建立在伯克利 Gallant 实验室的基础之上，该实验室在十年前构建了首批皮层语义图谱；Huth 等人 2016 年、Nishimoto 等人 2011 年以及其他研究奠定了使之成为可能的基础。
A team at Tongji extended the same idea from stills to video. They played people short clips, and then decoded the videos from their minds. Pull that thread forward a decade, and you can imagine your phone displaying live visuals of what you just imagined.
同济大学的一个团队把同样的思路从静态图像扩展到了视频。他们给受试者播放短片，然后从其大脑中解码出这些视频。把这条线索向前延伸十年，你就可以想象手机实时显示出你刚刚想象的画面。
We extracted a video out of someone’s head from only a few tens of hours of scan data, how is that even possible? As shocking as it may sound, this is the current state of the field.
我们仅凭几十个小时的扫描数据，就从一个人的脑中提取出了一段视频，这怎么可能？尽管听起来令人震惊，但这就是该领域的现状。
And while none of these demonstrations, by itself, proves that scaling the technology will be easy (fMRI does not scale to the living room and invasive microelectrode arrays do not scale to healthy consumers), they show something much more fundamental: that the signal is there, and that modern models can read it when the data is clean enough. And this is not just wishful thinking anymore, those are published, peer-reviewed demonstrations. Together, they all point to the same structural truth: if a thought leaves a trace in the signal, a large enough model can find it.
虽然这些演示本身都不能证明这项技术的规模化会很容易（fMRI 无法规模化地进入客厅，侵入式微电极阵列也无法规模化地应用于健康消费者），但它们揭示了更为根本的东西：信号就在那里，只要数据足够干净，现代模型就能读取它。这也不再只是一厢情愿，它们都是已发表、经同行评审的演示。它们共同指向同一个结构性事实：只要一个想法在信号中留下痕迹，一个足够大的模型就能找到它。
The remaining question is how much of that signal can be recovered non-invasively, at scale, in real life.
剩下的问题是：在真实生活中，能以非侵入方式、大规模地恢复多少这种信号。
As can be seen from those examples, the current frontier work in BCI is doing impressive things in narrow domains with tiny datasets. One of the core claims of this manifesto is that the BCI field is about to undergo the exact same phase transition AI went through in 2017: moving from clever human engineering to brute-force scaling. The same structural shift which produced LLMs.
从这些例子可以看出，当前 BCI 的前沿工作是在狭窄领域、用极小的数据集做出令人印象深刻的成果。本宣言的核心主张之一是：BCI 领域即将经历 AI 在 2017 年经历过的完全相同的相变——从巧妙的人工工程转向蛮力式规模化。正是这种结构性转变造就了大语言模型。
But before we look into the most promising way to scale BCI, there is one important piece of human physiology worth discussing in the context of human-machine communication. It sets both the floor a BCI must surpass to become useful and the natural ceiling on our ability to communicate with AI; I am talking about our peripheral nervous system.
不过，在探讨规模化 BCI 最有前景的方式之前，在人机通信的语境下，有一个重要的人体生理结构值得讨论。它既设定了 BCI 要变得有用所必须超越的下限，也设定了我们与 AI 通信能力的天然上限；我说的是我们的周围神经系统。
03 The Peripheral Nervous System Bar
03 周围神经系统门槛
“The hand is the cutting edge of the mind.” — Jacob Bronowski, The Ascent of Man, 1973
“手是心智的利刃。”——雅各布·布罗诺夫斯基（Jacob Bronowski），《人类的攀升》（The Ascent of Man），1973 年
The peripheral nervous system is key to understanding the role and limitations of BCI in interfacing with machines.
周围神经系统是理解 BCI 在与机器交互中的作用及其局限的关键。
The fancy wording “peripheral nervous system” simply refers to the biological hardware that connects your brain to the outside world, otherwise known as eyes, ears, hands, vocal cords etc. The PNS is the way your brain interacts with reality; any effect your mind has ever had on the world has first had to pass through it.
“周围神经系统”（peripheral nervous system, PNS）这个花哨的说法，指的不过是将大脑与外部世界连接起来的生物硬件，也就是眼睛、耳朵、双手、声带等等。PNS 是你的大脑与现实互动的方式；你的心智对世界产生过的任何影响，都必须先经过它。
It occupies a strange position between humans and machines. For AI, it is a painfully narrow bottleneck; while for BCI, it is an extraordinarily capable incumbent.
它在人与机器之间占据着一个奇特的位置。对 AI 而言，它是一个窄得令人痛苦的瓶颈；而对 BCI 而言，它是一个能力超群的“现任者”。
Too slow for AI
对 AI 而言太慢
From the perspective of AI, your peripheral nervous system is slow, too slow, and is the first half of the bandwidth problem3 (the other half being the speed of thinking itself).
从 AI 的角度看，你的周围神经系统很慢，太慢了，它构成了带宽问题⟦3⟧的前一半（另一半是思维本身的速度）。
Indeed, every computer interface humans have ever built sits between two clocks (a clock in information theory defines how quickly a system processes information): the biological one and the machine one; and humans now have a slower clock speed than AI. At this point, the human clock is inconveniently slow: speech runs at about 150 words per minute, typing at 50, reading at maybe 250, inner thought is harder to measure but probably runs at 1,000 to 4,000 words per minute equivalent, and a great deal more if you count the non-verbal parts of cognition. All while AI cognition is orders of magnitude faster.
事实上，人类构建过的每一种计算机界面都处于两个时钟之间（在信息论中，时钟定义了一个系统处理信息的速度）：生物时钟和机器时钟；而人类的时钟速度如今已慢于 AI。眼下，人类时钟慢得令人尴尬：说话约每分钟 150 词，打字约 50 词，阅读大约 250 词；内心思维较难测量，但大致相当于每分钟 1,000 到 4,000 词，如果把认知中的非语言部分算进去，还要多得多。而与此同时，AI 的认知速度要快上几个数量级。
3. The problem arising from the fact that AI can now take in and produce information thousands of times faster than a human can ingest and process it.
3. 指这样一个问题：AI 如今吸收和产出信息的速度，比人类摄取和处理信息的速度快数千倍。
For most of computing’s history, the gap between the two clocks did not matter much. Machines were slow enough that a keyboard could keep up, and humans were comfortably able to control the machines; but AI changed that. Machine capability has scaled by orders of magnitude in the last five years; yet the interface still routes through a keyboard. Models can now read a book in seconds and write one in minutes, and they are only getting faster. Increasingly, the slowest component in the loop has become the human. Both our cognition speed and our interface bandwidth are becoming the bottleneck.
在计算机历史的大部分时间里，两个时钟之间的差距并不怎么重要。机器足够慢，键盘跟得上，人类也能从容地控制机器；但 AI 改变了这一切。过去五年，机器能力提升了几个数量级；然而界面依然要经过键盘。模型如今几秒钟就能读完一本书，几分钟就能写出一本，而且只会越来越快。回路中最慢的环节越来越变成了人类。我们的认知速度和界面带宽都正在成为瓶颈。
To provide an analogy, imagine trying to direct a civilization that experiences ten years between each word you speak. A ten-word instruction would take it a century to receive. That is the emerging human-AI interface problem: the machine can race through vast amounts of reasoning while human reasoning is left in the dust and intent still trickles in through speech, typing, and clicks.
打个比方：想象你要指挥一个文明，而你每说一个词，它就要经历十年。一条十个词的指令，它要用一个世纪才能接收完毕。这就是正在浮现的人机界面问题：机器能飞速完成海量推理，而人类的推理被远远甩在后面，意图仍然只能通过说话、打字和点击一点一滴地输入。
That is where neurotechnology comes into play, it can help close that gap. As AI gets better at thinking and worse at waiting, the value of letting humans co-think with it grows fast; a direct neural channel removes the need to compress multi-dimensional thoughts and emotions into lossy, sequential streams of typed or spoken words. It lets you communicate your intent unfiltered to the AI, and, at the limit, it lets you upgrade your cognitive hardware to match the machines.
这正是神经技术发挥作用的地方，它可以帮助缩小这一差距。随着 AI 越来越擅长思考、越来越不擅长等待，让人类与其“共同思考”的价值迅速增长；一条直接的神经通道使我们无需再把多维的思想和情绪压缩成有损的、顺序化的打字或口语词流。它让你能够不经过滤地向 AI 传达意图，而在极限情况下，它让你能够升级自己的认知硬件，以匹配机器。
Sidenote · 旁注
Zooming out, this mismatch stems from the fact that two forms of intelligence are now co-evolving; machine intelligence, scaling at the rate of compute, and human intelligence, scaling at the rate of biology. Learning to co-exist with a new form of intelligence which surpasses us on so many levels is an unprecedented challenge. Each year AI beats most of the new benchmarks we create for it, it unlocks new skills, and it improves itself at an accelerating rate. Figuring out ways to successfully navigate this transition while keeping humans in the loop is a fundamentally unresolved problem, and a hard one too.
放眼全局，这种错配源于两种形式的智能正在共同演化：机器智能以算力的速度扩展，而人类智能以生物学的速度扩展。学会与一种在诸多层面都超越我们的新智能形式共存，是一项前所未有的挑战。每一年，AI 都会攻克我们为它设计的大多数新基准，解锁新技能，并以越来越快的速度自我改进。想办法成功度过这一转变、同时让人类留在回路之中，是一个根本上尚未解决的问题，而且是一个难题。
BCI is promising to be a critical tool to address this growing AI-human mismatch. It is arguably the only tool we have that focuses on raising human capability rather than containing AI capability. Currently, most of the levers the alignment4 community is reaching for (interpretability, evals, governance, scalable oversight) are defensive; they try to either slow AI capability, constrain it, control it, or interpret it after the fact5. And in the long run, only a lever that raises the human capability can keep the human side of the loop meaningful as the machine side continues to improve. That is what makes BCI, and neurotechnology more broadly, a fundamental pillar of any long-term arrangement in which humans stay in the loop.6
BCI 有望成为应对这种日益扩大的人机错配的关键工具。可以说，它是我们拥有的唯一一个着眼于提升人类能力、而非遏制 AI 能力的工具。目前，对齐⟦4⟧社区所诉诸的大多数杠杆（可解释性、评估、治理、可扩展监督）都是防御性的；它们试图减缓 AI 能力、约束它、控制它，或在事后解释它⟦5⟧。而从长远看，只有提升人类能力的杠杆，才能在机器一侧持续进步的同时，让回路中人类一侧依然有意义。正因如此，BCI 以及更广义的神经技术，才成为任何让人类留在回路中的长期安排的根本支柱。⟦6⟧
We will come back to the topic of AI and BCI convergence in the second half of this manifesto (Part III and Part IV). But before doing so, let us continue our exploration of BCI.
我们将在本宣言的后半部分（第三部分和第四部分）再回到 AI 与 BCI 汇合这一话题。但在此之前，让我们继续探索 BCI。
Too fast for BCI
对 BCI 而言太快
From the perspective of BCI, your fingers can hit a key in a few hundred milliseconds with high accuracy, and your tongue and jaw produce roughly 150 wpm of speech. The bar a BCI must clear to be useful to a person whose hands and mouth still work is therefore extreme. It is not enough for a BCI to read your intent, it has to read your intent with an inference latency low enough, and an accuracy high enough, to beat what your hands and mouth already do for free. If sub-vocal text takes 800 ms to decode at a rate of 10 wpm, the user will simply use their thumbs.
从 BCI 的角度看，你的手指能在几百毫秒内高精度地按下一个键，你的舌头和下颌能产出每分钟约 150 词的语音。因此，对于一个双手和嘴巴仍然正常的人来说，BCI 要变得有用所必须跨越的门槛是极高的。BCI 仅仅读懂你的意图是不够的，它必须以足够低的推理延迟和足够高的准确率读懂你的意图，从而胜过你的双手和嘴巴本来就免费提供的能力。如果默读文本需要 800 毫秒才能解码、速率只有每分钟 10 词，用户干脆就会用拇指打字。
4. Alignment, in AI safety, is the problem of ensuring an AI system’s behavior matches what humans actually want, rather than a misleading proxy of it. The harder version, often called scalable alignment, asks how we ensure this alignment continues as AI becomes smarter than us. This is an unsolved problem.
4. 在 AI 安全领域，对齐（alignment）是指确保 AI 系统的行为符合人类真正想要的，而非某种具有误导性的替代指标。其更难的版本通常被称为可扩展对齐（scalable alignment），探讨的是当 AI 变得比我们更聪明时，如何确保这种对齐得以持续。这是一个尚未解决的问题。
5. I do not discount the current work in interpretability, evaluations, governance, scalable oversight etc... Those efforts remain absolutely critical. The point I am making is rather that neurotechnology has an equally important role to play in alignment, complementary to these approaches.
5. 我并不否定当前在可解释性、评估、治理、可扩展监督等方面的工作……这些努力依然至关重要。我想说的是，神经技术在对齐问题上扮演着同等重要的角色，与这些方法相辅相成。
6. To be honest, if AI were not advancing so quickly, I would advocate slowing down the development of neurotechnology as this is a very risky technology. But with AGI now so close, it would be a mistake to do so.
6. 坦白说，如果 AI 的发展没有这么快，我会主张放缓神经技术的发展，因为这是一项风险很高的技术。但既然 AGI 已近在咫尺，这样做将是一个错误。
This is why every reasonable timeline for BCI splits into two milestones. There is “does decoding work at all”, which we are largely past for many tasks; and there is “does decoding work at a level a person without an existing peripheral-nervous-system disability would pay for”. Here, progress is non-linear; small fidelity gains near the threshold drive disproportionate usefulness. Voice recognition is the cleanest analogy, a 95% transcription system is unusable, whereas a 99.9% system can be used everywhere.
这就是为什么任何合理的 BCI 时间表都会分成两个里程碑。一个是“解码到底行不行”，在许多任务上我们基本已经越过了这一关；另一个是“解码能否达到一个周围神经系统没有残疾的人愿意付费的水平”。在这里，进步是非线性的；在门槛附近，保真度的微小提升会带来不成比例的实用性。语音识别是最清晰的类比：准确率 95% 的转写系统无法使用，而 99.9% 的系统可以用在任何地方。
This is also why medical applications have the lowest bar to clear, and are the first to fall. Patients with severe neuromuscular conditions (ALS, locked-in syndrome, paralysis) do not have an intact peripheral nervous system to compete against. The bar to do something useful for them sits well below the consumer bar. Tolerance for physical risk is higher, and the urgency is absolute. Almost every working BCI demonstration to date has been in this regime.
这也是为什么医疗应用要跨越的门槛最低，并且最先被攻克。患有严重神经肌肉疾病（如渐冻症 ALS、闭锁综合征、瘫痪）的患者，没有一个完好的周围神经系统可以与之竞争。为他们做些有用之事的门槛远低于消费级门槛。他们对身体风险的容忍度更高，需求也极为迫切。迄今为止，几乎所有成功的 BCI 演示都处于这一领域。
But medical only reaches a few thousand patients, the only way to reach hundreds of millions, at least in the short term, is through a consumer non-invasive product. Non-invasive is the fastest path to scale.
但医疗应用只能惠及几千名患者；至少在短期内，触达数亿人的唯一途径是消费级非侵入式产品。非侵入式是规模化的最快路径。
04 The Fastest Path to Scale
04 规模化的最快路径
So far, I have had a stronger focus on non-invasive BCI, as opposed to invasive or semi-invasive. There is a reason for that. Non-invasive BCI has a couple of advantages over alternatives which make it a much more suitable candidate for applying the AI scale paradigm. And while the cleanest signal we can get comes from inside the brain, the fastest path to scale BCI will be through non-invasive.
到目前为止，我更侧重于非侵入式 BCI，而非侵入式或半侵入式。这是有原因的。与其他方案相比，非侵入式 BCI 具备几项优势，使其成为应用 AI 规模化范式的更合适人选。尽管我们能获得的最干净信号来自大脑内部，但规模化 BCI 的最快路径将是非侵入式。
Twenty years ago, the consensus held that meaningful brain reading required going inside the skull. The signal-to-noise through bone seemed too poor for anything else. That belief is why the surviving old-guard BCI companies (Neuralink, Blackrock, Synchron, Paradromics, Precision Neuroscience) all chose surgery from the start; the bet was reasonable at the time. For writing back into the brain, it still is, putting precise patterns of activity into specific neural circuits needs resolution only invasive or semi-invasive can deliver, and that still holds true this decade. For reading, however, large language models have rewritten the rules of the game. Models trained on enough biological noise can pull structure the unaided eye could not see; the cost of extracting cognitive signal from messy non-invasive signal has been dropping by orders of magnitude. The skull still attenuates the signal, but large models just get better at reading through it over time.
二十年前，学界共识认为，有意义的脑读取必须进入颅骨内部。穿过骨骼后的信噪比似乎太差，别的办法都行不通。正是这一信念，使得幸存下来的老牌 BCI 公司（Neuralink、Blackrock、Synchron、Paradromics、Precision Neuroscience）从一开始就都选择了手术路线；这一押注在当时是合理的。就向大脑写回信息而言，它至今依然合理：要把精确的活动模式置入特定神经回路，所需的分辨率只有侵入式或半侵入式才能提供，这在本十年内依然成立。然而就读取而言，大语言模型已经改写了游戏规则。在足够多生物噪声上训练的模型，能够提取出肉眼看不到的结构；从杂乱的非侵入式信号中提取认知信号的成本，已经下降了几个数量级。颅骨仍会衰减信号，但大模型透过颅骨读取信号的能力会随时间不断提升。
Sonar technology uses the same principle as some BCI devices, sending a pulse and then capturing its interaction with the environment allowing it to see otherwise invisible structures.
声呐技术采用与某些 BCI 设备相同的原理：发出一个脉冲，再捕捉它与环境的相互作用，从而“看见”原本不可见的结构。
But if invasive signals are obviously the cleanest, why bother with non-invasive ones at all? Why not just, as a field, commit to the invasive path?
但既然侵入式信号显然最干净，为何还要费力研究非侵入式？为什么整个领域不干脆全力押注侵入式路线？
There are a few reasons.
原因有几个。
First, surgery is the hardest constraint to overcome. On top of the fact that most healthy people are not exactly lining up for elective brain surgery. Brain surgery means FDA pre-market approval (a regulatory pathway that takes 7 to 10 years and tens of millions per device). It means hospital procurement cycles (18 to 36 months from outreach to first scheduled patient). It means risk-averse insurance underwriting. It means an operating-room throughput limit. Even at maximum velocity, a single neurosurgical center can only do dozens to low hundreds of implant procedures per year7. And even in a best-case scenario, a brilliant invasive technology working perfectly will only be able to reach a few thousand patients in the time a successful non-invasive product could reach tens of millions.
第一，手术是最难克服的约束。更何况，大多数健康人并不会排着队去做择期脑部手术。脑部手术意味着需要 FDA 上市前批准（这一监管路径需要 7 到 10 年，每款设备耗资数千万美元）；意味着医院采购周期（从接洽到首位患者排期需 18 到 36 个月）；意味着风险厌恶的保险承保；意味着手术室的通量上限。即便全速推进，一家神经外科中心每年也只能完成几十到一两百例植入手术⟦7⟧。而且即使在最理想的情况下，一项完美运行的卓越侵入式技术，在一款成功的非侵入式产品能够触达数千万人的时间里，也只能惠及几千名患者。
As of May 2026, fewer than 100 people worldwide had received chronic invasive BCIs, mostly through Neuralink and Blackrock/BrainGate, with annual growth still measured in dozens. Non-invasive consumer BCIs, by contrast, already exceed one million users. Muse alone reports over 500,000, Emotiv over 70,000, and BrainCo tens of thousands, with Neurable, NextSense, and Meta expanding the category.
截至 2026 年 5 月，全球接受长期植入式侵入 BCI 的人不足 100 人，主要来自 Neuralink 和 Blackrock/BrainGate，年增长量仍以几十人计。相比之下，非侵入式消费级 BCI 的用户已超过一百万。仅 Muse 一家就报告了超过 50 万用户，Emotiv 超过 7 万，BrainCo（强脑科技）有数万用户，而 Neurable、NextSense 和 Meta 也在拓展这一品类。
7. The entire US neurosurgeon capacity (roughly 4,000–5,000 practicing surgeons) caps invasive throughput at roughly 25,000 to 100,000 implants per year, and that is even if we assume partial robotic-surgery autonomy. Reaching one million healthy adults requires a fundamentally different surgical pipeline that simply will not happen this decade. The earliest plausible mass-scale invasive timeline lands in the late 2030s at best, with the broader build-out running deep into the 2040s.
7. 美国全部神经外科医生的产能（约 4,000–5,000 名执业医生）将侵入式植入的通量限制在每年约 25,000 到 100,000 例，而这还是在假设机器人手术部分自主化的前提下。要覆盖一百万健康成年人，需要一条根本不同的手术流水线，而这在本十年内根本不会出现。侵入式技术大规模普及的最早可信时间，充其量也要到 2030 年代后期，更广泛的铺开则会一直延续到 2040 年代。
Fully invasive systems will keep advancing, and they will eventually produce the highest-bandwidth channel into the brain. There is no doubt that they are the final destination of the BCI field, but physics, biology, and the medical establishment dictate their timeline.
全侵入式系统将持续进步，并最终打造出通向大脑的最高带宽通道。毫无疑问，它们是 BCI 领域的最终归宿，但它们的时间表由物理学、生物学和医疗体制所决定。
Second, we do not know the limit of non-invasive. While we know that invasive BCI is ultimately better in the long run, we have no idea what the performance ceiling is for non-invasive. The honest scientific position on non-invasive BCI is that we do not know how good it can get, because nobody has stress-tested it at scale. Every reasonable counterargument to non-invasive points to signal quality through the skull, but every reasonable counterargument has the same hole: it argues from a theoretical physics standpoint about a problem that is inherently empirical. No data-backed argument has shown that building a consumer device with non-invasive BCI is not achievable. The few experiments that have looked harder show that scaling laws are still holding.
第二，我们不知道非侵入式的极限在哪里。虽然我们知道从长远看侵入式 BCI 终究更好，但我们完全不知道非侵入式的性能上限是多少。关于非侵入式 BCI，诚实的科学立场是：我们不知道它能做到多好，因为没有人在大规模下对它进行过压力测试。所有反对非侵入式的合理论点都指向穿过颅骨后的信号质量，但所有这些论点都有同一个漏洞：它们从理论物理的角度去论证一个本质上是经验性的问题。没有任何基于数据的论证表明，用非侵入式 BCI 打造一款消费级设备是不可实现的。少数更深入考察过的实验显示，规模定律依然成立。
Third, a working non-invasive BCI would cause a flywheel effect. The fastest way to accelerate any frontier technology is to ship a consumer product at scale. In the AI world, that was ChatGPT. A first working product of a groundbreaking technology ignites a flywheel, it generates attention, attention pulls capital, capital pulls talent, and talent builds the next iteration. But flywheels need a first push to start turning, right now, the BCI flywheel is almost static. There are some medical demonstrations and some lab papers, but no real consumer device. And the only chance to get there that we have within the next five years is through non-invasive.
第三，一款可用的非侵入式 BCI 将引发飞轮效应。加速任何前沿技术的最快方式，就是大规模推出一款消费级产品。在 AI 领域，这款产品就是 ChatGPT。一项突破性技术的首款可用产品会点燃飞轮：它引来关注，关注吸引资本，资本吸引人才，人才打造下一次迭代。但飞轮需要第一推动力才能转起来；眼下，BCI 的飞轮几乎是静止的。有一些医疗演示和一些实验室论文，但还没有真正的消费级设备。而在未来五年内，我们实现这一目标的唯一机会就是非侵入式。
Take Tesla. Before the Roadster8, electric cars were a fringe idea; a sliver of attention with almost no capital. Then Tesla shipped a flawed but working sports car, and the world saw, for the first time, that an electric car was a thing you could actually own and drive. Public attention shifted and capital followed. The trillion-dollar pipeline that built the Model S and the decade of EV companies behind it was only possible once that first basic car proved the category existed. Non-invasive BCI is where Tesla’s first car for BCI is made. What will fund the rest of the field is then the public-belief shift that arrives when a working consumer device exists.
以特斯拉（Tesla）为例。在 Roadster⟦8⟧ 之前，电动汽车是一个边缘想法；只吸引了些许关注，几乎没有资本。后来特斯拉推出了一款有缺陷但能用的跑车，世界第一次看到，电动汽车是一种你真的可以拥有和驾驶的东西。公众的注意力随之转移，资本也紧随其后。造就 Model S 的万亿美元级资金管道，以及其后十年涌现的电动车公司，都只有在那第一款简陋的车证明了这一品类的存在之后才成为可能。非侵入式 BCI，就是 BCI 领域打造“特斯拉第一款车”的地方。届时，为整个领域其余部分提供资金的，将是一款可用消费级设备问世时所带来的公众信念转变。
8. Tesla’s first car.
8. 特斯拉的第一款车。
Sidenote · 旁注
But what if the skull is simply too thick? If you remain skeptical that non-invasive hardware can clear the consumer bar, and think it is a waste of effort, you can evaluate the worst-case scenario, the strategy is hedged.
但如果颅骨就是太厚了呢？如果你仍然怀疑非侵入式硬件能否跨过消费级门槛，并认为这是白费力气，那么你可以评估一下最坏的情况：这一策略是有对冲的。
First, the engineering transfers. The decoder architectures, data conventions, and multi-modal sensor stacks being built right now are largely hardware-agnostic; they port cleanly to semi-invasive and invasive systems. The talent and capital that enter the field stay in the field. Second, the data is an asset. Even a stalled consumer push can generate a large data corpus of multi-modal neural data; this would still be an extraordinarily valuable resource for the rest of the field. Even in the worst-case scenario, pushing the non-invasive limit would still accelerate the rest of the field.
首先，工程成果可以迁移。当前正在构建的解码器架构、数据规范和多模态传感器栈在很大程度上与硬件无关；它们可以顺畅地移植到半侵入式和侵入式系统上。进入这一领域的人才和资本会留在这个领域。其次，数据本身就是资产。即便消费级推进陷入停滞，也能产生一个大规模的多模态神经数据语料库；这对该领域其余部分而言仍将是极其宝贵的资源。即便在最坏的情况下，推动非侵入式的极限仍会加速整个领域的发展。
The BCI flywheel assumes it is possible in the first place to build a working product from non-invasive technology in the short term. The bet is that the brain emits enough signal through the skull to decode useful information non-invasively. The question is whether the signal that survives (through the skull, through movement, through a Monday afternoon, through a different user) carries enough information for better sensors and larger models to make sense of it at scale.
BCI 飞轮的前提是：首先，要能在短期内用非侵入式技术打造出可用的产品。这一押注认为，大脑透过颅骨发出的信号足以让我们以非侵入方式解码出有用信息。问题在于，那些幸存下来的信号（穿过颅骨、经历身体移动、熬过一个周一下午、换到另一位用户身上）是否携带足够的信息，使更好的传感器和更大的模型能够在大规模下理解它。
The fundamental thesis is not just that signal exists, it is that enough of that signal is stable across people, sessions, and contexts that pretraining can amortize the cost of learning one new brain. That is more of an empirical question than a theoretical one. We do not know the limit of non-invasive brain decoding yet because nobody has stress-tested it at scale. We will only know what the physics actually allows once we build the infrastructure to test it. As of 2026, the true ceiling of the non-invasive hardware remains completely uncharted.
根本论点不仅仅是信号存在，而是这些信号中有足够多的部分在不同的人、不同的记录时段和不同的情境之间保持稳定，从而使预训练能够摊薄学习一个新大脑的成本。这与其说是一个理论问题，不如说是一个经验问题。我们还不知道非侵入式脑解码的极限，因为没有人在大规模下对它进行过压力测试。只有当我们建起检验它的基础设施时，才会知道物理学究竟允许什么。截至 2026 年，非侵入式硬件的真正上限仍完全是未知领域。
86 billion biological wires, the tangled mess we are trying to decode.
860 亿根生物“导线”，这就是我们试图解码的那团乱麻。
This sets the threshold of capabilities that a consumer BCI must meet to be useful and scale. For the first time, the conditions to clear this threshold are coming together; and they draw a pattern that I have seen before.
这设定了消费级 BCI 要变得有用并实现规模化所必须达到的能力门槛。跨越这一门槛的条件第一次正在汇聚；而它们勾勒出的模式，我以前见过。
05 Why Now?
05 为什么是现在？
Back in the rainy London winter of 2018, I read a book which changed my life: Life 3.09. I picked it up out of curiosity, but by the time I turned the final page, I was convinced that the transition towards AGI was already underway. From there onwards, I decided to pivot my life around that conviction.
2018 年那个多雨的伦敦冬天，我读了一本改变我人生的书：《生命 3.0》（Life 3.0）⟦9⟧。我出于好奇拿起它，但当我翻到最后一页时，我已确信通往 AGI 的转变早已在进行中。从那以后，我决定围绕这一信念调整我的人生方向。
Digging into BCI over the past decade has brought back the same sense of recognition. The evidence remains scattered across laboratories and modalities, and the technology still looks crude enough to dismiss. Yet I have seen what happens when capability compounds faster than consensus, and BCI now looks to me the same way AI did in 2018: underestimated and closer than it appears.
过去十年深入研究 BCI，让我再次产生了同样的似曾相识之感。证据依然分散在各个实验室和不同模态之中，这项技术看上去仍粗糙得足以让人不屑一顾。然而，我见过当能力的复利增长快于共识形成时会发生什么；在我看来，今天的 BCI 就像 2018 年的 AI：被低估，且比看上去更近。
9. Still to this day the best book on the topic of AI, I highly recommend reading it.
9. 时至今日，它仍是关于 AI 主题的最佳著作，强烈推荐阅读。
Eight-year Offset
八年时差
To borrow the AI timeline for a second: BCI has roughly an 8-year delay relative to AI; it is at the same maturity level as AI was in 2017. The year the transformer architecture10 emerged, two years before GPT-2 made scaling laws visible and five years before ChatGPT made it obvious to everyone else.
借用一下 AI 的时间线：BCI 相对于 AI 大约滞后 8 年；它目前的成熟度相当于 2017 年的 AI。那一年，Transformer 架构⟦10⟧问世；两年后 GPT-2 让规模定律显现出来，五年后 ChatGPT 让所有人都看清了这一点。
The parallel runs deeper than timing, the brain and AI have had a deeply intertwined history since their very inception. AI was initially modelled on ourselves, or on our brain to be more accurate; neurons, neural networks, synaptic weights, and training are all concepts borrowed from biology. AI took inspiration from that architecture to build its foundations. However, this flow is about to reverse, it is now time for BCI to learn from AI to make the next leap forward. Specifically, to use the AI scaling paradigm for the BCI field. Most people, including a meaningful chunk of the neurotechnology field itself, still do not recognize how impactful it would be to apply scaling to BCI. But scaling is about to become the dominant narrative in BCI as well.
这种平行关系不仅仅体现在时间上，大脑与 AI 自诞生之初就有着深刻交织的历史。AI 最初是以我们自身为模型的，更准确地说，是以我们的大脑为模型；神经元、神经网络、突触权重和训练，都是从生物学借来的概念。AI 从这一架构中汲取灵感，奠定了自己的基础。然而，这股流向即将逆转：现在轮到 BCI 向 AI 学习，以实现下一次飞跃。具体来说，就是将 AI 的规模化范式用于 BCI 领域。大多数人，包括神经技术领域内相当一部分人，仍未认识到将规模化应用于 BCI 会产生多大的影响。但规模化也即将成为 BCI 领域的主导叙事。
10. The Transformer is the neural-network architecture introduced in 2017 by Google in the Attention Is All You Need paper. It replaced the recurrent and convolutional networks that came before with a simpler mechanism called attention, which is the precursor of every modern large model.
10. Transformer 是谷歌（Google）于 2017 年在论文《Attention Is All You Need》中提出的神经网络架构。它用一种更简单的、称为“注意力”（attention）的机制取代了此前的循环网络和卷积网络，是所有现代大模型的前身。
The Ignition Stack
点火组合
The running joke in the field is that “BCI has been five years away for 30 years”,11 but the joke may finally be running out of material. The conditions for scaling BCI have been converging in the past few years, specifically:
这个领域流传着一个老笑话：“BCI 三十年来一直都是‘还差五年’”，⟦11⟧但这个笑话或许终于要讲不下去了。过去几年，规模化 BCI 的条件一直在汇聚，具体而言：
11. Neuroscientists are not the only optimists in the room, the same affliction has plagued nuclear fusion, which has been “ten years away” since the 1950s.
11. 神经科学家并不是唯一的乐观派，同样的毛病也困扰着核聚变——自 1950 年代以来，它一直“还差十年”。
AI models got good enough. Brain decoders are only as strong as the foundation models behind them. Until roughly 2024 those models were too small or too narrow to pull structure from biological noise. Today’s frontier-class models are finally able to.
AI 模型变得足够好了。脑解码器的强弱取决于其背后的基础模型。直到 2024 年前后，这些模型要么太小、要么太窄，无法从生物噪声中提取结构。今天的前沿级模型终于做到了。
Sensors got cheap. EEG, fNIRS, sEMG, the first consumer-grade OPM and ultrasound front-ends have all dropped significantly, riding smartphone and wearables curves.
传感器变得便宜了。脑电图（EEG）、功能性近红外光谱（fNIRS）、表面肌电（sEMG），以及首批消费级光泵磁力计（OPM）和超声前端，价格都已大幅下降，搭上了智能手机和可穿戴设备的成本下降曲线。
Brain decoding graduated from toy technology to scalable technology. The previous generation of work topped out at simple motor commands: cursor left, cursor right, one character at a time. The new generation reads sentences, images, and speech directly from brain activity. Tang, Willett, Metzger, Scotti, and the ENIGMA family each demonstrate this from a different angle.
脑解码从玩具技术毕业，成为可规模化的技术。上一代工作的上限是简单的运动指令：光标左移、光标右移、一次一个字符。新一代则能直接从脑活动中读取句子、图像和语音。Tang、Willett、Metzger、Scotti 以及 ENIGMA 系列研究，各自从不同角度证明了这一点。
Capital is finally available. Neurotech venture funding hit $2.3 billion in 2024 and ~$4.8 billion in 2025. Cumulative private capital now exceeds cumulative US federal BCI-specific funding. VCs, frontier labs and governments have all been ramping up their investments in neurotechnology.
资本终于到位了。神经技术风险投资在 2024 年达到 23 亿美元，2025 年约为 48 亿美元。累计私人资本如今已超过美国联邦政府在 BCI 专项上的累计资助。风险投资机构、前沿实验室和各国政府都在加大对神经技术的投资。
Although BCI has existed at the margins of science for decades, it is only now approaching an inflection point. Thanks to advances in sensors, foundation models, and large-scale neural data, it is beginning to be possible to do what once only belonged to speculative fiction: human thought can become a native interface to machines, and eventually the extension of the biological brain through an external cognitive layer, or exocortex12.
尽管 BCI 几十年来一直处于科学的边缘，但直到现在它才逼近拐点。得益于传感器、基础模型和大规模神经数据的进步，过去只属于科幻小说的事情开始变得可能：人类思想可以成为机器的原生界面，并最终通过一个外部认知层——即“外皮层”（exocortex）⟦12⟧——实现生物大脑的延伸。
CHECKPOINT I · 检查点 I
Part I focused on presenting the structural realities of AI and BCI. Specifically, we
第一部分着重呈现了 AI 与 BCI 的结构性现实。具体而言，我们讨论了以下内容：
1. The Existence Proof. BCI is feasible. Modern computers can already decode raw meaning and visual images from the brain’s biological noise; the physics allows it.
1. 存在性证明。BCI 是可行的。现代计算机已经能够从大脑的生物噪声中解码出原始语义和视觉图像；物理学允许这样做。
2. The Bottleneck. Human biology is slowing down relative to AI. As machine capability scales vertically, expanding the human-machine bandwidth and upgrading the biological cognitive system is the only way to bridge the gap and remain relevant.
2. 瓶颈。相对于 AI，人类生物学正在变慢。随着机器能力纵向扩展，扩大人机带宽、升级生物认知系统，是弥合差距、保持自身价值的唯一途径。
12. An exocortex is an external digital layer of your mind that extends your memory, attention, and mental computation beyond your biological brain while functioning as part of it. Your phone has served as a crude, indirect version of this for over a decade; a BCI-linked AI could become a faster, more integrated version of it.
12. 外皮层是你心智的一个外部数字层，它把你的记忆、注意力和心智运算延伸到生物大脑之外，同时作为大脑的一部分运作。十多年来，你的手机一直充当着它的一个粗糙、间接的版本；而一个与 BCI 连接的 AI 可能会成为它更快、更一体化的版本。
3. The Biological Bar: To become a consumer reality, non-invasive BCI has to clear a major hurdle: it must first beat the speed and accuracy of the peripheral nervous system.
3. 生物学门槛：要成为消费级现实，非侵入式 BCI 必须跨越一个重大障碍：它首先必须在速度和准确性上胜过周围神经系统。
4. The Hardware. The brain can be read, but surgery does not scale. Non-invasive BCI hardware is the only viable short-term path to scale.
4. 硬件。大脑可以被读取，但手术无法规模化。非侵入式 BCI 硬件是短期内唯一可行的规模化路径。
5. The Eight-Year Offset. BCI is exactly where AI was in 2017. The foundational proofs of concept are starting to work in labs, but the world has not noticed yet.
5. 八年时差。BCI 正处在 AI 2017 年所处的位置。基础性的概念验证开始在实验室中奏效，但世界还没有注意到。
6. The Timing. The compute is available, the AI model exists, the sensors are cheap, frontier AI labs are hitting the text-data wall and regulations are favorable. The conditions for ignition are fully set.
6. 时机。算力已经具备，AI 模型已经存在，传感器价格低廉，前沿 AI 实验室正撞上文本数据之墙，监管环境也很有利。点火的条件已全部就绪。
At this point, the theoretical debates are mostly over; the physics allows non-invasive brain reading. What remains is an execution problem; to cross the
至此，理论之争已基本结束；物理学允许非侵入式脑读取。剩下的是执行问题：要在未来五年内跨越消费级实用门槛，BCI 领域必须从根本上改变自身的架构。它必须不再作为一门定制化的医学科学，而开始像一个规模化工程问题那样运作。
It is now time to unleash the AI scaling playbook on the human brain.
是时候把 AI 的规模化打法施展到人类大脑上了。
PART II.
第二部分
The Scaling
规模化（The Scaling）
Does the AI scaling playbook work on the brain?
AI 的规模化打法在大脑上行得通吗？
06 The Brain’s Bitter Lesson
06 大脑的“苦涩教训”
“The biggest lesson that can be read from 70 years of AI research is that general methods that leverage computation are ultimately the most effective, and by a large margin.”
“从 70 年的 AI 研究中可以读出的最大教训是：利用计算的通用方法最终是最有效的，而且优势巨大。”
— Rich Sutton, The Bitter Lesson, 2019
——里奇·萨顿（Rich Sutton），《苦涩的教训》（The Bitter Lesson），2019 年
Let’s strip away the biology for a second. If you look at the brain purely as a computer scientist, what is it?
让我们暂时抛开生物学。如果纯粹以计算机科学家的眼光看大脑，它是什么？
It is a massive graph of densely connected nodes (neurons) firing off noisy electrical signals across multiple time scales. It has extreme redundancy and almost no labels. To decode the brain, you have to extract meaning from a giant, chaotic graph of raw data.
它是一个由密集连接的节点（神经元）构成的巨大图，在多个时间尺度上发出嘈杂的电信号。它具有极高的冗余度，几乎没有任何标签。要解码大脑，你必须从一个巨大、混乱的原始数据图中提取意义。
One cubic millimeter of cortex with every neuron individually traced.
一立方毫米的皮层，其中每一个神经元都被单独追踪描绘。
But we have seen this exact architecture before; we even already solved it.
但我们以前见过完全相同的结构；我们甚至已经解决了它。
We just did not call it a brain. We called it the internet.
只不过我们没有称它为大脑。我们称它为互联网。
The internet is a vast ocean of unlabeled text, images, connections and noise, far too big for human engineers to manually sort. But we made it tractable, and true to Sutton’s rule, we didn’t do it through clever, hand-crafted tricks.
互联网是一片由无标注的文本、图像、链接和噪声组成的浩瀚海洋，大到人类工程师根本无法手动整理。但我们让它变得可处理了，而且正如萨顿法则所言，我们并不是靠巧妙的手工技巧做到的。
We did it by scaling an embarrassingly simple technique relentlessly: pointing very large models at very large amounts of data, and letting them figure it out.
我们做到这一点，靠的是毫不停歇地把一种简单得令人尴尬的技术规模化：让非常大的模型面对非常大量的数据，然后让它们自己摸索出规律。
The brain is the exact same shape of problem. It is a graph. Messier than the internet, denser than the internet, and profoundly harder to instrument. But the fundamental math is the same. Which means the playbook that conquered the silicon graph should work for the biological one.
大脑恰恰是同一类形态的问题。它是一张图。比互联网更杂乱，比互联网更稠密，测量起来也难得多。但根本的数学是一样的。这意味着，征服了硅基之图的那套打法，也应该适用于生物之图。
This is no longer just a compelling handwavy theory, it is a published, measurable reality.
这已不再只是一个引人入胜却含糊其辞的理论，而是已发表、可测量的现实。
In early 2025, researchers at Meta’s AI lab released a paper titled Scaling laws for decoding images from brain activity13. They fed an AI decoder nearly 500 hours of brain recordings, comprising 2.3 million trials across EEG, MEG, and fMRI scanners.
2025 年初，Meta AI 实验室的研究人员发表了一篇题为《从脑活动中解码图像的规模定律》（Scaling laws for decoding images from brain activity）⟦13⟧的论文。他们给一个 AI 解码器输入了近 500 小时的脑记录，涵盖 EEG、脑磁图（MEG）和 fMRI 扫描仪上的 230 万次试次。
What they found is AI’s Bitter Lesson14 playing out in biological real time:
他们的发现，是 AI 的“苦涩教训”⟦14⟧在生物学中的实时上演：
The Straight Line. Decoding accuracy scales log-linearly with data, with no plateau in sight. Just like a language model, if you feed the decoder more data, it gets predictably better at reading the mind.
一条直线。解码准确率随数据量呈对数线性增长，看不到平台期。就像语言模型一样，给解码器喂更多数据，它读取心智的能力就会可预测地提升。
13. Despite its recent missteps in the metaverse and AI, Meta has been impressively ahead of the curve in neurotechnology, as its latest research demonstrates.
13. 尽管 Meta 最近在元宇宙和 AI 上有所失误，但正如其最新研究所显示的，它在神经技术领域一直领先得令人印象深刻。
14. The Bitter Lesson, articulated by Rich Sutton in 2019, is that general methods that scale with data and computation eventually outperform handcrafted human knowledge. It is “bitter” because researchers repeatedly resist the discovery that their clever domain expertise matters less than brute-force scale.
14. “苦涩的教训”由里奇·萨顿于 2019 年提出，其要义是：随数据和算力扩展的通用方法，最终会胜过人类手工设计的知识。之所以“苦涩”，是因为研究者们一再抗拒这一发现：他们精巧的领域专长，远不如蛮力式的规模来得重要。
The Hardware Offset. Higher-fidelity sensors (like an fMRI) simply raise the line compared to lower-fidelity sensors (like an EEG). The scaling law holds regardless of the sensor.
硬件偏移。保真度更高的传感器（如 fMRI）只是比保真度较低的传感器（如 EEG）把这条直线整体抬高。无论使用哪种传感器，规模定律都成立。
The Data Shape. At fixed total hours, recording one subject for 100 hours drastically outperforms recording 100 subjects for one hour. This is expected: below a certain amount of data per brain, a model cannot yet generalize across people, much like at a small scale a language model would learn far more if you trained it on 100 books in one language rather than one book from 100 languages. With scale, this constraint will disappear.
数据形态。在总时长固定的情况下，对一名受试者记录 100 小时，效果远胜于对 100 名受试者各记录 1 小时。这在意料之中：当每个大脑的数据量低于一定水平时，模型尚无法在不同人之间泛化；这就好比在小规模下，用同一种语言的 100 本书训练语言模型，要比用 100 种语言各一本书训练学到的多得多。随着规模扩大，这一限制将会消失。
That is the structural reality that will shape the future of the entire field. The scaling laws do not care about scattered, one-off clinical trials; they demand deep, continuous data. What we currently need for decoding the human mind is not a multimillion-dollar hospital scanner collecting sparse but highly accurate data, what we need is a cheap wearable BCI that can collect data at scale.
这就是将塑造整个领域未来的结构性现实。规模定律不在乎零散的、一次性的临床试验；它们需要深度、连续的数据。我们目前解码人类心智所需要的，不是一台价值数百万美元、采集稀疏但高精度数据的医院扫描仪，而是一款能够大规模采集数据的廉价可穿戴 BCI。
We should not wait 70 years to learn the lesson that AI had to learn the hard way. We can skip the hand-crafted era of BCI entirely and jump straight to the scaling playbook.
我们不应该再花 70 年去学习 AI 用惨痛代价才学到的教训。我们可以完全跳过 BCI 的手工设计时代，直接采用规模化打法。
Part II of this essay will focus on exploring how the AI scaling strategy can be applied to non-invasive BCI in order to create a first generation of consumer products and set a powerful flywheel effect in motion. Over the next 5 to 10 years, this flywheel effect will in turn accelerate the momentum of the field significantly and help BCI close the gap with AI.
本文第二部分将着重探讨如何把 AI 的规模化策略应用于非侵入式 BCI，以打造第一代消费级产品，并启动强大的飞轮效应。在未来 5 到 10 年里，这一飞轮效应将反过来显著加速该领域的发展势头，帮助 BCI 缩小与 AI 的差距。
Whiteboard Skepticism
白板上的怀疑
Of course, this is not a guaranteed victory. Brain data is noisier and more variable across subjects than text. It is time-correlated across multiple scales, and the labels are much weaker than simply predicting the next token. Yes, when scaling laws have been tested at a small scale in BCI (through datasets like ZuCo, DEAP, and AllJoined), they appear to hold, but small-scale trends do not automatically extrapolate.
当然，这并不是稳操胜券。与文本相比，脑数据噪声更大，在不同受试者之间差异也更大。它在多个时间尺度上存在时间相关性，其标签也远比“预测下一个词元（token）”弱得多。诚然，当规模定律在 BCI 中以小规模接受检验时（通过 ZuCo、DEAP 和 AllJoined 等数据集），它们似乎是成立的，但小规模的趋势并不能自动外推。
On top of it, right now, there is no such thing as a decoder that works on a new brain out of the box. Every model built so far still requires a calibration phase specifically tuned to your brain before it actually works. The field has never had to merge tens of thousands of unique human brains into one universal training set without relying on per-subject calibration.
此外，目前还不存在一个对新大脑开箱即用的解码器。迄今构建的每一个模型，都仍需要一个专门针对你的大脑进行调校的校准阶段，才能真正发挥作用。这个领域从未需要在不依赖逐个受试者校准的前提下，把数万个独一无二的人类大脑合并成一个通用训练集。
Sidenote · 旁注
The most counterintuitive aspect of our current ability to decode the mind is that we don’t even fully know how the brain computes. We built the internet, so we understand how it works but we do not know for sure if cognition is reducible to simple neuron electrical impulses, or if sub- neuronal physics actually matters. Microtubules , glial signaling, and even quantum effects are all still live hypotheses as underlying mechanisms for thoughts. All we know is that if we put a sensor on a head, grab a bunch of messy data, and ask an AI to find the pattern, it actually starts reading minds.
我们当前解码心智的能力中，最反直觉的一点是：我们甚至并不完全知道大脑是如何计算的。互联网是我们建造的，所以我们了解它如何运作；但我们并不确定认知是否可以还原为简单的神经元电脉冲，还是说亚神经元层面的物理过程确实起作用。微管、胶质细胞信号传导，甚至量子效应，都仍是关于思维底层机制的活跃假说。我们所知道的只是：如果把传感器放在头上，采集一大堆杂乱的数据，再让 AI 去寻找模式，它确实开始读心了。
The scaling playbook is a high-stakes wager, it is a bet that brute-force compute can extract enough usable structure from the noisy macro-signals of the brain without needing a perfect map of human cognition first. A bet that we can decode the mind without fully understanding it.15 If this turns out to be right, the scaling lines will continue pointing upwards and new breakthroughs will be achieved in fast succession as data and compute scale.
规模化打法是一场高风险的赌注，它押注蛮力计算能够从大脑嘈杂的宏观信号中提取出足够多可用的结构，而无需先绘制出一幅完美的人类认知地图。押注我们可以在没有完全理解心智的情况下解码心智。⟦15⟧如果这被证明是对的，规模化的直线将继续向上延伸，随着数据和算力的扩展，新的突破将接踵而至。
If the last decade of AI taught us anything, it is that betting against compute makes you sound incredibly smart, right up until it does not.
如果说过去十年的 AI 教会了我们什么，那就是：押注算力会失败，会让你听起来无比聪明——直到它不再如此的那一刻。
Theoretical counter-arguments can only go so far here. All the caveats above simply tell us that the brain is not exactly the internet, which is already obvious from the premise. The thesis is that it is similar enough that the exact same laws of compute apply. But you fundamentally cannot disprove scaling laws with a whiteboard. It is inherently an empirical problem. The only way to prove that scaling fails is to actually run the experiment and hit a wall.
理论上的反驳在这里作用有限。上述所有限定条件只是告诉我们，大脑并不完全等同于互联网，而这从前提来看本就显而易见。本文的论点是，二者足够相似，以至于完全相同的计算定律都适用。但你根本无法用一块白板来证伪规模定律。它本质上是一个经验问题。证明规模化失败的唯一方式，就是真正去做实验并撞上一堵墙。
And so far, every attempt to scale has only reinforced the evidence.
而到目前为止，每一次规模化尝试都只是强化了证据。
The Bitter Lesson, as Sutton put it (or its older sibling, Peter Norvig’s The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Data, which made the exact same point in 2009), will come for every field eventually. It has not yet come for BCI; it is overdue.
正如萨顿所说的“苦涩的教训”（或者它更早的“兄长”——彼得·诺维格（Peter Norvig）的《数据的不合理有效性》（The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Data），早在 2009 年就提出了完全相同的观点），最终会降临到每一个领域。它还没有降临到 BCI，而且已经迟到了。
15. There is a deeper question lurking here: whether “understanding the brain” is even a well-defined milestone. I believe it is not: understanding is compression, and there is only so far a system as complex as the brain will compress. Tal Yarkoni makes the fuller case, and argues we might not even notice reaching it, in If we already understood the brain, would we even know it?
15. 这里潜藏着一个更深层的问题：“理解大脑”是否是一个定义明确的里程碑。我认为不是：理解即压缩，而像大脑这样复杂的系统，可压缩的程度终究有限。塔尔·亚尔科尼（Tal Yarkoni）在《如果我们已经理解了大脑，我们会知道吗？》（If we already understood the brain, would we even know it?）一文中对此作了更完整的论证，并认为即便我们达到了这一点，可能也不会察觉。
Purkinje cells (A) and granule cells (B) of the cerebellum, drawn by hand by Santiago Ramón y Cajal, 1899.
小脑的浦肯野细胞（A）和颗粒细胞（B），由圣地亚哥·拉蒙-卡哈尔手绘，1899 年。
Why no one has run the playbook yet
为什么还没有人执行这套打法
If the scaling bet is so obvious, a glaring question remains. BCI has had decades, why has nobody actually run the playbook?
如果规模化的押注如此显而易见，一个突出的问题依然存在：BCI 已经有了几十年时间，为什么没有人真正执行这套打法？
There are a couple of reasons.
原因有几个。
First and foremost, the specific AI architectures and self-supervised techniques required to actually execute this strategy were only proven in the last few years.
首要的是，真正执行这一策略所需的特定 AI 架构和自监督技术，直到最近几年才被证明可行。
But this is not the only reason, this stopped being a blocker a few years ago. What explains the delay in adopting this strategy is institutional inertia. Culture evolves at a glacial pace, or as Max Planck colorfully puts it: “Science advances one funeral at a time”.
但这不是唯一的原因，而且几年前它就已不再是障碍。真正解释这一策略迟迟未被采纳的，是体制惯性。文化的演变如冰川般缓慢，或者如马克斯·普朗克（Max Planck）生动地说的那样：“科学是一场葬礼接着一场葬礼地前进的。”
The entire BCI field was born in the world of invasive medical devices. Going from a culture shaped by FDA approvals, extreme patient safety and statistical rigor on small datasets to one of brute-force and scale is a brutal paradigm shift. Researchers who spent their careers learning to carefully hand- craft algorithms for specific patients do not naturally reach for the “throw 100K hours at a wall of GPUs” strategy. It certainly has a lot less scientific appeal. This heavy medical baggage was built for brain surgery, but it has completely bled over into the non-invasive space, and it is a real drag on the field.
整个 BCI 领域诞生于侵入式医疗器械的世界。从一种由 FDA 审批、极致的患者安全和小数据集上的统计严谨所塑造的文化，转向一种蛮力与规模的文化，是一次残酷的范式转换。那些毕生致力于为特定患者精心手工打造算法的研究者，不会自然而然地想到“把十万小时数据砸向一堵 GPU 墙”的策略。这种策略在科学上的吸引力当然也小得多。这套沉重的医学包袱是为脑部手术而建立的，却已完全渗透到非侵入式领域，成为这一领域的真正拖累。
Furthermore, a lot of the hardware for non-invasive BCI only recently became cheap and powerful enough for this purpose. Historically, the highest-performance, most “serious frontier” BCI results have been invasive or semi-invasive. Which explains why non-invasive hardware took a while to get the attention it deserved. There has also been a lab-fit hardware culture. Research-grade hardware optimizes for signal quality in a controlled setting; scaling-grade hardware optimizes for daily wear at the expense of signal quality.
此外，许多非侵入式 BCI 硬件直到最近才变得足够便宜、足够强大，能满足这一用途。从历史上看，性能最高、最“严肃前沿”的 BCI 成果一直来自侵入式或半侵入式。这解释了为什么非侵入式硬件花了一段时间才获得应有的关注。此外还存在一种适配实验室的硬件文化。研究级硬件针对受控环境下的信号质量进行优化；而规模级硬件则以牺牲信号质量为代价，针对日常佩戴进行优化。
Lastly, large coordinated data collection efforts only started to come to life recently. There is no Common Crawl16 or ImageNet17 for the human brain. We have several partial equivalents, but they are fragmented, small, clinical, lab-specific, and rarely track the same person over a long period of time. Data fragmentation is a major issue. Each lab collects its own dataset with its own preprocessing, electrode layouts, and approval pipelines. Some initiatives like BIDS and NWB are nascent but adoption is uneven.
最后，大规模协同数据采集工作直到最近才开始出现。人类大脑没有自己的 Common Crawl⟦16⟧ 或 ImageNet⟦17⟧。我们有几个部分对应物，但它们零散、规模小、以临床为主、局限于特定实验室，而且很少长期追踪同一个人。数据碎片化是一个重大问题。每个实验室都用自己的预处理方法、电极布局和审批流程采集自己的数据集。BIDS 和 NWB 等一些倡议尚处于起步阶段，采用程度参差不齐。
A lot of these are coordination problems. Coordination problems get fixed the moment one or two actors finally make the new optimal visible, forcing the rest of the field to follow, just like OpenAI and Google did for AI. The reason the scaling playbook has not run yet is not because it violates the laws of physics. It is because no coordinated push has been done in this direction, but that will change this decade.
其中许多都是协调问题。只要有一两个参与者最终让新的最优方案变得可见，迫使领域其余部分跟进，协调问题就会迎刃而解，正如 OpenAI 和谷歌在 AI 领域所做的那样。规模化打法至今未被执行，并不是因为它违背了物理定律，而是因为还没有人朝这个方向进行过协同推进；但这一点将在本十年内改变。
The best strategy for the BCI field is not to try to be clever about brain decoding, it is to be willing to scale.
BCI 领域的最佳策略，不是试图在脑解码上耍聪明，而是愿意去规模化。
16. Common Crawl is a non-profit that has been continuously crawling and publishing the web since 2008; the resulting petabyte-scale dataset is what most large language models trained on.
16. Common Crawl 是一家非营利组织，自 2008 年以来一直持续抓取并发布网络内容；由此产生的 PB 级数据集是大多数大语言模型的训练来源。
17. ImageNet is a labeled dataset of ~14 million images across 20K+ categories. The 2012 ImageNet competition is widely credited as the beginning of the deep-learning era in computer vision.
17. ImageNet 是一个带标注的数据集，包含约 1,400 万张图像，涵盖 2 万多个类别。2012 年的 ImageNet 竞赛被普遍认为是计算机视觉深度学习时代的开端。
The natural next question is: how do we actually apply scaling in practice? To map the execution out, we are going to use a simple mental model, the BCI pyramid.
接下来自然要问：我们在实践中究竟如何应用规模化？为了勾勒执行路径，我们将使用一个简单的思维模型：BCI 金字塔。
07 The Pyramid
07 金字塔
Before diving into the details of how to scale non-invasive BCI, we need to break it down to its core. Fundamentally, what is the field of non-invasive BCI made of?
在深入探讨如何规模化非侵入式 BCI 的细节之前，我们需要把它拆解到最核心的部分。从根本上说，非侵入式 BCI 领域由什么构成？
The simplest mental model is a pyramid. It has four layers, with every layer built directly upon the foundation of the one below it: Hardware, Data, Algorithms, and Product.
最简单的思维模型是一座金字塔。它有四层，每一层都直接建立在下一层的基础之上：硬件、数据、算法和产品。
Hardware is the base. Sensors, magnetometers, transducers, hardware sets the ceiling on signal quality. The rule is simple: anything you cannot record, you cannot decode. Right now, this layer is severely underserved. The equipment is often bespoke, expensive, and hacky. The foundation of the entire field is still very underdeveloped.
硬件是基座。传感器、磁力计、换能器——硬件决定了信号质量的上限。规则很简单：凡是你记录不到的，你就解码不出来。眼下，这一层严重缺乏投入。设备往往是定制的、昂贵的、东拼西凑的。整个领域的地基仍然非常不发达。
Data sits directly above that. The corpora of recordings, the protocols, the formatting conventions. Data sets the ceiling on how smart your model can get. You can only perform so much mathematical magic with 100 hours of recordings. Just like the hardware beneath it, this layer is critically immature and fragmented.
数据直接位于硬件之上。包括记录语料库、实验规程和格式规范。数据决定了你的模型能有多聪明的上限。只有 100 小时的记录，你能施展的数学魔法终究有限。和它下面的硬件一样，这一层极不成熟，且高度碎片化。
Algorithm sits above the data. These are the AI models that turn raw signal into actual meaning. Better algorithms can compensate for noisy data, but only up to a point. Today, a massive amount of the field’s time and talent is wasted here, trying to squeeze every last drop of signal out of tiny datasets.
算法位于数据之上。它们是把原始信号转化为实际意义的 AI 模型。更好的算法可以弥补数据的噪声，但只能弥补到一定程度。今天，该领域大量的时间和人才都浪费在这里，试图从极小的数据集中榨出最后一滴信号。
Product sits at the very top. It is entirely constrained by the three layers below it. Most of the products built in non-invasive BCI today have severely limited capabilities because the foundations they sit on are not robust. Building a consumer BCI right now is like trying to build ChatGPT when you only own a hundred GPUs and ten gigabytes of text. It is a structurally impossible task. The companies doing it are working miracles, but they are fighting a brutal systemic headwind.
产品位于最顶端。它完全受制于下面三层。今天在非侵入式 BCI 领域打造的大多数产品能力都极其有限，因为它们所依托的基础并不牢固。现在打造一款消费级 BCI，就像只有一百块 GPU 和十 GB 文本却想造出 ChatGPT 一样。这在结构上是不可能完成的任务。正在做这件事的公司都在创造奇迹，但它们正顶着残酷的系统性逆风前行。
The core dynamic here is simple, each layer is bottlenecked by the one below it. You cannot ship a product that requires data your hardware cannot collect. You cannot train a foundation-grade decoder on a couple hours of a graduate student staring at a screen in a basement.
这里的核心动态很简单：每一层都受制于其下一层。你无法推出一款需要硬件采集不到的数据的产品。你也无法用一个研究生在地下室盯着屏幕几个小时的数据，训练出一个基础模型级别的解码器。
The problem with the field today is that too much attention is at the top of the pyramid and not enough at the bottom. Everyone is obsessing over the penthouse while the foundation is sinking. The hardware layer is starved of well-funded and focused companies. The data layer needs aggressive scaling. And yet, every month brings dozens of beautiful papers proposing new decoders trained on tiny datasets. The algorithm is not the main problem. Data is the problem, and the hardware that collects the data is the problem before that.
当今该领域的问题在于，过多的注意力集中在金字塔顶端，而底层得到的关注远远不够。所有人都痴迷于顶层豪华套房，而地基却在下沉。硬件层缺乏资金充足、专注投入的公司。数据层需要激进的规模化。然而，每个月都有几十篇漂亮的论文提出在微小数据集上训练的新解码器。算法不是主要问题。数据才是问题，而在此之前，采集数据的硬件才是问题。
Take the ZuCo dataset, every time a research team works a mathematical miracle to squeeze a new paper out of the brain data of 12 people reading for six hours18, they are demonstrating the field’s problem more than they are solving it. Squeezing blood from a stone is impressive, but at a certain point, the miracle just becomes proof of the bottleneck. We need to stop praising the squeezing and start asking why we only have stones.
以 ZuCo 数据集为例，每当一个研究团队施展数学奇迹，从 12 个人阅读六小时的脑数据⟦18⟧中再榨出一篇新论文时，他们与其说是在解决这个领域的问题，不如说是在展示这个问题。从石头里榨出血来固然令人惊叹，但到了某个时候，奇迹本身就成了瓶颈的证明。我们需要停止赞美“榨取”，开始追问为什么我们只有石头。
Now that we have a clear mental model of the field, we are going to dissect exactly what is causing the bottleneck at the top. We will walk up the pyramid layer by layer. First the hardware, then the data, then the AI algorithms, and finally, the complex coordination required across the entire pyramid to actually pull this off.
既然我们对这个领域有了清晰的思维模型，接下来我们将剖析究竟是什么造成了顶层的瓶颈。我们将沿着金字塔逐层向上：先是硬件，然后是数据，再是 AI 算法，最后是为真正实现这一切而需要在整座金字塔上进行的复杂协调。
18. Yes, I promise you this is not a joke. One of the most commonly used datasets in the entire field literally consists of 12 people recorded for six hours each. That is 72 total hours of data. We are trying to build the ultimate foundation model for the human brain using the equivalent of one long weekend of recordings. It is like teaching an AI Japanese by showing it one sushi menu.
18. 是的，我向你保证这不是玩笑。整个领域最常用的数据集之一，字面意义上就是由 12 个人、每人记录六小时构成的。总共 72 小时的数据。我们正试图用相当于一个长周末的记录量，构建人类大脑的终极基础模型。这就像只给 AI 看一份寿司菜单来教它日语。
08 Level One: Hardware Is Hard
08 第一层：硬件之难
“People who are really serious about software should make their own hardware.” — Alan
“真正认真对待软件的人，应该自己制造硬件。”——艾伦·凯（Alan Kay），1982 年
Let us begin at the bottom of the pyramid: Hardware.
让我们从金字塔的底层开始：硬件。
AI hardware has been all over the news recently. We watch trillions of dollars flow into massive GPU data centers because everyone now understands that compute is the ultimate bottleneck for artificial intelligence. But hardware is not just the blocker for AI, it is also one of the key gating factors for brain-computer interfaces, and right now, the BCI field desperately needs its own hardware paradigm shift.
AI 硬件最近频频登上新闻。我们看着数万亿美元涌入庞大的 GPU 数据中心，因为如今所有人都明白，算力是人工智能的终极瓶颈。但硬件不仅仅是 AI 的阻碍，它也是脑机接口的关键制约因素之一；眼下，BCI 领域迫切需要属于自己的硬件范式转变。
A device engineered to gather massive datasets is a fundamentally different species of machine than a device engineered for a laboratory, and one of the most important differences is portability. If you are optimizing for the absolute cleanest signal possible, you demand precision at all costs. You accept the electromagnetically shielded room, the liquid helium cryogenics, and the completely immobile subject. However, if you are optimizing for the largest dataset possible, the math changes, you still care about signal quality, but the trade-off tilts heavily toward portability. Slightly noisier signal collected at a thousand times the volume beats a clean signal you cannot collect outside a shielded room. That is the trade-off the field has not yet fully internalized.
为采集海量数据集而设计的设备，与为实验室设计的设备，从根本上说是两种不同物种的机器，而其中最重要的差异之一就是便携性。如果你追求的是绝对最干净的信号，你就会不惜一切代价追求精度。你会接受电磁屏蔽室、液氦低温系统和完全静止不动的受试者。然而，如果你追求的是尽可能大的数据集，算法就变了：你仍然关心信号质量，但权衡会大幅向便携性倾斜。以千倍数量采集的稍嘈杂一些的信号，胜过一个你在屏蔽室之外无法采集的干净信号。这正是该领域尚未完全内化的权衡。
A slightly noisier signal collected at a thousand times the volume completely beats a pristine signal you cannot collect outside a shielded room
以千倍数量采集的稍嘈杂一些的信号，完全胜过一个你在屏蔽室之外无法采集的纯净信号。
Today, the current state of the art is heavy, expensive, and lives in a room with no windows, and not just metaphorically. That is fine for a paper, but it is incompatible with collecting tens of thousands of hours per subject, across thousands of subjects, in real-world conditions. The field needs a person to be able to put a helmet on in the morning, go about their day, and come home with eight hours of multi-modal neural data perfectly paired with the ambient video and audio.
如今，最先进的设备笨重、昂贵，被安置在一个没有窗户的房间里——这并不只是比喻。这对于发表论文来说没问题，但它与在真实条件下、对数千名受试者每人采集数万小时数据的目标格格不入。这个领域需要的是：一个人早上戴上头盔，照常度过一天，回家时带着八小时与环境视频和音频完美配对的多模态神经数据。
This does not necessarily imply that all data should be collected in fully uncontrolled environments with hardware that could withstand anything. Reality has a long tail of edge cases. In practice, in the short term, a significant amount of data will still be collected in semi-controlled environments. But even with marginal improvements, more portable hardware unlocks the flexibility to seamlessly record across a wide range of scenarios, that flexibility is exactly what can compound into vast, robust datasets.
这并不一定意味着所有数据都应在完全不受控的环境中、用能够承受任何情况的硬件来采集。现实中存在大量长尾的边缘情况。在实践中，短期内仍有相当一部分数据将在半受控环境中采集。但即使只是边际改进，更便携的硬件也能带来在广泛场景中无缝记录的灵活性，而正是这种灵活性，可以不断累积成庞大而稳健的数据集。
Pristine lab data will still matter, doing model fine-tuning and evaluation with high quality, low noise data still provides a significant advantage19, and we will always need those clinical devices. But for the vast bulk of the training, having slightly noisier data at massive scale is much more valuable.
纯净的实验室数据仍然重要：用高质量、低噪声的数据进行模型微调和评估仍有显著优势⟦19⟧，我们也始终需要那些临床设备。但对于绝大部分训练而言，大规模的、稍嘈杂一些的数据要有价值得多。
Sidenote · 旁注
Of course, there is a limit to how much noise a dataset can have while remaining useful, and historically, consumer neural recordings have been 10x or even 100x noisier than their lab counterparts, reducing their practical usefulness for training. However, as hardware is now improving quickly, that limitation is disappearing fast.
当然，一个数据集在保持有用的前提下能容纳的噪声是有限度的；从历史上看，消费级神经记录的噪声比实验室记录高 10 倍甚至 100 倍，降低了它们在训练中的实际用处。然而，随着硬件迅速改进，这一限制正在快速消失。
Two things follow.
由此得出两点。
First, the field needs more specialized hardware companies. Companies that focus on the scaling paradigm rather than the highest-signal-quality-at-any-cost paradigm. Hardware iteration is its own brutal discipline, treating it as a side-effect of an algorithm paper has held the field back for two decades. Conway’s Law20 applies here: organizations ship their structure, and an academic field has shipped academic hardware. Thankfully, more specialized hardware companies are finally seeing the light of day, the momentum is already shifting away from signal purity toward scaling.
第一，这个领域需要更多专业化的硬件公司。这些公司专注于规模化范式，而不是“不惜一切代价追求最高信号质量”的范式。硬件迭代本身就是一门残酷的学科，把它当作算法论文的副产品，已经拖累了这个领域二十年。康威定律（Conway’s Law）⟦20⟧在这里同样适用：组织交付的产品会反映其自身结构，而一个学术领域交付的就是学术化的硬件。所幸，更多专业化的硬件公司终于开始涌现，发展势头已经从追求信号纯度转向规模化。
19. The best strategy for training large brain models probably mirrors the one used in autonomous driving: train the base model on abundant but imperfect data (in their case simulated data), then fine-tune and validate it on smaller volumes of high-quality data (in their case real-world data). For BCIs, that would translate to pretraining on noisy consumer recordings, then fine-tuning and evaluating on cleaner lab data; there is already evidence that this works.
19. 训练大型脑模型的最佳策略，大概与自动驾驶领域的做法如出一辙：先在丰富但不完美的数据上训练基础模型（在自动驾驶中是模拟数据），再用少量高质量数据进行微调和验证（在自动驾驶中是真实世界数据）。对 BCI 而言，这意味着先在嘈杂的消费级记录上预训练，再在更干净的实验室数据上微调和评估；已有证据表明这种做法行之有效。
Second, the right design choices are different when scale is the goal. If portability is the first pillar of scale-fit hardware, multimodality is the second.
第二，当规模成为目标时，正确的设计选择也随之不同。如果说便携性是规模适配型硬件的第一根支柱，那么多模态就是第二根。
Many modalities can be used in tandem. MEG, fNIRS, and EEG measure entirely different physical phenomena; they do not interfere with each other, so you can stack them. When you are aiming for massive, robust datasets, capturing multiple biological signals at a low marginal cost is an obvious choice. The same way that modern LLMs are trained on text, videos and audio, brain foundation models should learn from multiple complementary biological signals. Multimodality should not be just a clever trick, it should be the sensible default.
多种模态可以协同使用。MEG、fNIRS 和 EEG 测量的是完全不同的物理现象；它们互不干扰，因此可以叠加使用。当你的目标是海量、稳健的数据集时，以较低的边际成本同时采集多种生物信号是显而易见的选择。正如现代大语言模型在文本、视频和音频上进行训练一样，大脑基础模型也应当从多种互补的生物信号中学习。多模态不应只是一个巧妙的技巧，而应是理所当然的默认选项。
Sidenote · 旁注
Additionally, in the past, models and sensors were mostly developed independently, with people making educated guesses about which changes to noninvasive sensors would most improve them. But if we now train large models directly on the sensor’s output, we don’t have to guess anymore, we can co-design sensors and models to create a very tight feedback loop.
此外，过去模型和传感器大多是各自独立开发的，人们只能凭经验猜测对非侵入式传感器做哪些改动最能提升效果。但如果我们现在直接在传感器的输出上训练大模型，就不必再猜了，我们可以协同设计传感器与模型，形成一个非常紧密的反馈回路。
Once you accept portability and multimodality as your foundation, the rest of the engineering constraints follow. Building for scale means solving for:
一旦你接受便携性和多模态作为基础，其余的工程约束便随之而来。为规模而构建，意味着要解决：
Manufacturing. How do you actually ship a million units? Context. Embedding cameras and microphones to give the neural data real-world grounding. Tolerance. Ensuring your sensor array can handle hair, sweat, physical motion, and the chaos of daily life. Standardization. One of the most critical and underweighted components. If your hardware outputs data that cannot be cleanly merged with another lab’s recordings, the data layer above it stays hopelessly uncoordinated.
制造：你究竟如何交付一百万台设备？情境：嵌入摄像头和麦克风，为神经数据提供真实世界的锚定。容错性：确保传感器阵列能够应对头发、汗水、身体运动以及日常生活的混乱。标准化：这是最关键却最被低估的环节之一。如果你的硬件输出的数据无法与另一个实验室的记录干净地合并，那么它上面的数据层就会始终陷于无望的各自为政。
The hardware bottleneck is slowly being overcome, but unevenly.
硬件瓶颈正在被缓慢克服，但进展并不均衡。
Each of the major non-invasive modalities (EEG, ultrasound, fNIRS, OPM-MEG, eventually NV- magnetometry) has started making the jump from “lab” to “wearable”. Butterfly Network put ultrasound on a chip, Kernel built a wearable fNIRS rig, Cerca Magnetics ships OPM arrays you wear.
每一种主要的非侵入式模态（EEG、超声、fNIRS、OPM-MEG，以及未来的 NV 色心磁力测量）都已开始从“实验室”迈向“可穿戴”。Butterfly Network 把超声做进了芯片，Kernel 打造了可穿戴的 fNIRS 设备，Cerca Magnetics 推出了可佩戴的 OPM 阵列。
20. Conway’s Law: software systems mirror the communication structures of the organizations that build them. Coined by Melvin Conway in 1968.
20. 康威定律：软件系统会映照出构建它们的组织的沟通结构。由梅尔文·康威（Melvin Conway）于 1968 年提出。
Hardware is also where the geopolitical reality sets in. China has been investing aggressively in BCI hardware over the last several years. Their five-year plan put BCI squarely inside the national strategic technology stack. China does not see BCI as a neuroscience curiosity anymore, it already sees it as a strategic asset.
硬件也是地缘政治现实开始显现的地方。过去几年，中国一直在积极投资 BCI 硬件。其五年规划明确将 BCI 纳入国家战略技术体系。中国已不再把 BCI 视为神经科学的新奇玩意儿，而是已经将其视为一项战略资产。
We have seen this movie before, the AI decade was a fight over chips; the defining geopolitical fact has been who can make them; this has come down to one Taiwanese fab, one Dutch lithography company, a handful of US export controls, and a trillion dollars of strategic anxiety on top. This race has already started for BCI hardware, if brain data becomes strategically important, the machines that collect it will become strategically important too. Within a few years, the TSMCs of brain hardware will start taking form, and China is clearly trying to make sure the answer is not automatically American.
这部电影我们以前看过：AI 的十年是一场围绕芯片的争夺；最具决定性的地缘政治事实是谁能制造芯片；最终这归结为一家台湾晶圆厂、一家荷兰光刻机公司、少数几项美国出口管制，以及叠加其上的价值万亿美元的战略焦虑。BCI 硬件领域的这场竞赛已经开始：如果脑数据变得具有战略重要性，那么采集它的机器也将变得具有战略重要性。几年之内，脑硬件领域的“台积电”（TSMC）将开始成形，而中国显然在努力确保答案不会自动是美国。
To sum it up, the point of this section is that the hardware unblock matters more than another decoder paper. The next leap in non-invasive BCI will not come from a smarter algorithm trained on a tiny dataset. It will come from sensors that can collect data at scale, sensors which can be worn by many people, for extended periods of time.
总而言之，本节的要点是：打通硬件比再写一篇解码器论文更重要。非侵入式 BCI 的下一次飞跃，不会来自在微小数据集上训练出的更聪明的算法。它将来自能够大规模采集数据的传感器——能被许多人长时间佩戴的传感器。
But sensors alone only produce raw numbers. Once the hardware to capture the signal at scale is no longer the bottleneck, the next one becomes: how to collect it at scale.
但仅有传感器，只能产出原始数字。一旦大规模捕获信号的硬件不再是瓶颈，下一个瓶颈就变成了：如何大规模地采集数据。
09 Level Two: The Data Scaling Argument
09 第二层：数据规模化论证
