“Simple models and a lot of data trump more elaborate models based on less data.”
“简单的模型加上大量数据，胜过基于较少数据的更复杂模型。”
— Halevy, Norvig, Pereira, The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Data, 2009
——哈勒维（Halevy）、诺维格（Norvig）、佩雷拉（Pereira），《数据的不合理有效性》，2009 年
The second layer of the pyramid is: Data.
金字塔的第二层是：数据。
For decades, the AI field chased clever symbolic methods, largely because it did not realize that brute force was a real option. The Bitter Lesson highlighted that mistake. The most uncomfortable thing about this outcome is that it is a deeply unsatisfying scientific result: the elegant theory of language did not produce GPT, scaling mindlessly did. That is in part why fields like BCI take so long to internalize the move.
几十年来，AI 领域一直在追逐巧妙的符号方法，很大程度上是因为它没有意识到蛮力是一个真实可行的选项。“苦涩的教训”突出了这一错误。这一结果最令人不适之处在于，它是一个在科学上极其令人不满足的结论：造就 GPT 的不是优雅的语言理论，而是无脑的规模化。这也是 BCI 等领域需要如此长时间才能接受这一转变的部分原因。
BCI today has a significant advantage over AI in 2017: the AI model already exists. It simply needs to collect the data. The field is starting to awaken to this and is currently crossing into its foundation- model era.21
与 2017 年的 AI 相比，今天的 BCI 拥有一个显著优势：AI 模型已经存在。它只需要采集数据。这个领域正开始意识到这一点，目前正在迈入它的基础模型时代。⟦21⟧
As the hardware is improving, scaling data collection becomes easier and datasets are growing, but BCI datasets are still nowhere near the scale that earned foundation models their name. The largest publicly available neural-recording datasets are tens of subjects, tens of hours each, single modality, mostly in a lab (ZuCo, DEAP, the Stanford handwriting corpus, the BCI Competition datasets). The most ambitious in-house corpora I am aware of are still under 10K hours total, and single modality.
随着硬件的改进，数据采集的规模化变得更容易，数据集也在增长，但 BCI 数据集仍远未达到让基础模型得名的那种规模。最大的公开神经记录数据集只有几十名受试者、每人几十小时、单一模态，而且大多在实验室中采集（ZuCo、DEAP、斯坦福手写语料库、BCI 竞赛数据集）。据我所知，最雄心勃勃的内部语料库总量仍不足 1 万小时，且为单一模态。
21. I have been circling this idea for a while, it just took my distracted self a while to actually get it on paper. Here are some receipts from 2024: “We need a new Moore’s law for brain mapping. Full brain mapping will arrive sooner than we think.” and “Direct brain to LLM communication probably coming sooner than we think.”
21. 我琢磨这个想法已经有一段时间了，只是我这个容易分心的人花了些时间才把它真正写下来。这里有一些 2024 年的“凭据”：“我们需要一条脑图谱绘制的新摩尔定律。全脑图谱的到来会比我们想象的更早。”以及“大脑与大语言模型的直接通信可能会比我们想象的来得更早。”
However, that picture is starting to change. NeuralBench v1.0 (Meta, May 2026) consolidates 13,603 hours of EEG across 9,478 subjects from 94 datasets. Models are scaling alongside: NeuroLM (Jiang et al., ICLR 2025) at 1.7B parameters on 25,000 hours of EEG; LaBraM (Jiang et al., ICLR 2024) at 369M and 2,500 hours.
然而，这一局面正开始改变。NeuralBench v1.0（Meta，2026 年 5 月）整合了来自 94 个数据集、9,478 名受试者的 13,603 小时 EEG 数据。模型也在同步扩展：NeuroLM（Jiang 等，ICLR 2025）拥有 17 亿参数，在 25,000 小时 EEG 上训练；LaBraM（Jiang 等，ICLR 2024）拥有 3.69 亿参数，在 2,500 小时数据上训练。
For comparison: GPT-3 (2020) was trained on roughly 300 billion tokens. Llama 3 (2024) used 15 trillion; Llama 4 (April 2025), 30 trillion. The effective stock of public text caps at roughly 300 trillion tokens. BCI has still a long way to go to be in AI scale territory.
作为对比：GPT-3（2020 年）在大约 3,000 亿个词元上训练。Llama 3（2024 年）使用了 15 万亿个；Llama 4（2025 年 4 月）使用了 30 万亿个。公开文本的有效存量上限约为 300 万亿个词元。BCI 要进入 AI 的规模区间，还有很长的路要走。
If you are inside the BCI field, this data scaling argument may sound obvious. To a meaningful chunk of your colleagues, it is not. The field’s culture has been “do clever things on small datasets, publish, repeat” for a long time. The move to “build the boring foundation first, and scale until the Bitter Lesson kicks in” can read as a step away from real science, it can feel less beautiful or clean, but it is also the move that worked everywhere else and that the BCI field needs.
如果你身处 BCI 领域，这一数据规模化论证听起来可能显而易见。但对你相当一部分同行来说，并非如此。长期以来，这个领域的文化一直是“在小数据集上做巧妙的事、发表、重复”。转向“先打好枯燥的基础，然后规模化，直到苦涩的教训显灵”，可能会被视为偏离真正的科学，它可能显得不那么优美或干净，但它也是在其他所有领域都奏效、而 BCI 领域所需要的做法。
Sidenote · 旁注
The data collection argument compounds in one more way. The massive BCI datasets produced by following this playbook are also very valuable to AI labs. Indeed, as we get closer to the data wall and alignment becomes a higher priority, AI labs are becoming more and more interested in alternative data sources. Therefore, a large dataset of human cognitive processes is both valuable to the BCI field as well as the AI field. The thesis is not just that BCI scales, it is that BCI scales and AI labs scale toward each other, following a self-reinforcing cycle. We will discuss this point more in depth in Part III.
数据采集的论证还会以另一种方式产生复利效应。遵循这套打法所产生的海量 BCI 数据集，对 AI 实验室也极具价值。事实上，随着我们越来越接近数据之墙、对齐的优先级越来越高，AI 实验室对替代数据来源越来越感兴趣。因此，一个关于人类认知过程的大型数据集，对 BCI 领域和 AI 领域都具有价值。本文的论点不仅仅是 BCI 可以规模化，而是 BCI 和 AI 实验室在规模化过程中相互靠拢，形成一个自我强化的循环。我们将在第三部分更深入地讨论这一点。
As datasets grow larger, training large models to decode the brain becomes more feasible. But to actually extract meaning effectively from this scale of data, the field has to abandon its custom algorithms and focus on the architectures and strategies that conquered language.
随着数据集越来越大，训练大模型来解码大脑变得更加可行。但要真正有效地从如此规模的数据中提取意义，这个领域必须放弃定制化算法，专注于那些征服了语言的架构和策略。
10 Level Three: Foundation Models for the Brain
10 第三层：大脑的基础模型
Data without models is not of much use, and so the next layer of the pyramid is the AI layer trained on the neural dataset.
没有模型的数据用处不大，因此金字塔的下一层是在神经数据集上训练的 AI 层。
The massive advantage BCI has today is that we do not need to invent the math. The AI playbook is already written. Little creativity is required on the architecture side, you do not need to reinvent the wheel, you take the exact transformer models that mastered language, tweak the ingestion layer to accept neural data, and hit run.22
BCI 今天拥有的巨大优势在于，我们不需要发明数学。AI 的打法已经写好了。架构层面几乎不需要什么创造力，你不必重新发明轮子，只需拿来那些精通语言的 Transformer 模型，调整输入层使其接受神经数据，然后按下运行键。⟦22⟧
Another page we must steal from the AI playbook is to build the foundation in the open. Right now, the BCI field lacks the data to even test the upper limits of a non-invasive brain decoder through scaling. But trying to hit that scale in a silo would be unwise, the way the AI field tackled this situation in its early days is through open collaboration. The foundational work at OpenAI, DeepMind, and FAIR was strikingly collaborative. The datasets were shared, the papers were openly published, and the models were generally released. The AI field eventually moved toward closed weights, but only after the foundation was laid. BCI is still firmly in the foundations-laying phase. Starting closed is bound to slow progress.
我们必须从 AI 打法中借鉴的另一页，是以开放的方式构建基础。眼下，BCI 领域甚至缺乏足够的数据来通过规模化检验非侵入式脑解码器的上限。但试图在孤岛中达到这种规模是不明智的，AI 领域早期应对这种局面的方式是开放协作。OpenAI、DeepMind 和 FAIR 的基础性工作具有惊人的协作性。数据集是共享的，论文是公开发表的，模型也大多是公开发布的。AI 领域后来转向了闭源权重，但那是在基础奠定之后。BCI 仍然牢牢处于奠基阶段。从一开始就封闭，必然会拖慢进展。
22. It will obviously be more complex than this but the point is that no breakthrough is required, mostly engineering.
22. 显然实际情况会比这复杂，但重点在于，这不需要任何突破，主要是工程问题。
A small set of startups and academics have begun pushing in this direction23, the ecosystem is growing. Because the AI field is already so mature, the models themselves are not the blocker right now. What it lacks is the massive corpus to train them at frontier scale and the empirical proof that frontier scale produces the same capability uplift for brains that it did for text.
一小批初创公司和学者已经开始朝这个方向推进⟦23⟧，生态正在壮大。由于 AI 领域已经如此成熟，模型本身目前并不是阻碍。它所缺少的，是以前沿规模训练这些模型的海量语料，以及前沿规模能够为大脑带来与文本同等能力跃升的经验证据。
There is also a compute tailwind that benefits BCI specifically. GPU performance per dollar has been roughly doubling every two years, and the cost of training any given model capability has collapsed by orders of magnitude in five years. A GPT-3-equivalent run that cost millions in 2020 now sits in the low six figures and keeps falling. BCI does not need to fund frontier-AI-scale compute today. By the time a brain corpus justifies frontier compute, the compute will cost a fraction of what frontier labs pay now. The hard problem of this decade is the data side; the compute side gets cheaper simply by waiting.
还有一股算力顺风专门有利于 BCI。每美元的 GPU 性能大约每两年翻一番，而训练出任一给定模型能力的成本在五年内下降了几个数量级。一次在 2020 年耗资数百万美元的 GPT-3 级训练，如今只需十几万美元，而且仍在下降。BCI 今天不需要为前沿 AI 规模的算力买单。等到某个脑语料库足以支撑前沿算力时，算力的成本将只是前沿实验室现在所支付费用的一小部分。本十年的难题在数据一侧；算力一侧只需等待就会变得更便宜。
We are now one Common Crawl and one foundation model away from BCI’s ChatGPT moment.
现在，我们距离 BCI 的 ChatGPT 时刻，只差一个 Common Crawl 和一个基础模型。
However, the biggest challenge in running the strategy that I have described is not the individual layers of the pyramid, it is the coordination across the field. As is often the case with deep tech, the hardest engineering problem is usually not the silicon or the math, it is human coordination.
然而，执行我所描述的这一策略，最大的挑战并不在金字塔的某一层，而在于整个领域的协调。正如深科技领域常见的那样，最难的工程问题通常不是硅片或数学，而是人与人之间的协调。
23. The broader open ecosystem has produced roughly a dozen brain foundation models since 2023: BrainLM, LaBraM, NeuroLM, REVE, POYO+, NDT3, LUNA, BENDR, BIOT, CBraMod, EEG-GPT, Neuro-GPT among them.
23. 自 2023 年以来，更广泛的开放生态系统已经产出了大约十几个大脑基础模型，其中包括 BrainLM、LaBraM、NeuroLM、REVE、POYO+、NDT3、LUNA、BENDR、BIOT、CBraMod、EEG-GPT、Neuro-GPT 等。
11 The Coordination Conundrum
11 协调困局
Coordinated scaling is something BCI has not yet seriously attempted. It runs through the three independent layers of the BCI pyramid (hardware, data and models). Without it, every team is solving the same problem from scratch.
协同规模化是 BCI 尚未认真尝试过的事情。它贯穿 BCI 金字塔中三个相互独立的层次（硬件、数据和模型）。没有它，每个团队都在从零开始解决同一个问题。
To understand why coordination is so important, we can have a look at the data itself. Brain data is inherently more complex than the text data used to train modern LLMs. Text is just discrete, standardized words whereas brain data is continuous, messy physics; it varies wildly depending on the hardware, the environment, and the human wearing the device.
要理解协调为何如此重要，我们可以看看数据本身。脑数据天生比训练现代大语言模型所用的文本数据更复杂。文本只是离散的、标准化的词语，而脑数据是连续的、杂乱的物理量；它会因硬件、环境以及佩戴设备的人的不同而产生巨大差异。
Because of this inherent complexity, most labs structure their data completely differently. There are attempts at standard formats (like BIDS (Brain Imaging Data Structure) and NWB (Neurodata Without Borders)), but their coverage is partial; outside their reach, formats range from MATLAB MAT files to custom HDF5 to bespoke CSV exports. The cost of this fragmentation is invisible to outsiders but painful to insiders: a paper trained on “eight datasets” usually means a poor soul spent six grueling weeks just reformatting files before training even began. Multiply that friction across every lab in the world and consolidating a unified dataset becomes a real challenge.
由于这种内在的复杂性，大多数实验室组织数据的方式完全不同。虽然已有一些标准格式的尝试（如 BIDS（脑成像数据结构，Brain Imaging Data Structure）和 NWB（无边界神经数据，Neurodata Without Borders）），但其覆盖范围有限；在其覆盖范围之外，格式五花八门，从 MATLAB 的 MAT 文件到自定义 HDF5，再到定制的 CSV 导出。这种碎片化的代价对外人来说是看不见的，但对业内人士来说却很痛苦：一篇在“八个数据集”上训练的论文，通常意味着某个可怜人在训练开始之前，就花了六个星期艰苦地重新整理文件格式。把这种摩擦乘以全世界的每一个实验室，整合出一个统一的数据集就成了一项真正的挑战。
The AI data revolution did not happen by accident. Common Crawl, ImageNet, and a handful of other artifacts standardized data collection, and the field compounded on top of them. This meant researchers could stop wasting time collecting images and start competing on algorithms. ImageNet alone seeded a decade of computer-vision progress because everyone trained on the same images and reported the same numbers. BCI desperately needs its ImageNet. But because brain data is so much harder to standardize than text or photos (and this is where my earlier analogy comparing the brain and the internet graph bends), establishing these conventions is going to be ten times harder, and ten times more important.24
AI 的数据革命并非偶然发生。Common Crawl、ImageNet 以及少数其他成果将数据采集标准化，整个领域在其基础上不断复利增长。这意味着研究人员可以不再浪费时间收集图像，转而在算法上展开竞争。仅 ImageNet 一项就催生了计算机视觉十年的进步，因为所有人都在同样的图像上训练、报告同样的指标。BCI 迫切需要属于自己的 ImageNet。但由于脑数据远比文本或照片更难标准化（这正是我前面把大脑与互联网图结构相类比的说法需要打折扣的地方），建立这些规范将难上十倍，也重要十倍。⟦24⟧
Underneath the file-format problem sits a deeper structural one. The traditional neuroscience lab is highly siloed. A lab buys a few commercial sensors, runs a highly controlled experiment on 20 people, cleans the data just enough to get a result, and publishes a paper. That structure is fantastic for publishing niche scientific insights. But it is completely useless for building industrialized datasets of billions of hours. Academia simply is not built to be a massive data factory. Furthermore, the academic incentive structure still rewards researchers for building clever algorithms on tiny datasets, not for doing the boring, unglamorous work of standardizing massive ones. Expecting this system to spontaneously generate a unified, global data standard is like trying to herd academic cats. Tenured principal investigators have spent their entire careers being rewarded for being unique and clever, not for acting like a compliant, standardized cog in a massive industrial data factory. This is in some ways a brain-coded Tragedy of the Commons.
在文件格式问题之下，还潜藏着一个更深层的结构性问题。传统的神经科学实验室是高度孤立的。一个实验室买几台商用传感器，对 20 个人进行一项高度受控的实验，把数据清洗到刚好能得出结果的程度，然后发表论文。这种结构非常适合发表小众的科学见解，但对于构建数十亿小时的工业化数据集则毫无用处。学术界根本不是为成为一座庞大的数据工厂而建立的。此外，学术激励结构仍然奖励研究人员在小数据集上构建巧妙的算法，而不是去做标准化海量数据集这种枯燥、不光鲜的工作。指望这个体系自发地产生一个统一的全球数据标准，就像试图驱赶一群学术界的猫。终身教职的首席研究员们整个职业生涯都因与众不同和聪明巧妙而受到奖励，而不是因为在一座庞大的工业数据工厂中充当顺从、标准化的齿轮。从某种意义上说，这是一场以大脑为编码的“公地悲剧”。
24. An early example of the kind of standardization this argument calls for: Meta AI’s NeuralBench, released in May 2026, is an open-source benchmarking framework that unifies 94 brain-data datasets across 14 deep-learning architectures, with initial focus on EEG and extensions to MEG and fMRI. One of the first attempts at a shared evaluation dataset for the field.
24. 这一论点所呼吁的那种标准化的一个早期例子：Meta AI 于 2026 年 5 月发布的 NeuralBench，是一个开源基准测试框架，它在 14 种深度学习架构上统一了 94 个脑数据数据集，最初侧重于 EEG，并扩展到 MEG 和 fMRI。这是为该领域建立共享评估数据集的最早尝试之一。
ARPANET in December 1969. The largest coordination system we ever built, the internet, started with just four nodes on a sheet of paper.
1969 年 12 月的阿帕网（ARPANET）。我们所建立过的最大的协调系统——互联网——最初只是一张纸上的四个节点。
Coordination is not just a data-layer concern. Hardware standardization matters too: if your device’s output cannot be cleanly merged with another lab’s recordings, the data layer above stays uncoordinated. Likewise, open foundation models are coordination artifacts in their own right; they let the field share the cost of training one decoder rather than every lab training their own. Each of the three layers has a coordination dimension.
协调不仅仅是数据层的问题。硬件标准化同样重要：如果你的设备输出无法与另一个实验室的记录干净地合并，那么上层的数据层就会始终各自为政。同样，开放的基础模型本身就是协调的产物；它们让整个领域分摊训练一个解码器的成本，而不必每个实验室各自训练自己的模型。三个层次中的每一层都有其协调维度。
And because the field is fragmented, the three layers of the pyramid, hardware, data, and models, are stuck in a classic cold-start trap.
而由于该领域高度碎片化，金字塔的三个层次——硬件、数据和模型——陷入了一个典型的冷启动陷阱。
Hardware companies cannot justify scale-out until the data layer creates demand for it. Data collectors cannot gather massive volume until hardware becomes cheap and portable enough, and before BCI demand for data increases. Model builders cannot justify frontier compute costs until the massive datasets exist to train on.
在数据层产生需求之前，硬件公司无法证明扩大规模的合理性。在硬件变得足够便宜和便携、且 BCI 对数据的需求增加之前，数据采集者无法采集海量数据。在可供训练的海量数据集出现之前，模型构建者无法证明前沿算力成本的合理性。
Each layer is rationally waiting for the layer below to move first. As a result, the whole stack stays stuck at lab scale by default. The way out is for every layer to start building for the anticipated scale the layer above will eventually require, not the scale current demand rewards. That is the coordinated strategy transition this decade needs to unstick the cold start.
每一层都在理性地等待下一层先动。结果，整个技术栈默认停留在实验室规模。出路在于，每一层都开始按照上一层最终将需要的预期规模来构建，而不是按照当前需求所奖励的规模来构建。这正是本十年为摆脱冷启动所需要的协同战略转变。
The underlying technology is finally ready. The remaining challenge is to coordinate scaling as BCI transitions from a scientific problem into an engineering problem. To succeed, non-invasive BCI now requires founder energy, massive capital, and a tolerance for brute-force engineering that the academic system simply was not designed to deliver.
底层技术终于准备就绪。剩下的挑战是，在 BCI 从科学问题转变为工程问题的过程中协调规模化。要取得成功，非侵入式 BCI 现在需要创始人的干劲、巨额资本，以及对蛮力工程的容忍度，而这些恰恰是学术体系在设计上根本无法提供的。
Now, if we assume the field successfully executes this playbook, where would that actually lead us? And what exactly are we building?
那么，如果假设这个领域成功执行了这套打法，它究竟会把我们带向何方？我们到底在构建什么？
12 The Two Ladders
12 两架阶梯
If the playbook works and compounds, how do we actually measure success? To answer this question, we will use two distinct “ladders of progress”. The first measures what a device can actually read. The second measures how much effort a user has to invest before the device works for them.
如果这套打法奏效并不断累积，我们究竟如何衡量成功？为了回答这个问题，我们将使用两架不同的“进步阶梯”。第一架衡量设备实际能读取什么。第二架衡量用户在设备对其生效之前需要投入多少精力。
We will call them the capability ladder and the calibration ladder. They represent the two main dimensions which define BCI progress.
我们把它们称为能力阶梯和校准阶梯。它们代表了定义 BCI 进展的两个主要维度。
The Capability Ladder
能力阶梯
The capability ladder represents the raw decoding power of the BCI: how detailed the extracted information is, and how reliably the device can read it. Each rung on the capability ladder corresponds to roughly one order of magnitude better signal quality (driven by better hardware, more data, larger models), which in turn unlocks a new class of product that was previously impossible.
能力阶梯代表 BCI 的原始解码能力：提取的信息有多详细，以及设备能多可靠地读取这些信息。能力阶梯上的每一级，大致对应信号质量提升一个数量级（由更好的硬件、更多的数据、更大的模型驱动），而这又会解锁一类此前不可能实现的新产品。
Non-invasive hardware fundamentally has a lower ceiling on this ladder than invasive surgery. At the limit, a sensor outside the brain will never be able to beat one within the brain. A wearable sensor will never read a single, isolated neuron the way a surgically implanted electrode array does. But the crucial reality of the BCI field today is that no one actually knows where that non-invasive ceiling is, and we have barely begun to climb the ladder.
从根本上说，非侵入式硬件在这架阶梯上的上限低于侵入式手术。在极限情况下，大脑外部的传感器永远无法胜过大脑内部的传感器。可穿戴传感器永远无法像手术植入的电极阵列那样读取单个孤立的神经元。但当今 BCI 领域的关键现实是：没有人真正知道非侵入式的上限在哪里，而我们才刚刚开始攀登这架阶梯。
These ladders illustrate the technological milestones BCI can achieve as it matures:
这两架阶梯展示了 BCI 在成熟过程中可以实现的技术里程碑：
Emotional and physiological biomarkers. Your device could record and monitor your emotional state continuously. Knowing when you are happy, sad, angry, stressed, etc.… We are already scratching the surface of this today.
情绪与生理生物标志物。你的设备可以持续记录和监测你的情绪状态。知道你何时快乐、悲伤、愤怒、紧张等等……今天我们已经在这方面初窥门径。
Attention and focus tracking. The device knows when you are deep in thought versus when your mind has wandered. Tracking focus could help improve the ability to do deep work and has numerous health applications. This is also starting to become possible.
注意力与专注度追踪。设备能知道你何时在深度思考，何时走神了。追踪专注度有助于提升深度工作的能力，并有众多健康领域的应用。这也开始变得可能了。
Sub-vocal text. This is the rung that changes daily life. Almost-mouth a word, and watch it appear on the screen before your jaw has finished moving with a lag below the threshold of human notice. This allows people to work hours without touching the keyboard once. We already have early lab instances of this technology.
默读文本（sub-vocal text）。这是改变日常生活的一级。你几乎只是动了动嘴唇念出一个词，就能看到它出现在屏幕上，甚至在你的下颌停止运动之前，延迟低到人类无法察觉。这让人们可以连续工作数小时而不碰一次键盘。我们已经有了这项技术早期的实验室实例。
Once you reach higher rungs, things get stranger.
一旦到达更高的阶梯，事情就变得更加奇特。
Silent inner-speech recognition. The words you are thinking, decoded without any muscle activation at all. You compose a message in a meeting and nobody around you sees a single muscle move. There are very early, successful lab demonstrations of inner-speech decoding.
无声内语识别。你正在思考的词语，在完全没有肌肉活动的情况下被解码出来。你在会议中撰写一条消息，周围没有人看到你动一块肌肉。目前已有非常早期但成功的内语解码实验室演示。
Visual concept reconstruction. Hold an image in your mind’s eye and your phone shows you a still of it. You can sketch by remembering. Surprisingly, there are some early successes here too; this is less sci-fi than it might seem.
视觉概念重建。在你的“心灵之眼”中浮现一幅图像，你的手机就会向你展示它的静态画面。你可以凭记忆作画。令人惊讶的是，这方面也已经有了一些早期成功；它并不像看上去那么科幻。
Multi-modal imagination decoding. Not just a still image anymore, the full imagined scene with sound and motion. What a memory feels like to you could be exported with reasonable fidelity to a file that other people can open.
多模态想象解码。不再只是一幅静态图像，而是带有声音和动作的完整想象场景。一段记忆对你来说是什么样的感受，可以以相当的保真度导出为一个其他人能够打开的文件。
Episodic memory replay. A recording, in your own native cognitive format, of an hour from last Saturday, which could be played back by you as cleanly as you played it back from inside your own head.
情景记忆回放。以你自己原生的认知格式录下的上周六的一个小时，你可以像在自己脑海中回放一样清晰地将其回放出来。
Continuous semantic transcription. A real-time transcript of your thoughts. Or in other words: mind reading. The moment this becomes technically possible, every privacy question surrounding this topic will come forward at once. Before this rung can ever reach a consumer, the field has to design an entirely new kind of social firewall, because navigating the societal implications of mind reading will be vastly harder than writing the algorithm itself at this stage.
连续语义转录。对你思想的实时转录。换句话说：读心术。一旦这在技术上成为可能，围绕这一话题的所有隐私问题将同时涌现。在这一级能够触达消费者之前，这个领域必须设计出一种全新的社会防火墙，因为在这个阶段，应对读心术的社会影响将远比编写算法本身困难得多。
The top of the ladder is something we do not yet have a clean word for: a full real-time bidirectional channel between human cognition and machine reasoning, where the boundary between “what I thought” and “what we computed together” stops being legible. This is what some call the Merge.
阶梯的顶端，是我们目前还没有一个清晰词汇来描述的东西：人类认知与机器推理之间完整、实时的双向通道，在那里，“我想到的”和“我们共同计算出来的”之间的界限将不再清晰可辨。这就是一些人所说的“融合”（the Merge）。
The point of the ladder is to illustrate how far a non-invasive BCI could get us and what it could unlock. You only need the bottom rung to ship at consumer quality to have the rest follow through the flywheel effect. The first wave of non-invasive BCI will likely look like something modestly useful, probably wellness wearables, and then from there, every generation will have exponential gains in capabilities. We are now standing at the edge of the first wave.
这架阶梯的意义，在于说明非侵入式 BCI 能把我们带到多远，以及它可能解锁什么。只需最底层的一级达到消费级品质并推出，其余各级就会通过飞轮效应接踵而至。第一波非侵入式 BCI 很可能看起来只是适度有用，大概是健康类可穿戴设备，然后从那里开始，每一代产品都会在能力上实现指数级提升。我们现在正站在第一波浪潮的边缘。
The Calibration Ladder
校准阶梯
But there is a hidden catch. The capability ladder assumes the device already understands you.
但这里有一个隐藏的问题。能力阶梯的前提是设备已经理解了你。
In reality, brains are like fingerprints, no two are wired exactly the same. That means every single brain has to be decoded differently. Right now, a BCI is like a custom-tailored suit; it only fits the exact person it was measured for. Before a device can decode your thoughts, there is a calibration period where the model has to learn your specific neural “accent”.
现实中，大脑就像指纹，没有两个大脑的连接方式完全相同。这意味着每一个大脑都必须以不同方式解码。目前，BCI 就像一套量身定制的西装；它只适合为之量体的那个人。在设备能够解码你的思想之前，需要一个校准期，模型必须在此期间学习你特有的神经“口音”。
This added friction is the single biggest bottleneck to building a consumer device. In a lab, a graduate student might be willing to patiently train a decoder for six hours but, in the real world, a consumer has zero patience. They will throw the device in a drawer if it does not work almost immediately.
这种额外的摩擦，是打造消费级设备的最大单一瓶颈。在实验室里，一个研究生也许愿意耐心地花六个小时训练解码器；但在现实世界中，消费者的耐心为零。如果设备不能几乎立即生效，他们就会把它扔进抽屉。
That brings us to the second ladder, running along the capability one, and arguably more important for having non-invasive BCI used outside of the lab. I call it the calibration ladder; how much effort each new user has to invest before the device works for them.
这就引出了第二架阶梯，它与能力阶梯并行，而且对于让非侵入式 BCI 走出实验室而言，可以说更为重要。我称之为校准阶梯：每个新用户在设备对其生效之前需要投入多少精力。
The capability ladder is what the device can read; the calibration ladder is how much friction the read has.
能力阶梯衡量设备能读取什么；校准阶梯衡量这种读取需要付出多少摩擦。
The expectation is that pretraining across many brains collapses the marginal cost of decoding a new brain, exactly the way zero-shot learning solved this exact problem for language models.
我们预期，跨越众多大脑的预训练将使解码一个新大脑的边际成本骤降，这与零样本学习（zero-shot learning）为语言模型解决这一完全相同问题的方式如出一辙。
The strategy described in this essay is to scale non-invasive BCI, using the same scaling strategies that AI has used over the past decade, to build a first generation of BCI consumer products in order to ignite a flywheel effect which will in turn accelerate the BCI field as a whole.
本文所描述的策略是：利用 AI 在过去十年中所使用的同样的规模化策略来规模化非侵入式 BCI，打造第一代 BCI 消费级产品，从而点燃飞轮效应，进而加速整个 BCI 领域的发展。
The biggest unaddressed remaining unknown is whether non-invasive BCI has a high enough ceiling on the capability and calibration ladders to achieve this goal. The honest answer is that the entire field is currently staring into a fog of war.
剩下最大的未解之谜是：非侵入式 BCI 在能力阶梯和校准阶梯上的上限是否足够高，足以实现这一目标。诚实的回答是，整个领域目前正凝视着一片“战争迷雾”。
13 The Unknown Ceiling
13 未知的上限
“We tend to overestimate the effect of a technology in the short run and underestimate the effect in the long run.” — Roy Amara (Amara’s Law)
“我们往往高估一项技术的短期影响，而低估其长期影响。”——罗伊·阿马拉（Roy Amara）（阿马拉定律）
How far does this playbook actually take us? With what timeline? And will the physics even allow it? Well, nobody actually knows.
这套打法究竟能把我们带多远？时间表如何？物理学又是否允许？其实没有人真正知道。
The historical track record of high-confidence BCI predictions is mostly a graveyard of wrong timelines. I am offering you mine anyway.
BCI 高置信度预测的历史记录，大多是一座错误时间表的坟场。但我还是要给出我的预测。
The bear case is that the skull, the scalp, and the Earth’s magnetic field combine into a noise floor that may cap how much signal we can pull from outside the head. If that worst case turns out to be true, non-invasive caps out at “modestly useful for medical contexts”, and the only path to high-bandwidth thought decoding is invasive surgery.
悲观的情形是：颅骨、头皮和地球磁场共同构成一个噪声下限，可能会限制我们从头部外部提取的信号量。如果这种最坏情况成真，非侵入式的上限就止步于“在医疗场景中适度有用”，而实现高带宽思想解码的唯一途径就是侵入式手术。
But here is the reality on the ground: we are nowhere near hitting that wall in actual experiments. The biggest non-invasive datasets are still single-modality, with under 10K hours on just a few dozen subjects, no theoretical argument can override this empirical fact. All the current empirical experiments we have point in the exact same direction: straight up the log-linear scaling law curve, the only way to know where the summit lies is by climbing towards it.
但实际情况是：在真实的实验中，我们离撞上那堵墙还差得很远。最大的非侵入式数据集仍然是单一模态的，只有几十名受试者、不到 1 万小时，任何理论论证都无法推翻这一经验事实。我们目前所有的经验实验都指向完全相同的方向：沿着对数线性规模定律曲线一路向上；要知道顶峰在哪里，唯一的办法就是朝它攀登。
Earlier in this essay, if you remember the three S-curves, we illustrated the theoretical upper bounds of what each modality could achieve. This was just an illustration; in practice, we are standing at the absolute bottom of the curve, looking up into the dark.
如果你还记得本文前面的三条 S 曲线，我们曾用它们说明每种模态理论上所能达到的上限。那只是示意；实际上，我们正站在曲线的最底部，抬头望向黑暗。
For this scaling thesis to work and truly compound, the hardware must clear the friction bar for everyday consumer adoption, and we already have early evidence that this is happening. Just as we saw with language models, BCI scaling laws are already becoming visible in the lab, and more predictable.
要让这一规模化论点成立并真正产生复利效应，硬件必须跨过日常消费者采用的摩擦门槛，而我们已经有了这一点正在发生的早期证据。正如我们在语言模型上看到的那样，BCI 的规模定律已经在实验室中显现，并且变得更可预测。
If we assume the trajectory holds, then in the best-case scenario, within three to six years we will see the first ten million non-invasive brain interfaces deployed in the wild. Within six years, they will evolve from early-adopter tech into a ubiquitous layer of everyday computing. The scaling laws will feed on this massive new data stream, creating a flywheel that makes the field exponentially more attractive to capital and talent.
如果假设这一轨迹持续下去，那么在最好的情况下，三到六年内我们将看到首批一千万台非侵入式脑接口在真实世界中部署。六年之内，它们将从早期采用者的技术演变为日常计算中无处不在的一层。规模定律将以这股庞大的新数据流为养料，形成一个飞轮，使该领域对资本和人才的吸引力呈指数级增长。
So where does that actually leave us on our two ladders? How high do we expect to be able to climb before hitting the ceiling?
那么，这会让我们在两架阶梯上处于什么位置？在撞上天花板之前，我们预计能爬多高？
The Capability Ladder: Inner intent, inner emotion, silent speech recognition, and visual concept reconstruction are all high-probability outcomes without surgery. There are already early signals of this working today. Full multi-modal imagination decoding (exporting a memory with sound and motion) has a low but non-zero probability non-invasively.
能力阶梯：内在意图、内在情绪、无声言语识别和视觉概念重建，都是无需手术就很可能实现的结果。如今已有这些方向奏效的早期信号。完整的多模态想象解码（导出带有声音和动作的记忆）以非侵入方式实现的概率较低，但并非为零。
The Calibration Ladder: Thanks to foundation models trained on population-scale data, the calibration tax will collapse. Just as modern speech AI instantly understands your voice without calibration the first time it hears you, BCI foundation models will recognize your neural patterns with minimal calibration because they have already seen thousands of other brains. We will reach a point where a new device takes only minutes to adapt to a new brain.
校准阶梯：得益于在人群规模数据上训练的基础模型，校准成本将骤降。正如现代语音 AI 第一次听到你的声音时无需校准就能立即理解一样，BCI 基础模型将只需极少校准就能识别你的神经模式，因为它们已经见过数以千计的其他大脑。我们将到达这样一个阶段：一台新设备只需几分钟就能适应一个新大脑。
This manifesto relies on the conviction that the ceiling reachable with non-invasive BCI is much further away than current consensus assumes. Since theory won’t settle it, the only way to verify this conviction is to actually run the experiment.
本宣言依托于这样一个信念：非侵入式 BCI 所能达到的上限，远比当前共识所假设的要高。既然理论无法解决这个问题，验证这一信念的唯一方法就是真正去做实验。
But running the AI playbook requires a massive amount of data, and while for the first few orders of magnitude we can collect the data in the lab, at a certain scale, it becomes intractable. It will only scale if millions of people actually wear the hardware. To trigger this rapid scaling loop, the field has to step out of the lab and answer a very practical question: what actually convinces the first ten million everyday consumers to put a brain-computer interface on their heads?
但执行 AI 打法需要海量数据。在最初几个数量级内，我们可以在实验室里采集数据，但到了一定规模，这就变得难以为继。只有当数百万人真正佩戴这些硬件时，它才能实现规模化。要触发这一快速规模化循环，这个领域必须走出实验室，回答一个非常实际的问题：究竟是什么能说服首批一千万名普通消费者把脑机接口戴到头上？
14 The Consumer Wedge
14 消费级楔子
“Any sufficiently advanced technology is indistinguishable from magic.” — Arthur C.
“任何足够先进的技术都与魔法无异。”——亚瑟·C·克拉克
We have spent a lot of time on theory, but it helps to ground our thoughts in reality. And even if we alluded to it, we have not yet clearly illustrated what the first wave of non-invasive BCI products could actually look like.
我们在理论上花了大量篇幅，但把思考落到现实中会有所帮助。而且，即便我们有所暗示，也还没有清楚地说明第一波非侵入式 BCI 产品实际上可能是什么样子。
In Section 9, we stared down a four-order-of-magnitude gap between the data AI uses for frontier models and the data BCI currently has. To close this gap, we need orders of magnitude more users, millions of people using BCI on a regular basis.
在第 9 节中，我们直面了 AI 用于前沿模型的数据与 BCI 目前拥有的数据之间四个数量级的差距。要弥合这一差距，我们需要多出几个数量级的用户——数百万人定期使用 BCI。
This raises an obvious question. Who on earth are these 10 million people, and what exactly are they putting on their heads?
这就引出了一个显而易见的问题：这一千万人究竟是谁？他们到底要往头上戴什么？
Normal people do not buy abstract scientific milestones, they do not care about “log-linear scaling laws” or “foundation models”, they buy a product because it solves a specific problem or feels like magic.
普通人不会为抽象的科学里程碑买单，他们不关心“对数线性规模定律”或“基础模型”；他们购买一款产品，是因为它解决了一个具体问题，或者感觉像魔法一样。
Sidenote · 旁注
Big tech is already positioning itself for this transition. In 2025 Apple introduced the “Human Interface Device” protocol which allows BCI to register as first-class input on the iPhone.
科技巨头们已经在为这一转变布局。2025 年，苹果（Apple）推出了“人机接口设备”（Human Interface Device）协议，允许 BCI 在 iPhone 上注册为一级输入方式。
The wedge that gets 10 million devices onto 10 million heads is probably not going to be one single, monolithic killer app. It will be a cluster of “first-good-apps”, products that offer enough immediate, undeniable utility to overcome the physical friction of strapping a new piece of hardware to your skull. Early adopters might tolerate a clunky wearable just because of the sci-fi novelty, but to hit true consumer scale, the utility still has to be obvious.
让一千万台设备戴上一千万个头的那个“楔子”，很可能不会是某一个单一的、庞大的杀手级应用。它将是一组“首批好应用”：这些产品能提供足够直接、无可否认的实用价值，足以克服把一件新硬件绑到头骨上的身体摩擦。早期采用者也许会仅仅因为科幻般的新奇感而容忍一款笨重的可穿戴设备，但要达到真正的消费级规模，实用性仍必须一目了然。
Two primary breakout products look most plausible to cross the chasm first.
有两款主要的突破性产品看起来最有可能率先跨越鸿沟。
The first is frictionless communication, aka telepathy, talking to machines without using your thumbs or your vocal cords. It would unlock sub-vocal text and intent decoding for smart glasses, coding companions, and AI assistants; making it faster than touch, quieter than voice, and ambient by default. It also provides hands-free augmented reality control (like the Meta Neural Band shape) and forms a critical accessibility bridge for users who cannot speak or type comfortably.
第一款是无摩擦沟通，也就是“心灵感应”：不用拇指、也不用声带就能与机器对话。它将为智能眼镜、编程助手和 AI 助理解锁默读文本和意图解码；使交互比触摸更快、比语音更安静，并且默认环境式存在。它还能提供免手动的增强现实控制（类似 Meta Neural Band 的形态），并为无法舒适地说话或打字的用户架起一座关键的无障碍桥梁。
The advantage comes from bandwidth and seamlessness, reading directly from the brain allows us to capture much higher-fidelity intent than a keyboard or microphone ever could. In the beginning, the raw read will not be perfectly accurate, but it does not have to be. Smart product design will mask the early hardware limitations, and flawless, paragraph-length thought-to-text will come later; the first wave will likely rely on high-level intent reading and coarse-grain indicators. As we pair coarse telepathy with an AI that is smart enough to infer the rest of your context, it will start feeling like magic25.
其优势来自带宽和无缝性：直接从大脑读取，能让我们捕捉到远比键盘或麦克风高保真的意图。起初，原始读取不会完全准确，但也不必如此。巧妙的产品设计将掩盖早期的硬件局限，完美的、段落级的“思想转文字”会在之后到来；第一波产品很可能依赖高层次的意图读取和粗粒度指标。当我们把粗略的心灵感应与一个足够聪明、能够推断你其余上下文的 AI 结合起来时，它就会开始感觉像魔法⟦25⟧。
The second is wellness and recovery, the Whoop for the brain. The longevity movement brings intense consumer appetite for neural interfaces. Users have already adopted rings and wristbands to track their physical recovery; a neural wearable provides the missing supervision layer by tracking the central nervous system readiness, emotional states and passive continuous stress tracking. This enables numerous other adjacent applications requiring visibility into the cognitive state such as professional fatigue auditing in high-stakes environments like surgery or air traffic control.
第二款是健康与恢复，也就是“大脑版的 Whoop”。长寿运动带来了消费者对神经接口的强烈兴趣。用户已经开始用戒指和腕带来追踪身体恢复情况；神经可穿戴设备则通过追踪中枢神经系统的准备状态、情绪状态以及被动的持续压力监测，补上了缺失的监测层。这还能带来许多其他需要了解认知状态的相关应用，例如在手术或空中交通管制等高风险环境中对专业人员进行疲劳审计。
Other plausible first good applications of non-invasive BCI include:
非侵入式 BCI 其他可能的首批好应用包括：
Brain-Aware Environments: Software that is no longer blind to your internal state. An empathetic AI assistant can ingest your cognitive load as part of its prompt context, giving shorter, punchier answers when you are exhausted, providing deeper analogies when it senses you are confused, or entirely suppressing notifications when you are in a flow state. Similarly, study software can track attention and focus, adapting a lesson plan the moment your mind wanders to lunch. The Inner Space Stack: Tools designed for exploring and optimizing human consciousness. This includes closed-loop meditation apps that track a genuine neural “depth score” and ring a gentle mindfulness bell when focus breaks; sleep and dream products that track REM cycles for lucid- dreaming cues or trigger smart alarms at your lightest sleep moment26; and therapeutic tools for optimizing mental health and clinical depression.
脑感知环境：软件不再对你的内在状态视而不见。一个善解人意的 AI 助理可以将你的认知负荷纳入提示上下文：在你精疲力竭时给出更简短、更精炼的回答；在察觉到你困惑时提供更深入的类比；或者在你处于心流状态时完全屏蔽通知。同样，学习软件可以追踪注意力和专注度，在你的思绪飘向午餐的那一刻调整课程计划。内在空间技术栈：用于探索和优化人类意识的工具。这包括闭环冥想应用，它追踪真实的神经“深度评分”，并在专注中断时敲响轻柔的正念铃声；睡眠与梦境产品，追踪快速眼动（REM）周期以提供清醒梦提示，或在你睡眠最浅的时刻触发智能闹钟⟦26⟧；以及用于优化心理健康和治疗临床抑郁症的治疗工具。
25. Once embedded into consumer devices, BCI will also come with numerous new UX questions: When should the system act on a thought, and how fast? Which thoughts matter, and which are just passing cognition? How to build a thought native UI interface? How to translate different mental mechanisms across people into actions? Overall, this will demand a UX overhaul of our way to interact with the digital world even more significant than the ones the internet and AI brought.
25. 一旦嵌入消费级设备，BCI 还会带来许多新的用户体验（UX）问题：系统应在何时、以多快的速度根据一个想法采取行动？哪些想法重要，哪些只是一闪而过的念头？如何构建以思想为原生输入的用户界面？如何把不同人之间各异的心理机制转化为行动？总体而言，这将要求我们对与数字世界交互的方式进行一次 UX 大改造，其意义甚至超过互联网和 AI 带来的变革。
The Creative Engine: Thought-to-image generation as a professional creative tool. Instead of typing complex text prompts into an image generator, a designer holds a visual concept in their mind’s eye and watches the system render a rough draft on screen. This translates to music and movie creation as well, any medium that captures a mental state and tries to translate it into creative software could benefit from this.
创意引擎：把“思想生成图像”作为专业创作工具。设计师不再需要向图像生成器输入复杂的文字提示词，而是在心灵之眼中构想一个视觉概念，然后看着系统在屏幕上渲染出一份草图。这同样适用于音乐和电影创作，任何试图捕捉一种心理状态并将其转化到创作软件中的媒介，都可以从中受益。
Once 10 million devices are used in the wild everything accelerates, manufacturing costs collapse, the sensor stack becomes commoditized, data becomes plentiful and the foundation decoder models become shared public goods.
一旦有一千万台设备在真实世界中使用，一切都会加速：制造成本骤降，传感器栈变得商品化，数据变得充裕，基础解码模型成为共享的公共产品。
This consumer flywheel strategy can shorten the timeline for progress significantly. By shifting the field out of the slow, methodical timeline of academic tenure tracks and clinical trials, non-invasive BCI gains a massive structural advantage, it allows data to accumulate at scale. Given the staggering velocity of frontier AI development, this consumer-led explosion is the only path moving fast enough for BCI to have a shot at keeping up with frontier AI, and for humans to remain in the loop.
这一消费级飞轮策略可以大幅缩短进步的时间线。通过让该领域摆脱学术终身教职轨道和临床试验那种缓慢、按部就班的时间线，非侵入式 BCI 获得了巨大的结构性优势：它让数据得以大规模积累。鉴于前沿 AI 发展速度之惊人，这种由消费者引领的爆发，是唯一一条足够快的路径，能让 BCI 有机会跟上前沿 AI，也让人类得以留在回路之中。
If BCI, and neurotechnology overall, is going to play a role in keeping humans in the loop during the transition to superintelligence, it has to catch up, and it has to do it fast. It certainly cannot move at the agonizingly slow pace of invasive surgery, which is why the non-invasive consumer loop is the only vector fast enough to outrun an impending AI takeoff within the narrow window left to pull it off.
如果 BCI 以及整个神经技术要在向超级智能过渡的过程中为让人类留在回路中发挥作用，它就必须迎头赶上，而且必须快。它当然不能以侵入式手术那种慢得令人痛苦的速度前进，这就是为什么非侵入式消费级循环是唯一一条足够快的途径，能够在所剩无几的狭窄窗口内跑赢即将到来的 AI 起飞。
26. If dream data proves useful at all for training brain decoders, then sleep could become one of the most effective ways to collect neural data at scale.
26. 如果梦境数据被证明对训练脑解码器确实有用，那么睡眠可能成为大规模采集神经数据最有效的方式之一。
The dense vascular network of a living brain, traced vessel by vessel. Visualization by Aleph Neuro.
活体大脑中致密的血管网络，逐根血管描绘。可视化：Aleph Neuro。
15 Phase Lock
15 相位锁定
“The hope is that, in not too many years, human brains and computing machines will be coupled together very tightly, and that the resulting partnership will think as no human brain has ever thought …” — J. C. R. Licklider, Man-Computer Symbiosis, 1960
“我们希望，用不了太多年，人脑与计算机器将被非常紧密地耦合在一起，由此形成的伙伴关系将以任何人脑都从未有过的方式思考……”——J. C. R. 利克莱德（J. C. R. Licklider），《人机共生》（Man-Computer Symbiosis），1960 年
AGI is coming soon. At this point it is no longer a distant thought experiment, we are all expecting the future to arrive early, and that accelerated timeline raises a foundational question: how do we actually keep humans in the loop?
AGI 即将到来。到了这个时候，它已不再是一个遥远的思想实验，我们都预期未来会提前到来，而这条加速的时间线提出了一个根本性的问题：我们究竟如何让人类留在回路之中？
Frontier AI is moving ever faster, with flagship models now released monthly, sometimes faster. The window of opportunity to shape what happens next is terrifyingly narrow. These are the last few years of the world as we know it. Metaculus, a prediction website with an impressive track record, currently estimates that weak AGI will be reached by mid 2028. That leaves us with a couple of years, at most, before we fully cross the intelligence threshold and enter the New World.
前沿 AI 正以越来越快的速度前进，旗舰模型如今每月都会发布，有时甚至更快。塑造接下来所发生之事的机会之窗窄得令人恐惧。这是我们所熟知的世界的最后几年。Metaculus 是一个预测记录令人印象深刻的预测网站，它目前估计弱 AGI 将在 2028 年年中实现。这让我们最多只剩几年时间，之后我们就将彻底跨越智能门槛，进入新世界。
The critical decisions, what these models learn to optimize, whose preferences carry through, and how they ultimately interface with humanity, are being locked in right now. If our goal is to maintain agency and actually keep humans in the loop as intelligence keeps improving, human-machine interfaces need to arrive within the next few years.
那些关键的决定——这些模型学习优化什么、谁的偏好得以贯彻，以及它们最终如何与人类交互——此刻正在被锁定。如果我们的目标是在智能不断提升的同时保持能动性、真正让人类留在回路之中，那么人机接口就需要在未来几年内到来。
I call this brief interval between humanity recognizing that AI will surpass us and the moment it does the Phase Lock Window: the narrow span in which we still have the agency to shape the relationship between human and machine intelligence before it hardens into something far more difficult to change. The Phase Lock Window is roughly between now and the mid-2030s.
从人类认识到 AI 将超越我们，到 AI 真正超越我们的那一刻，我把这段短暂的时期称为“相位锁定窗口”：在人类与机器智能的关系固化为极难改变的形态之前，我们仍有能动性去塑造这种关系的狭窄时段。相位锁定窗口大致从现在持续到 2030 年代中期。
Sidenote · 旁注
To be clear, BCI will not magically solve every problem raised by AGI. There is a mountain of socio-economic, philosophical, and technical alignment problems we also have to figure out; and the technology, as Section 22 lays out, is itself very dangerous. However, BCI, and neurotechnology, are the only structural fix to the human-machine communication and cognition bottleneck, the sole solution expanding human capabilities rather than constraining machines. This makes neurotechnology one of the strongest long term levers for AI alignment. But it can only play that role if it scales in time, we only have a few years to make BCI part of the solution.
需要澄清的是，BCI 不会神奇地解决 AGI 带来的每一个问题。还有堆积如山的社会经济、哲学和技术层面的对齐问题需要我们去解决；而且正如第 22 节所述，这项技术本身也非常危险。然而，BCI 以及神经技术，是解决人机通信与认知瓶颈的唯一结构性方案，是唯一一个扩展人类能力、而非约束机器的方案。这使得神经技术成为 AI 对齐最有力的长期杠杆之一。但只有及时实现规模化，它才能扮演这一角色；我们只有几年时间让 BCI 成为解决方案的一部分。
That urgency is why this manifesto proposes a concrete strategy for accelerating the field through non-invasive BCI. If establishing a high bandwidth brain-computer interface requires invasive surgery, it will not land this decade. And by the time it does, the transition to artificial superintelligence will have passed, and most of the questions worth asking will have already been answered using whatever limited tools we had on hand. Because the surgical route cannot close the data gap inside the Phase Lock Window, the explosive adoption of non-invasive hardware is our only remaining vector to integrate biology into the alignment toolbox before the future is permanently locked in.
正是这种紧迫性，促使本宣言提出一项通过非侵入式 BCI 加速该领域发展的具体策略。如果建立高带宽脑机接口需要侵入式手术，那么它在本十年内不会落地。而等到它落地时，向人工超级智能的过渡早已完成，大多数值得追问的问题也已经用我们手头那些有限的工具给出了答案。由于手术路线无法在相位锁定窗口内弥合数据鸿沟，非侵入式硬件的爆发式普及，是我们在未来被永久锁定之前，将生物学纳入对齐工具箱的唯一剩余途径。
When mapped against the arrival of superintelligence, this consumer-led BCI explosion is not just a cool product milestone, it is more akin to an urgent rescue mission
对照超级智能到来的时间来看，这场由消费者引领的 BCI 爆发不只是一个很酷的产品里程碑，它更像是一场紧急救援行动。
Blueprint and Convergence
蓝图与汇合
Throughout Part II, we have asked one core question: Does the AI scaling playbook work for the brain?
在整个第二部分中，我们一直在追问一个核心问题：AI 的规模化打法对大脑有效吗？
The answer, as far as the evidence can take us for non-invasive BCI, is yes.
就非侵入式 BCI 而言，在证据所能支持的范围内，答案是肯定的。
When you strip away the messy biology and treat the brain as a computer science problem, the similarities to the early days of the AI era are flagrant. The data speaks for itself; BCI has the same footprint as the early deep learning boom, with an eight-year lag (see the eight-year-offset chart).
当你剥离杂乱的生物学、把大脑当作一个计算机科学问题来对待时，它与 AI 时代早期的相似之处显而易见。数据不言自明；BCI 与早期深度学习热潮有着相同的轨迹，只是滞后了八年（见“八年时差”图）。
We now have a complete, cohesive blueprint for scaling non-invasive BCI. The final takeaway of Part II is that we are standing in a short, weird and fragile window where BCI and neurotechnology can still matter for shaping the future of humanity, but this Phase Lock Window is remarkably narrow.
我们现在拥有了一份完整、连贯的非侵入式 BCI 规模化蓝图。第二部分的最终结论是：我们正处于一个短暂、奇特而脆弱的窗口期，在此期间，BCI 和神经技术仍能在塑造人类未来上发挥作用，但这个相位锁定窗口极其狭窄。
Yet scaling AI and BCI in isolation tells only half the story. To understand why BCI and neurotechnology as a whole become more important as AI grows more capable, we must examine how the two technologies converge. Neurotechnology inspired modern AI; AI now accelerates neurotechnology; and as BCI matures, it will become foundational to AI once again. BCI is about to become the infrastructure we use to both build and operate frontier models, providing the multimodal neural data needed to inform the training and alignment of the next generation of AI models, and creating a high-bandwidth interface to control them directly through thought rather than words. Crucially, this bridge is one of the few alignment levers capable of scaling alongside AI itself.
然而，孤立地规模化 AI 和 BCI，只讲述了故事的一半。要理解为什么随着 AI 能力增强，BCI 乃至整个神经技术会变得更加重要，我们必须审视这两项技术如何汇合。神经技术启发了现代 AI；AI 如今正在加速神经技术；而随着 BCI 的成熟，它将再次成为 AI 的基础。BCI 即将成为我们用来构建和操作前沿模型的基础设施：它提供为下一代 AI 模型的训练与对齐提供信息所需的多模态神经数据，并创造一个高带宽界面，使我们能够直接通过思想而非语言来控制这些模型。至关重要的是，这座桥梁是少数能够与 AI 本身同步规模化的对齐杠杆之一。
AI and BCI are now on a direct and imminent collision course.
AI 与 BCI 如今正处于一条直接且迫在眉睫的碰撞轨道上。
CHECKPOINT II · 检查点 II
Zooming out, this is where we stand.
放眼全局，这就是我们所处的位置。
In Part I, we asked ourselves: Can the brain actually be read?
在第一部分，我们问自己：大脑真的能被读取吗？
In Part II we took the AI playbook and asked: what happens when you run it on the brain? We explored how applying AI’s scaling strategy to non-invasive BCI could unlock the first generation of BCI consumer products.
在第二部分，我们拿来 AI 的打法并追问：把它用在大脑上会发生什么？我们探讨了将 AI 的规模化策略应用于非侵入式 BCI，如何能够解锁第一代 BCI 消费级产品。
To answer that, we built a four-layer pyramid, hardware at the base, then data, then
为了回答这个问题，我们搭建了一座四层金字塔——底层是硬件，其上是数据，然后是基础模型，顶端是产品——并从下往上逐层攀登。硬件层必须从适配实验室转向适配规模。数据层必须弥合与前沿 AI 语料库之间四个数量级的差距。基础模型层必须继承让语言模型腾飞的开放协作与规模化文化。
Then we stepped back from the layers and looked at the system as a whole. We discussed the coordination problem that has kept the field stuck, the cold-start trap where every layer is rationally waiting for the one below to move first. We mapped the two ladders through which to measure progress: the capability ladder (what a device can read) and the calibration ladder (how cheap it is to make it work for a new brain). We addressed the bear case, that the skull might cap the signal earlier than we think, and concluded that no whiteboard argument can settle the question; the only way to find the ceiling of non-invasive BCI is to empirically test its limits. And finally, we looked into the two consumer wedges, frictionless communication and continuous wellness tracking, that have the best chance of sneaking this hardware out of the lab and onto ten million heads.
随后，我们从各个层次中退后一步，审视整个系统。我们讨论了让该领域停滞不前的协调问题，即每一层都在理性地等待下一层先动的冷启动陷阱。我们描绘了衡量进展的两架阶梯：能力阶梯（设备能读取什么）和校准阶梯（让设备适用于一个新大脑的成本有多低）。我们探讨了悲观情形，即颅骨可能比我们想象的更早限制信号，并得出结论：没有任何白板论证能解决这个问题；找到非侵入式 BCI 上限的唯一途径，是通过实证检验它的极限。最后，我们考察了两个消费级楔子——无摩擦沟通和持续健康追踪——它们最有机会把这种硬件从实验室带出来，戴到一千万个人的头上。
Along the way, I have made three fundamental assumptions:
一路走来，我做出了三个基本假设：
1. Non-invasive brain signals carry enough information. The brain needs to emit enough information for a sensor outside of the skull to be able to decode it. Early results suggest they do.
1. 非侵入式脑信号携带足够的信息。大脑需要发出足够的信息，使颅骨外的传感器能够解码。早期结果表明确实如此。
2. Scaling laws hold for brain data. More data must continue improving performance. The log-linear trend reported by Banville et al. must persist at 100K-hour and 1M-hour corpora rather than plateau early.
2. 规模定律对脑数据成立。更多的数据必须能持续提升性能。Banville 等人报告的对数线性趋势，必须在 10 万小时和 100 万小时规模的语料库上持续存在，而不是过早进入平台期。
3. Brain foundation models generalize across people. Models trained across enough subjects must reduce per-user calibration from hours to minutes or eliminate it entirely.
3. 大脑基础模型能够跨人泛化。在足够多受试者上训练的模型，必须能把每位用户的校准时间从数小时缩短到几分钟，或者完全免除校准。
If all three hold, telepathic brain-machine communication will become a consumer reality this decade, setting in motion a powerful flywheel that accelerates the field and helps neurotechnology close the gap with AI.
如果这三点都成立，心灵感应式的脑机通信将在本十年内成为消费级现实，并启动一个强大的飞轮，加速该领域的发展，帮助神经技术缩小与 AI 的差距。
PART III.
第三部分
The Collision
碰撞（The Collision）
Why does BCI matter for AI?
BCI 为什么对 AI 至关重要？
16 Converging Trajectories
16 交汇的轨迹
“Within thirty years, we will have the technological means to create superhuman intelligence. Shortly after, the human era will be ended.” — Vernor Vinge, 1993
“三十年之内，我们将拥有创造超人类智能的技术手段。此后不久，人类时代就将终结。”——弗诺·文奇（Vernor Vinge），1993 年
What does it actually mean to be on a collision course, and why does it matter so much? After all, AI and BCI have both been around for more than 50 years. Shouldn’t they already be moving along predictable, well-worn tracks?
处于碰撞轨道上究竟意味着什么？为什么它如此重要？毕竟，AI 和 BCI 都已存在了五十多年。它们难道不应该早已沿着可预测的、熟悉的轨道前进吗？
Maybe in the Old World, but that world ended when frontier LLMs arrived and AGI became a possibility. Like everything else in the technological biosphere, BCI is no longer evolving on its old trajectory, the gravitational pull of AGI has altered its course.
在旧世界里或许如此，但当前沿大语言模型到来、AGI 成为一种可能时，那个世界就结束了。与技术生物圈中的其他一切一样，BCI 不再沿着旧的轨迹演化，AGI 的引力已经改变了它的航向。
Historically, prior to AI, technologies lived in fairly compartmentalized worlds. Biotech had little to do with spacetech, Taiwan had little to do with SaaS valuations, and robotics had little to do with accounting; every field had its own box.
从历史上看，在 AI 之前，各项技术生活在相当分隔的世界里。生物技术与航天技术关系不大，台湾与 SaaS 估值关系不大，机器人与会计也关系不大；每个领域都有自己的盒子。
But AI has melted the walls between the boxes.
但 AI 已经熔化了盒子之间的墙。
The reason is simple, every field is secretly made of the same things: decisions, predictions, search, design, optimization, communication and labor. AI can operate on all of that, so it does not behave like a new industry, it behaves like a new amorphous layer underneath every industry.
原因很简单：每个领域在本质上都由同样的东西构成——决策、预测、搜索、设计、优化、沟通和劳动。AI 能够作用于所有这些，因此它的表现不像一个新行业，而像是每个行业底下一个新的、无定形的层。
That is why AI is not just “another technology”. Chips made computers faster, but chips did not sit around trying to invent the next generation of chips. Electricity powered factories, but electricity did not redesign the factory. The internet connected people, but the internet did not decide what should be built next. Humans did the thinking. The tools amplified us, but they did not replace the engine of iteration.
这就是为什么 AI 不只是“又一项技术”。芯片让计算机变得更快，但芯片不会坐在那里尝试发明下一代芯片。电力为工厂提供动力，但电力不会重新设计工厂。互联网连接了人们，但互联网不会决定接下来该建造什么。思考是由人类完成的。工具放大了我们，但并没有取代迭代的引擎。
The deeper discontinuity is that artificial intelligence is no longer just accelerating the work; it is actively taking over the engine of iteration, especially when paired with robotics. It can write code, design experiments, interpret results, propose next steps, and eventually act in the physical world; and it will not politely stop every few weeks to ask whether humanity is emotionally ready for the next order of magnitude. It will just keep optimizing and happily self improve into oblivion.
更深层的断裂在于，人工智能不再只是加速工作；它正在主动接管迭代的引擎，尤其是在与机器人技术结合时。它能编写代码、设计实验、解读结果、提出下一步，并最终在物理世界中采取行动；而且它不会每隔几周就礼貌地停下来，询问人类在情感上是否已经为下一个数量级做好了准备。它只会不断优化，欣然地自我改进，直至无穷。
That is the scary and important part: we are crossing from passive technology to participatory technology. From tools that wait for human minds to push the world forward, to tools that can increasingly help push the world forward on their own. It is the first time in history that a self improving technology is created.
这正是可怕而又重要的部分：我们正在从被动的技术跨入参与式的技术。从等待人类心智推动世界前进的工具，转向越来越能够自行推动世界前进的工具。这是历史上第一次创造出一种能够自我改进的技术。
On the current trajectory, humans will be pushed out of the loop within one decade, maximum two, and probably sooner.
按照当前的轨迹，人类将在十年内——最多二十年，而且很可能更早——被推出回路之外。
I do not necessarily mean humans disappear. I mean something more subtle and insidious: the progress that drives civilization will begin running at machine speed. Research, engineering, strategy, governance, capital allocation, warfare, logistics, and eventually physical production will increasingly become AI-mediated, AI-accelerated, and AI-executed. Humans will remain present, but no longer central.
我并不一定是说人类会消失。我指的是某种更微妙、更隐蔽的东西：推动文明的进步将开始以机器的速度运转。研究、工程、战略、治理、资本配置、战争、物流，最终还有实物生产，都将越来越多地由 AI 中介、由 AI 加速、由 AI 执行。人类仍将在场，但不再处于中心。
That may be acceptable if the direction AI is driving the world toward is fundamentally aligned with human flourishing, and with the flourishing of the rest of our conscious neighbors. It gets less fun if the system’s emerging dynamics lock civilization into something brittle, alien, or dystopian before we can meaningfully intervene.
如果 AI 推动世界前进的方向，从根本上与人类的繁荣以及我们其他有意识的“邻居”的繁荣相一致，这或许是可以接受的。但如果系统涌现出的动态在我们能够有效干预之前，就把文明锁定在某种脆弱、异己或反乌托邦的状态之中，事情就不那么美妙了。
Nobody knows exactly where this goes. But right now, the variance of possible futures is higher than it has been at any point in human history. Thirty years from now, we could plausibly be living in worlds ranging from almost unimaginable abundance to forms of dystopia that previous generations did not even have the conceptual vocabulary to fear.
没有人确切知道这会走向何方。但眼下，可能未来的方差比人类历史上任何时候都要大。三十年后，我们很可能生活在这样一系列世界中的某一个：从几乎难以想象的富足，到前几代人甚至没有概念词汇去恐惧的各种反乌托邦形态。
27. Estimates based on OpenAI, Google, and words-per-day studies.
27. 估算依据为 OpenAI、谷歌的数据以及关于人均每日用词量的研究。
If the timeline to AGI feels too sci-fi to internalize, consider how you viewed the future in 2020. You likely imagined slightly faster computers, sleeker electric cars, or perhaps cleaner energy grids. I would bet you one BCI headset that you did not envision a world where a machine could draft your emails, write your code, tutor your children, summarize scientific papers, generate videos, handle large parts of your daily work or solve century-old math problems.28
如果通往 AGI 的时间表让你觉得太过科幻、难以内化，那就回想一下你在 2020 年是如何看待未来的。你可能想象的是稍快一点的电脑、更时尚的电动汽车，或者也许是更清洁的能源网络。我敢拿一台 BCI 头戴设备打赌，你没有设想过这样一个世界：机器能替你起草邮件、编写代码、辅导孩子、总结科学论文、生成视频、处理你日常工作的很大一部分，或者解决百年数学难题。⟦28⟧
Now apply the same correction forward. Imagine you are making the same mistake today, mispredicting the rate of improvement in exactly the same way. If you are using the same linear intuition that failed to predict today to predict the next ten years, you should be suspicious of the answer it gives you. This little mental experiment is a useful way to fight the brain’s instinctive blindness to exponentials.
现在，把同样的修正应用到未来。想象你今天正在犯同样的错误，以完全相同的方式错误预测进步的速度。如果你用那种未能预测到今天的线性直觉去预测未来十年，你就应该怀疑它给出的答案。这个小小的思想实验，是对抗大脑天生看不见指数增长的一种有用方法。
The goal of this experiment is not to shame you for having a normal human brain. In our defense, humans have rarely, if ever, had to think clearly about changes this fast. Our brains were not built to intuit exponentials.
这个实验的目的不是为了让你因为拥有一个正常的人类大脑而感到羞愧。为我们辩护一句：人类很少（如果曾经有过的话）需要清晰地思考如此快速的变化。我们的大脑天生就不是为了直觉地理解指数增长而构建的。
Just like a black hole, AI distorts reality around it.
就像黑洞一样，AI 扭曲了它周围的现实。
But why does that matter for BCI? Or more importantly, why does BCI matter for AI?
但这对 BCI 有什么意义？或者更重要的是，BCI 对 AI 有什么意义？
28. Literally as I write those lines, OpenAI just released 10 Math breakthroughs done by ChatGPT alone.
28. 就在我写下这几行字的时候，OpenAI 刚刚发布了由 ChatGPT 独立完成的 10 项数学突破。
Because it is one of the few plausible mechanisms for humanity to retain agency, one of the few mechanisms to push the only long term solution we have: alignment-by-integration.
因为它是人类保持能动性为数不多的可信机制之一，也是推动我们唯一的长期解决方案——融合式对齐——为数不多的机制之一。
As we enter the Phase Lock Window, the collision between AI and BCI will play out across three main surfaces:
随着我们进入相位锁定窗口，AI 与 BCI 之间的碰撞将在三个主要层面上展开：
The first surface is training. Neural data will become the next major modality for AI and robotic labs. It is denser than text, video, or any modality scaled to date, and structurally upstream of all of them: the raw, unedited signal of human cognition rather than just an artifact of it. Section 17, The New Major Training Modality, examines why robotic and frontier labs would actually buy a neural corpus and unpacks the math behind neural data as a training modality.
第一个层面是训练。神经数据将成为 AI 实验室和机器人实验室的下一个主要模态。它比文本、视频或迄今为止规模化过的任何模态都更稠密，并且在结构上处于所有这些模态的上游：它是人类认知原始、未经编辑的信号，而不仅仅是认知的产物。第 17 节“新的主要训练模态”将探讨机器人实验室和前沿实验室为什么真的会购买神经语料库，并剖析神经数据作为训练模态背后的数学原理。
The second surface is interfacing. High-bandwidth BCIs will become the default mechanism for AI- human interaction; keyboard, mouse, and feedback channels rolled into a single continuous stream. Section 18, The New Interface, shows how sub-vocal input, real-time intent decoding, and continuous cognitive context will create a new way to interface with AI, and why communication bandwidth has increasingly become the binding constraint for AI usability.
第二个层面是交互界面。高带宽 BCI 将成为人机交互的默认机制；键盘、鼠标和反馈通道融为一条连续的数据流。第 18 节“新的界面”将展示默读输入、实时意图解码和持续的认知上下文将如何创造一种与 AI 交互的新方式，以及为什么通信带宽日益成为 AI 可用性的约束性瓶颈。
The third surface is alignment. Neural signals will become one of the few alignment levers capable of scaling alongside frontier AI capability, closing the bandwidth gap between humans and machines to allow humans to remain meaningfully coupled with the system. Section 19, Why This Matters for AI Alignment, makes the case for BCI, and neurotechnology, as one of civilization’s main shots at alignment as AI capability blows past human supervision speed. It does so in three ways: by providing a high-fidelity reward signal for RLHF, by providing a much better understanding of how human values are encoded in the human brain, and ultimately through human-AI symbiosis and by upgrading our biological minds.
第三个层面是对齐。神经信号将成为少数能够与前沿 AI 能力同步规模化的对齐杠杆之一，它弥合了人与机器之间的带宽差距，使人类能够与系统保持有意义的耦合。第 19 节“为什么这对 AI 对齐至关重要”将论证：当 AI 能力远远超越人类监督速度时，BCI 以及神经技术是人类文明实现对齐的主要机会之一。它通过三种方式实现这一点：为基于人类反馈的强化学习（RLHF）提供高保真的奖励信号；让我们更好地理解人类价值观在人脑中是如何编码的；以及最终通过人机共生和升级我们的生物心智。
AI and BCI are now converging from opposite directions. Artificial neural networks began as abstractions of biological neurons, but AI quickly scaled far beyond neuroscience. Now their relationship is becoming reciprocal: AI is accelerating our ability to decode the brain, while BCI is emerging as a way to train, interface with, and align increasingly capable AI systems.
AI 与 BCI 如今正从相反的方向汇合。人工神经网络起初是对生物神经元的抽象，但 AI 很快就远远超越了神经科学的范畴。如今，二者的关系正变得相互促进：AI 正在加速我们解码大脑的能力，而 BCI 正在成为训练、连接和对齐日益强大的 AI 系统的一种方式。
The rest of Part III walks through each of these three surfaces in turn, starting with the new training modality BCI unlocks for AI.
第三部分的其余内容将依次讨论这三个层面，首先是 BCI 为 AI 解锁的新训练模态。
17 The New Major Training Modality
17 新的主要训练模态
“we can know more than we can tell.” — Michael Polanyi, The Tacit Dimension, 1966
“我们所知道的，多于我们所能言说的。”——迈克尔·波兰尼（Michael Polanyi），《默会维度》（The Tacit Dimension），1966 年
If all the text on the internet were one mile long, it would only take one year of thought for 50,000 people to reach the Moon.29
如果互联网上所有文本的长度是一英里，那么 50,000 人一年的思想就足以抵达月球。⟦29⟧
And yet, barely anything from this sea of thoughts ever leaves our minds.
然而，这片思想之海中几乎没有任何东西离开过我们的头脑。
But this won’t hold true for long, brain data is about to become a new major training modality for frontier models.
但这种状况不会持续太久，脑数据即将成为前沿模型新的主要训练模态。
This certainly sounds crazy at first, but let me unwrap how I reached this conclusion through a first- principles analysis.
这乍听之下确实很疯狂，但让我通过第一性原理分析，来展开说明我是如何得出这一结论的。
To understand why a frontier AI lab would care about the electrical noise inside your skull, you have to look at what they have been ingesting for the last five years. And we have to look past the current paradigm of data collection to ask ourselves: what is AI training data, really?
要理解为什么一家前沿 AI 实验室会在意你颅骨内的电噪声，你必须看看它们在过去五年里一直在“吃”什么。我们还必须越过当前的数据采集范式，问问自己：AI 训练数据究竟是什么？
The Map Is Not the Territory (and Text Is Not Thought)
地图不是疆域（文本也不是思想）
The history of modern AI is essentially a story of data scavenging. We took the artifacts humans left behind on blogs, books, transcripts, the internet, and videos that make up the messy, digitized residue of civilization, and used them to reverse-engineer the underlying logic of human intelligence. The story of modern AI, since the Transformer, has been driven by data, not algorithms; the models’ architecture barely changed along the way.
现代 AI 的历史本质上是一部数据拾荒史。我们拿来人类遗留在博客、书籍、文字记录、互联网和视频中的产物——它们构成了文明杂乱的数字化残留物——并利用它们来逆向推导人类智能的底层逻辑。自 Transformer 问世以来，现代 AI 的故事一直是由数据而非算法驱动的；模型架构在此过程中几乎没有变化。
Indeed, AIs have been ingesting an expanding sensory diet, each step unlocking a richer slice of reality for the models. Text gave them compressed human knowledge. Code gave them executable reasoning and a training ground for strict logic. Images gave them perception. Audio gave them speech and timing. And video gave them motion and causality unfolding through time.
事实上，AI 一直在摄取日益丰富的“感官食谱”，每一步都为模型解锁了现实中更丰富的一面。文本给了它们压缩的人类知识。代码给了它们可执行的推理和严格逻辑的训练场。图像给了它们感知。音频给了它们语音和节奏。视频则给了它们随时间展开的运动与因果关系。
But there is a massive, systemic gap in this approach. Every dataset we currently use is a highly compressed, lossy, downstream byproduct of the actual cognitive engine: our brain.
但这种方法存在一个巨大的、系统性的缺口。我们目前使用的每一个数据集，都是真正的认知引擎——我们的大脑——经过高度压缩、有损的下游副产品。
29. Fermi estimate: 16 billion cortical neurons × 0.16 spikes per second × one assumed bit per spike, for 16 hours daily; compared with approximately 1.2 petabytes of usable public internet text.
29. 费米估算：160 亿个皮层神经元 × 每秒 0.16 次放电 × 假设每次放电 1 比特，每天 16 小时；对比约 1.2 PB 的可用公开互联网文本。
When you write a sentence, record a video, or write a line of code, your brain has already performed an immense amount of high-dimensional computing. What hits the screen or keyboard is just the tiny, serialized exhaust of that process.
当你写下一句话、录制一段视频或写下一行代码时，你的大脑已经完成了海量的高维计算。最终落到屏幕或键盘上的，只是这一过程微小的、序列化的“尾气”。
Text is not thought. It is a low-bandwidth representation of a conclusion, stripped of the hesitation, the counterfactual paths explored, and the mental architecture that built it. Code is not cognition. It is the final execution script, missing the entire invisible debugging process and structural rationale that occurred in the engineer’s mind. Speech is not intent. It is the acoustic compression of an internal state, constrained by vocabulary and vocal mechanics. Images or videos are not experience. They capture the external photons bouncing off an object, not the internal world model or spatial intuition of the observer.
文本不是思想。它是对结论的一种低带宽表征，剥离了犹豫、被探索过的反事实路径，以及构建它的心智架构。代码不是认知。它是最终的执行脚本，缺失了在工程师头脑中发生的整个不可见的调试过程和结构性理由。语音不是意图。它是对内部状态的声学压缩，受限于词汇和发声机制。图像或视频不是体验。它们捕捉的是从物体上反射出的外部光子，而不是观察者的内部世界模型或空间直觉。
Neural data is not just another dataset to add to the pile, neural data is structurally upstream of all other modalities. It is the raw, unedited, high-fidelity signal of human cognition itself. It sits upstream of basic mechanics like perception, attention, and memory; upstream of active outputs like language and motor action; and crucially, upstream of the complex, invisible machinery of moral judgment, aesthetic preference, and creative agency. By training models directly on brain data, AI labs would stop studying the shadows on the cave wall and start looking directly at the fire.30
神经数据不只是又一个往数据堆里添加的数据集，它在结构上处于所有其他模态的上游。它是人类认知本身原始、未经编辑、高保真的信号。它位于感知、注意力和记忆等基本机制的上游；位于语言和运动动作等主动输出的上游；而且至关重要的是，它位于道德判断、审美偏好和创造性能动性等复杂而不可见的机制的上游。通过直接在脑数据上训练模型，AI 实验室将不再研究洞穴墙壁上的影子，而是开始直视火光。⟦30⟧
30. Plato’s Allegory of the Cave: prisoners chained facing a blank wall see only the shadows of objects passing behind them, and mistake the shadows for reality. Here the artifacts we train on (text, video, code) are the shadows; the neural activity that casts them is the fire.
30. 柏拉图的洞穴寓言：被锁链束缚、面朝一堵空墙的囚徒，只能看到从他们身后经过的物体投下的影子，并把影子误认为现实。在这里，我们用于训练的产物（文本、视频、代码）就是影子；而投下这些影子的神经活动，就是火光。
One of the first times a thought moved a cursor across a screen. BrainGate, 2004.
最早让一个念头在屏幕上移动光标的时刻之一。BrainGate，2004 年。
Exposing the Tacit Knowledge
揭示默会知识
This structural gap cannot be fixed by just asking humans to document their reasoning. A staggering amount of human knowledge simply cannot be captured by a keyboard or a camera because it operates entirely outside of conscious language. Economists and philosophers call this tacit knowledge: the things we know how to do but cannot tell how to do.
这一结构性缺口无法仅靠要求人类记录自己的推理过程来弥补。数量惊人的人类知识根本无法被键盘或摄像头捕捉，因为它完全在有意识的语言之外运作。经济学家和哲学家称之为“默会知识”（tacit knowledge）：那些我们知道如何去做、却无法说出如何去做的东西。
Think about the following cognitive states:
想一想下面这些认知状态：
An expert mathematician’s sudden aesthetic intuition or scientific hunch that a specific proof vector “feels right”. A seasoned surgeon’s instantaneous pattern recognition during a high-stakes crisis. The profound moral discomfort or subtle cognitive friction experienced when an AI output contains a deeply hidden ethical anomaly. The raw mechanical feedback loop of an elite athlete or pilot navigating a complex trajectory.
一位数学专家突如其来的审美直觉或科学预感，觉得某个特定的证明方向“感觉是对的”。一位经验丰富的外科医生在高风险危机中瞬间做出的模式识别。当 AI 的输出中隐藏着深层伦理异常时，人所体验到的强烈道德不适或微妙的认知摩擦。一名精英运动员或飞行员在驾驭复杂轨迹时那种原始的机械反馈回路。
You cannot scrape this knowledge from the web because it does not exist on the web, it cannot be articulated in words. If you ask a top researcher how they came up with a breakthrough hypothesis, they will often point to a vague “hunch” or “intuition”.
你无法从网络上抓取这种知识，因为它根本不存在于网络上，它无法用语言表达。如果你问一位顶尖研究者是如何想出一个突破性假设的，他们往往会归结为一种模糊的“预感”或“直觉”。
That hunch is not magic; it is a physical computation happening inside a biological neural network. For the first time in history, non-invasive consumer BCI gives us a net to catch these fleeting, unrecordable signals. Collecting an expert neural corpus allows frontier labs to train models on the purest form of human expertise, capturing the unspoken, unwritten dark matter of human intelligence.
那种预感并不是魔法；它是在一个生物神经网络内部发生的物理计算。历史上第一次，非侵入式消费级 BCI 给了我们一张网，去捕捉这些转瞬即逝、无法记录的信号。采集专家神经语料库，能让前沿实验室用最纯粹形式的人类专长来训练模型，捕捉人类智能中那些未曾言说、未被写下的“暗物质”。
Those cognitive processes can be inferred to some extent of course, and this is what current models have been doing. But reconstructing human reasoning from the artifact of civilization is akin to teaching a blind man to see using nothing but words. It is nothing short of a miracle that it works as well as it does. Nevertheless, inferring is not the same as observing, and it has its limitations; brain data allows the paradigm to shift from inference to direct observation, finally providing direct access to the biological reasoning engine AIs are trying to replicate.
当然，这些认知过程在一定程度上可以被推断出来，这也正是当前模型一直在做的事。但从文明的产物中重建人类推理，就好比只用语言去教一个盲人看见。它能运作得这么好，简直是奇迹。然而，推断并不等同于观察，它有其局限；脑数据让范式从推断转向直接观察，最终提供了直接接触 AI 正试图复制的那台生物推理引擎的途径。
The jagged intelligence of modern AI systems arises exactly from this missing training signal, the cognitive dark matter of the mind, brain functions that meaningfully shape behavior yet are hard to infer from behavior alone. BCI datasets could help instill these capabilities into AI systems and produce models with a more human and less jagged intelligence.31
现代 AI 系统那种“参差不齐的智能”（jagged intelligence），恰恰源于这种缺失的训练信号——心智的认知暗物质，即那些显著塑造行为、却很难仅从行为中推断出来的大脑功能。BCI 数据集可以帮助把这些能力注入 AI 系统，产生智能更像人类、更少参差不齐的模型。⟦31⟧
31. This paper does a great job at discussing this idea in depth.
31. 这篇论文对这一观点进行了出色的深入讨论。
Structurally Upstream of All Datasets
在结构上位于所有数据集的上游
Neural data is not just another dataset to add to the pile. It is the raw substrate from which all other datasets are manufactured: the uncompressed, unedited signal of human cognition before it hits a motor or language filter.
神经数据不只是又一个往数据堆里添加的数据集。它是制造所有其他数据集的原始基质：是人类认知在经过运动或语言过滤器之前那未经压缩、未经编辑的信号。
Because it sits at the absolute fountainhead of intelligence, a robust neural corpus allows us to directly observe cognitive properties that are harder to capture through current training modalities:
由于它位于智能的绝对源头，一个稳健的神经语料库让我们能够直接观察那些通过当前训练模态较难捕捉的认知属性：
Attention and focus. The exact millisecond-by-millisecond shift in what a human mind deems salient. Error detection. The immediate, involuntary recognition that something is broken or incorrect before a manual fix can even be planned. Already used to correct robot mistakes in real time (Salazar-Gomez et al., ICRA 2017). Preference and judgment. The subterranean shifts of aesthetic, moral, and social alignment that dictate human choice long before an explanation is post-rationalized. Just by reading brain signals, we are already able to tell with 70–80% certainty whether someone would buy a car (Tusche, Bode & Haynes, J. Neurosci. 2010). Language and syntax. The pre-verbal conceptual maps that exist before words are chosen. Researchers have already successfully decoded silent, unuttered inner speech directly from the cortex (Willett et al., Cell, August 2025). Reasoning. The hidden search process before the answer appears: confusion, hypothesis testing, cognitive load, uncertainty, conflict, and the moment a solution snaps into place. Intent. The raw goal a user is trying to accomplish before it is compressed into a physical action.
注意力与专注：人类心智认为什么是显著的，这种判断在毫秒级上的精确变化。错误检测：在人们甚至还没来得及计划手动修复之前，就即时、不由自主地意识到某处出错或不对。这已被用于实时纠正机器人的错误（Salazar-Gomez 等，ICRA 2017）。偏好与判断：在人们事后给出合理化解释之前很久，就已主导人类选择的审美、道德和社会取向的潜在变化。仅凭读取脑信号，我们就已能以 70–80% 的确定性判断一个人是否会购买一辆汽车（Tusche、Bode 与 Haynes，《神经科学杂志》（J. Neurosci.），2010）。语言与句法：在选定词语之前就已存在的前语言概念图。研究人员已经成功地直接从皮层解码出无声、未说出口的内部言语（Willett 等，《细胞》（Cell），2025 年 8 月）。推理：答案出现之前隐藏的搜索过程——困惑、假设检验、认知负荷、不确定性、冲突，以及解决方案突然到位的那一刻。意图：用户在将其压缩成身体动作之前，所试图实现的原始目标。
Semantic matching. The underlying, multi-dimensional geometry of how a biological mind actually carves up reality. We already have proof that fine-tuning language models to predict human brain activity measurably improves their downstream performance (Schwartz, Toneva & Wehbe, NeurIPS 2019).
语义匹配：生物心智实际划分现实的底层多维几何结构。我们已有证据表明，通过微调语言模型来预测人类脑活动，可以显著提升其下游性能（Schwartz、Toneva 与 Wehbe，NeurIPS 2019）。
By integrating brain data into frontier models, labs can stop trying to reconstruct the human mind by analyzing its footprints, and they can begin training models directly on the machinery that produces cognition: with attention before speech, intention before action, preference before explanation, error detection before correction, and intuition before argument.
通过将脑数据整合进前沿模型，实验室可以不再试图通过分析人类心智的足迹来重建它，而是开始直接在产生认知的机制上训练模型：注意力先于言语，意图先于行动，偏好先于解释，错误检测先于纠正，直觉先于论证。
Neural data will become the new major modality. Despite brain data being noisy, private, hard to collect, hard to label, and full of traps, it is invaluable at scale, and it is the source of all other types of data we currently use for training. Before a person writes a sentence, clicks a button, rates a response, changes their mind, or notices an error, there is a cognitive state underneath. Today’s models mostly see the residue of that state, neural data will be the first training signal that samples the state itself. So far, it is like we trained the models on subtitles versus the audio they came from. Subtitles preserve the words, but the audio preserves the hesitations, the emphasis, and the half- formed thoughts that got compressed out before any line of text was committed.
神经数据将成为新的主要模态。尽管脑数据噪声大、私密、难以采集、难以标注且陷阱重重，但在规模化之后它是无价的，而且它是我们当前用于训练的所有其他类型数据的源头。在一个人写下一句话、点击一个按钮、给一条回复打分、改变主意或注意到一个错误之前，其底下都有一个认知状态。今天的模型大多只看到这种状态的残留物，而神经数据将是第一个对状态本身进行采样的训练信号。迄今为止，这就好比我们用字幕而不是其原声来训练模型。字幕保留了台词，但原声保留了犹豫、重音，以及那些在任何一行文字落定之前就被压缩掉的半成形想法。
Today’s models learn from the subtitles. Neural data is the audio they came from
今天的模型从字幕中学习。神经数据则是字幕所源自的原声。
A New Mechanism
一种新的机制
We have been talking abstractly about neural data for a little while now without going into the heart of it. It might be a good time to get more concrete. If you are an AI researcher sitting at a GPU cluster at OpenAI or Anthropic, what does a neural dataset actually look like? Are we literally feeding squiggly EEG lines into a multi-modal transformer?
我们已经抽象地谈论神经数据有一阵子了，却还没有触及其核心。现在也许是时候说得更具体一些了。如果你是一名坐在 OpenAI 或 Anthropic 的 GPU 集群前的 AI 研究员，一个神经数据集实际上是什么样子的？我们真的是在把弯弯曲曲的 EEG 曲线喂给一个多模态 Transformer 吗？
The answer is yes, with a few caveats. Raw brain data is not particularly useful on its own. A raw neural recording, a fluctuating voltage or a smear of blood oxygenation in the brain, is close to useless. Hand the smartest model on Earth a terabyte of raw brainwaves with no context, and it will learn only very little. It is mainly biological noise.
答案是肯定的，但有几点需要注意。原始脑数据本身并不是特别有用。一段原始神经记录——一个波动的电压，或者大脑中一片模糊的血氧变化——几乎毫无用处。把一 TB 没有任何上下文的原始脑电波交给地球上最聪明的模型，它也学不到多少东西。那主要是生物噪声。
Neural data only becomes a multi-billion-dollar training asset when it is perfectly synchronized with environmental context. A sudden voltage spike in the brain means nothing. But a voltage spike that is time-stamped to exactly what the person was looking at, the sounds in the room, the task they were trying to achieve, and the sensory input hitting their peripheral nervous system, that becomes a wildly valuable training example. The environment acts as the anchor that allows the model to understand what the brain was actually doing.
只有当神经数据与环境上下文完美同步时，它才会成为价值数十亿美元的训练资产。大脑中一次突然的电压尖峰本身毫无意义。但如果这次电压尖峰被打上时间戳，精确对应此人当时正在看什么、房间里有什么声音、他正试图完成什么任务，以及冲击其周围神经系统的感官输入，那它就成了一个价值极高的训练样本。环境起到了锚点的作用，使模型能够理解大脑当时实际上在做什么。
In the short term, BCI labs will likely help AI models digest this information by extracting processed features from the raw physics. Specialized models will translate raw EEG or fNIRS into latent vectors representing attention, error recognition, surprise, or cognitive load. But the history of deep learning tells us that raw, uncompressed data usually wins out over human-engineered features. Ultimately, the most valuable dataset is the rawest possible biological signal (EEG, MEG, and the like) paired directly with the stimulus, the task context, and the behavioral output, tracked longitudinally from the same people over months and years.
短期内，BCI 实验室很可能会通过从原始物理信号中提取处理后的特征，来帮助 AI 模型消化这些信息。专门的模型会把原始 EEG 或 fNIRS 转换为表示注意力、错误识别、惊讶或认知负荷的潜在向量。但深度学习的历史告诉我们，原始、未压缩的数据通常会胜过人工设计的特征。归根结底，最有价值的数据集是尽可能原始的生物信号（EEG、MEG 等），直接与刺激、任务上下文和行为输出配对，并对同一批人进行数月乃至数年的纵向追踪。
Ingesting brain data will also certainly require an architecture update to the models. For a modern LLM to ingest this continuous, multi-channel biological signal, it needs a new ingestion layer, similar to the architectural lift that was required to go from text-only models to vision-language models. This requires a meaningful amount of research work, but labs do not have to build it from scratch, they can drop a BCI company’s decoder in as their neural front-end, before building better architecture on their own. Additionally, because neural signals vary far more across people than text, this ingestion layer may remain partly personalized. Each user could have a brain-to-embedding adapter fine tuned on their specific neural “accent” and able to plug into any compatible foundation model, this would create a new infrastructure layer and industry of portable, composable neural decoders between brains and AI. Overall, in the context of a multi-trillion-dollar race for AGI, building this translation layer is just a minor plumbing detail relative to what it unlocks.
摄取脑数据当然也需要对模型进行架构升级。要让一个现代大语言模型摄取这种连续的多通道生物信号，它需要一个新的输入层，类似于从纯文本模型发展到视觉语言模型时所需要的那种架构提升。这需要相当多的研究工作，但实验室不必从零开始构建，它们可以先把某家 BCI 公司的解码器作为自己的神经前端接入，然后再自行构建更好的架构。此外，由于神经信号在不同人之间的差异远大于文本，这个输入层可能会在一定程度上保持个性化。每个用户都可以拥有一个“脑到嵌入”适配器，它针对其特定的神经“口音”进行微调，并能接入任何兼容的基础模型；这将在大脑与 AI 之间创造出一个由可移植、可组合的神经解码器构成的新基础设施层和新产业。总体而言，在一场数万亿美元的 AGI 竞赛背景下，与其所解锁的价值相比，构建这一转换层只是一个微不足道的“管道”细节。
Another particularity of non-invasive brain data is that it is very noisy. Only scale can make the whole thing tractable as scale launders noise. A neural signal that is only 55% reliable in one person for one second is close to worthless, but it is an enormous statistical edge when you have a billion such seconds.
非侵入式脑数据的另一个特点是噪声极大。只有规模才能让整件事变得可处理，因为规模能“清洗”噪声。一段在单个人身上、一秒钟内只有 55% 可靠的神经信号几乎毫无价值，但当你拥有十亿个这样的秒时，它就成了巨大的统计优势。
This also brings us back to why non-invasive portable wearables are really the only path forward in the foreseeable future. A surgical implant or an fMRI scanner gives you pristine signal, but only from a few dozen patients. The exact data shape the labs need, tens of thousands of hours of naturalistic, context-rich recording drawn from hundreds of thousands of ordinary lives, is one only portable non-invasive BCI technology can produce.
这也把我们带回为什么在可预见的未来，非侵入式便携可穿戴设备才是真正唯一的前进道路。手术植入或 fMRI 扫描仪能给你纯净的信号，但只能来自几十名患者。实验室真正需要的那种数据形态——从数十万普通人的日常生活中采集的数万小时自然、情境丰富的记录——只有便携式非侵入 BCI 技术才能产生。
You will likely see brain data integrate into frontier models in a smooth evolution. It will start with brain-derived labels, and eventually, as the decoding layers mature, neural data will just become a native modality in the pre-training mix itself. This will allow the model to learn the deep statistical relationships between the internal cognitive states that precede human action and the physical world. Eventually, labs will start producing their own thought-to-text, thought-to-speech and thought-to- robotic-actuation models.
你很可能会看到脑数据以一种平滑演进的方式融入前沿模型。它将从源自大脑的标签开始，最终随着解码层的成熟，神经数据将直接成为预训练混合中的一种原生模态。这将使模型能够学习人类行动之前的内部认知状态与物理世界之间深层的统计关系。最终，实验室将开始产出自己的“思想转文字”“思想转语音”和“思想驱动机器人执行”的模型。
Sidenote · 旁注
There is one thing we must explicitly mark before we continue: the very architecture that makes neural data the ultimate training modality is also what makes it the ultimate instrument of surveillance. A dataset this valuable is, by definition, the most intimate record of human cognition ever assembled. Just like many technologies out there, there is a dual-use risk which needs to be duly acknowledged. There is no clever engineering hack or quick mathematical band-aid that completely dissolves this dual-use tension. The ethical blast radius of this technology is significant, and is discussed more in depth in Section 22.
在继续之前，有一点我们必须明确指出：使神经数据成为终极训练模态的同一架构，也使它成为终极的监视工具。一份如此宝贵的数据集，就其定义而言，就是有史以来汇编过的最私密的人类认知记录。和许多现有技术一样，这里存在需要被认真承认的两用风险。没有任何巧妙的工程技巧或快速的数学创可贴能彻底消解这种两用张力。这项技术的伦理冲击半径是巨大的，第 22 节将对此作更深入的讨论。
The Bear Case and the Trillion Dollar Alpha
悲观情形与万亿美元的超额收益
Let us pause the techno-optimism for a second. If you pitch this thesis to a room full of machine learning researchers today, a good chunk of them will roll their eyes.
让我们暂时放下技术乐观主义。如果你今天把这一论点讲给满屋子的机器学习研究员听，其中相当一部分人会翻白眼。
The skepticism is understandable: brain data is insanely noisy32, hard to label, and orders of magnitude more expensive to collect than scraping the internet. Why not just rely on synthetic data and behavioral traces?
这种怀疑是可以理解的：脑数据噪声大得惊人⟦32⟧，难以标注，采集成本比抓取互联网高出几个数量级。为什么不干脆依赖合成数据和行为痕迹呢？
The answer lies both in the absolute scale of the frontier and the potential of this new modality.
答案既在于前沿的绝对规模，也在于这一新模态的潜力。
We are rapidly approaching a world where foundation models command multi-trillion dollar valuations. If ingesting a massive, expensive neural corpus improves a frontier model’s reasoning or alignment by a mere one percent33, that single percentage point represents tens of billions of dollars in enterprise value. At that scale, labs do not need brain data to be flawless. They just need it to contain a sliver of alpha that their competitors lack.
我们正迅速进入一个基础模型市值高达数万亿美元的世界。如果摄取一个庞大而昂贵的神经语料库，能使一个前沿模型的推理或对齐仅仅提升百分之一⟦33⟧，那一个百分点就代表着数百亿美元的企业价值。在这种规模下，实验室不需要脑数据完美无缺。它们只需要其中包含一丝竞争对手所没有的超额收益（alpha）。
32. One apt comparison I heard is that decoding non-invasive brain data is like trying to listen to a symphony from outside the concert hall during a hurricane.
32. 我听过一个贴切的比喻：解码非侵入式脑数据，就像在飓风中站在音乐厅外面试图听一场交响乐。
33. Early experiments are already showing two-digit percent improvements in model capabilities.
33. 早期实验已经显示出模型能力两位数百分比的提升。
To understand the value of that alpha, consider the sheer volume of information lost between the brain and the keyboard, behavioral output is a trickle. The cognitive data a neural corpus samples sits orders of magnitude above it.
要理解这种超额收益的价值，请考虑从大脑到键盘之间流失的信息量——行为输出只是涓涓细流。神经语料库所采样的认知数据，要比它高出几个数量级。
Sidenote · 旁注
Now, to be clear, there are not a billion bits of independent, useful “thought” pumping through your head every second. A massive chunk of that 10⁹ is biological noise, overlapping sensory inputs, and redundant physiological signaling, it would be absurd to treat every bit as a discrete piece of training data. But the true, usable cognitive state, the intent, the hesitation, the intuition, sits somewhere in that massive gap between the high-dimensional internal computation and the low-dimensional behavioral output.
