The narrative shifts from medical necessity to human performance and communication with machines. This has already started and will accelerate over the coming years.
生产力与控制：免手动计算、空间界面导航、无声输入，以及实时认知负荷管理。叙事从医疗必要性转向人类表现及与机器的沟通。这已经开始，并将在未来几年加速。
The AI Data Engine: Neural data will reclassify from a niche biological dataset into an AI training modality, making the brain a frontier AI asset. This will become viable within a few years.
AI 数据引擎：神经数据将从一种小众生物数据集重新归类为一种 AI 训练模态，使大脑成为前沿 AI 资产。这将在几年内变得可行。
Deep Augmentation: Closed-loop human-AI collaboration where BCI becomes the new fundamental communication layer with AI. By early next decade, this will be a reality.
深度增强：闭环人机协作，BCI 成为与 AI 沟通的新的基础层。到下个十年初期，这将成为现实。
Follow the Money
跟着钱走
“Show me the incentive and I’ll show you the outcome.” — Charlie Munger
“给我看激励，我就给你看结果。”——查理·芒格（Charlie Munger）
Money speaks sense in a language all nations understand, and the transition of neurotechnology from academic curiosity to a high-stakes strategic race is best captured by looking directly at the money. Private funding into neurotechnology is currently experiencing a rapid consolidation phase.
金钱用所有国家都懂的语言讲道理，而神经技术从学术新奇转变为高风险战略竞赛的过程，最好通过直接看钱来把握。私人资金流入神经技术目前正经历一个快速整合阶段。
51. Putting conscious in quotes because I think the term is ill-defined and not very practical.
51. 把“有意识的”加上引号，是因为我认为这个术语定义不清，也不太实用。
The macro jump is staggering. Global neurotech venture capital ballooned from $663 million across 127 deals in 2022 to $4.8 billion across 140 deals in 2025,52 a 7x increase in just three years.
宏观跃升是惊人的。全球神经技术风险投资从 2022 年 127 笔交易的 6.63 亿美元，膨胀到 2025 年 140 笔交易的 48 亿美元，⟦52⟧ 仅仅三年就增长了 7 倍。
As this ecosystem consolidates, a dominant tier of well-capitalized corporate players has emerged, holding nearly $3 billion in combined committed capital:53
随着这一生态系统整合，一个资金充足的主导企业梯队已经出现，承诺资本合计近 30 亿美元：⟦53⟧
Neuralink: Valued near $9.5 billion following a massive $650 million Series E. The company has raised $1.3 billion cumulatively and rapidly scaled from a single participant to over a dozen human implants across four countries.
Neuralink：在一轮高达 6.5 亿美元的 E 轮融资后，估值接近 95 亿美元。该公司累计融资 13 亿美元，并迅速从单一受试者扩展到在四个国家完成十几例人体植入。
Science Corporation & BrainCo: Science holds $490 million in total funding after a recent Series C. BrainCo has accumulated roughly $400 million and filed for a Hong Kong IPO at a $1.3 billion valuation.
Science Corporation 与 BrainCo（强脑科技）：Science 在最近一轮 C 轮后总融资达 4.9 亿美元。BrainCo 累计融资约 4 亿美元，并以 13 亿美元估值申请在香港上市。
Synchron: Reached $345 million in total capital, placing its valuation around $1.0 billion with roughly ten active human implants.
Synchron：总资本达到 3.45 亿美元，估值约 10 亿美元，约有十例活跃的人体植入。
52. Figures from Naveen Rao’s Neurotech Funding Snapshot.
52. 数据来自 Naveen Rao 的《神经技术融资快照》（Neurotech Funding Snapshot）。
53. Sum of disclosed per-company funding, roughly Neuralink ~$1.29B, Science ~$490M, BrainCo ~$400M, Synchron ~$345M, Merge Labs $252M, and Blackrock Neurotech ~$200M.
53. 已披露的各公司融资合计，大约为 Neuralink 约 12.9 亿美元、Science 约 4.9 亿美元、BrainCo 约 4 亿美元、Synchron 约 3.45 亿美元、Merge Labs 2.52 亿美元，以及 Blackrock Neurotech 约 2 亿美元。
Paradromics: Around $120 million raised, advancing its first human trials through a strategic partnership with Saudi Arabia’s NEOM.
Paradromics：融资约 1.2 亿美元，通过与沙特阿拉伯新未来城（NEOM）的战略合作推进其首次人体试验。
The Next Wave: Merge Labs secured a $252 million round led by OpenAI to focus explicitly on semi- invasive BCI. Blackrock Neurotech was stabilized by a $200 million majority stake from Tether.
下一波：Merge Labs 获得由 OpenAI 领投的 2.52 亿美元融资，明确聚焦半侵入式 BCI。Blackrock Neurotech 因 Tether 收购 2 亿美元多数股权而稳住局面。
The adjacent neurotechnology stack to BCI has also grown larger in recent years: neuromodulation is already a $7 billion market, neuroprosthetics sits around $13.5 billion, and brain implants around $7 billion.54
与 BCI 相邻的神经技术栈近年来也变得更大：神经调控已是一个 70 亿美元的市场，神经假体约 135 亿美元，脑植入物约 70 亿美元。⟦54⟧
When you look past private markets and zoom out to the state level, the public funding balance reveals a broader pattern.
当你越过私人市场、拉远到国家层面时，公共资金的平衡揭示出一种更广泛的模式。
54. Sources: Grand View Research and MarketsandMarkets.
54. 来源：Grand View Research 和 MarketsandMarkets。
Western public brain-research funding is now over $4 billion. The US NIH BRAIN Initiative has deployed roughly $3.0 billion across more than 1,300 projects. Europe added €607 million through the Human Brain Project before it ended in 2023. Japan and Korea contributed roughly $330 million and $320 million respectively, while the UK is now adding around £200 million across ARIA and UK Biobank brain-imaging efforts.55
西方公共脑研究资助目前已超过 40 亿美元。美国国立卫生研究院（NIH）的 BRAIN 计划已在 1,300 多个项目上投入约 30 亿美元。欧洲通过“人脑计划”（Human Brain Project）在 2023 年结束前追加了 6.07 亿欧元。日本和韩国分别贡献了约 3.3 亿美元和 3.2 亿美元，而英国目前正通过 ARIA 和英国生物银行（UK Biobank）的脑成像工作追加约 2 亿英镑。⟦55⟧
China is moving at a comparable scale. It plans on deploying a ¥100 billion (roughly $14 billion) brain- project commitment over 15 years; and has already deployed $750 million from 2021 to 2025.
中国正以相当的规模推进。它计划在 15 年内投入 1,000 亿元人民币（约 140 亿美元）的脑计划承诺；并已在 2021 至 2025 年间投入 7.5 亿美元。
Cognitive Sovereignty
认知主权
The AI race has so far been a war over compute, fought through chips, fabs, and export controls. BCI opens a new front: neural data and access to human cognition. Whoever assembles the largest, highest-fidelity neural corpus will get to train the best foundation model for the brain.
迄今为止，AI 竞赛是一场围绕算力的战争，通过芯片、晶圆厂和出口管制来展开。BCI 开辟了一条新战线：神经数据以及对人类认知的获取。谁组装出最大、最高保真的神经语料库，谁就能训练出最好的大脑基础模型。
55. Sources for the Japan, Korea, and UK public figures: Japan’s Brain/MINDS, Korea’s Brain Initiative, and the UK’s ARIA plus UK Biobank brain imaging.
55. 日本、韩国和英国公共数据的来源：日本的 Brain/MINDS、韩国的脑计划，以及英国的 ARIA 加上英国生物银行脑成像。
The familiar dynamics we have seen with AI turning into a strategic technology will follow: concentration, sovereignty, capital, standards, and eventually export controls. But this time, the asset is not text, code, or images, it is the human mind itself. Over the coming years, governments will move more aggressively to control and dominate this new frontier.
我们在 AI 转变为战略技术时所看到的熟悉动态将会重演：集中、主权、资本、标准，以及最终的出口管制。但这一次，资产不再是文本、代码或图像，而是人类心智本身。在未来几年里，各国政府将更加积极地控制和主导这一新前沿。
Cognitive sovereignty will become a major issue: the question of who can collect, model, and act on signals from the nervous system. Neural data is especially sensitive because its meaning can change retroactively, a noisy EEG trace that reveals little in 2026 may expose critical information to a better model in 2032.
认知主权将成为一项重大议题：谁能够采集、建模并作用于来自神经系统的信号。神经数据尤为敏感，因为它的含义可以事后改变——一条在 2026 年几乎什么都揭示不了的嘈杂 EEG 轨迹，到 2032 年面对更好的模型时，可能暴露出关键信息。
As a result, who owns neural data, and on whose soil it sits, will become a matter of national security. Governments will require it to be stored domestically, as several already do for genomic and health data.
因此，谁拥有神经数据、以及它存放在谁的土地上，将成为国家安全问题。各国政府将要求它在国内存储，正如若干国家已经对基因组和健康数据所做的那样。
Sidenote · 旁注
The same dynamic already played out with DNA. As sequencing matured, world leaders and their security teams grew wary of leaving biological traces in hostile places, on the theory that a stray sample could later be turned into a targeted bioweapon. This paranoia escalated to the point of cleaning up after presidential visits to avoid the creation of a targeted bioweapon by adversaries (see Hacking the President’s DNA). The threat need not even materialize; the data simply gets more sensitive in hindsight, once a better technology can make use of it.
同样的动态已经在 DNA 上上演过。随着测序技术成熟，各国领导人和他们的安保团队对在敌对地点留下生物痕迹变得警惕，其理论是：一份散落的样本日后可能被转化为定向生物武器。这种偏执升级到了在总统访问后进行清理、以免对手制造定向生物武器的程度（见《黑客入侵总统的 DNA》（Hacking the President’s DNA））。威胁甚至不必真正兑现；一旦更好的技术能够利用这些数据，数据在事后就会变得更加敏感。
Neuroethics will also become one of the most consequential policy arenas of the coming decade. The countries that establish the first serious standards for neural data will therefore do more than protect their citizens, they will shape who can build the technology, what it can be trained on, and who ultimately controls it.
神经伦理也将成为未来十年最具后果的政策场域之一。因此，那些率先为神经数据建立严肃标准的国家，所做的将不只是保护自己的公民，它们还将塑造谁可以建造这项技术、它可以在什么数据上训练，以及最终由谁控制它。
The Neural Chessboard
神经棋盘
The main actors in the BCI race are currently the United States, China, Europe, and a few other major countries like the UK, Korea and Japan.
BCI 竞赛的主要参与者目前是美国、中国、欧洲，以及英国、韩国和日本等其他几个主要国家。
As of now, the US still leads the pack. A momentum partly due to Silicon Valley and partly to the US government.
截至目前，美国仍领先于群体。这股势头部分源于硅谷，部分源于美国政府。
On the private side, the US has the strongest startup ecosystem, the deepest venture capital markets, the frontier AI labs, the cloud infrastructure, and companies like Neuralink, Synchron, Paradromics, Precision, and others. On the public side, agencies like DARPA have funded neurotechnology for decades, especially in the awkward early phase where the science was too advanced for normal grants but too immature for the private market.
在私人一侧，美国拥有最强的初创生态系统、最深的风险投资市场、前沿 AI 实验室、云基础设施，以及 Neuralink、Synchron、Paradromics、Precision 等公司。在公共一侧，DARPA 等机构数十年来一直资助神经技术，尤其是在科学对于普通资助来说过于超前、对于私人市场来说又过于不成熟的那个尴尬早期阶段。
However, the US government has been less coordinated than China in recent years.
然而，近年来美国政府的协调程度不如中国。
The US is already developing an export-control instinct around neurotechnology. The Congressional Research Service has flagged BCI as a potential control category.56 Similar to AI, a new battleground will open on the BCI supply chain front. It will happen across a vast array of chokepoints: sensors and electrodes, flexible bioelectronics, BCI implants, surgical robotics, biocompatible materials, neural decoding models, neural datasets, regulatory approvals, clinical trial networks, talent, and more. The geopolitical fight will not just be over who builds the best headset or implant. It will be over who controls the underlying means of production.
美国已经在围绕神经技术形成一种出口管制本能。国会研究服务部已将 BCI 列为潜在的管制类别。⟦56⟧ 与 AI 类似，一场新的争夺战将在 BCI 供应链前线展开。它将跨越一系列广泛的卡点：传感器和电极、柔性生物电子、BCI 植入物、手术机器人、生物相容材料、神经解码模型、神经数据集、监管审批、临床试验网络、人才，等等。地缘政治之争将不只是谁造出最好的头戴设备或植入物，还将是谁控制底层的生产资料。
As things stand, the US is still the most likely country to produce the first truly powerful consumer- grade neurotech platforms. However, its fragmented regulatory environment and habit of letting private platforms become de facto governance systems make it equally likely to produce the first major neurodata scandal. That is the American model at its best and worst: incredible at creating platforms, terrible at governing them.
就目前而言，美国仍是最有可能产出第一批真正强大的消费级神经技术平台的国家。然而，其碎片化的监管环境，以及让私人平台成为事实上的治理体系的习惯，同样使它最有可能制造出第一起重大的神经数据丑闻。这就是美国模式最好和最坏的一面：极擅长创造平台，极不擅长治理它们。
China has been especially deliberate recently about BCI, turning it from a scientific ambition into an operational industrial plan. In the 14th Five-Year Plan, BCI was still mostly buried inside the broader language of brain science, brain-inspired computing, and brain-computer fusion. In the 15th Five- Year Plan, it moves up a level: BCI is explicitly named as one of China’s strategic future industries, next to quantum technology, biomanufacturing, hydrogen and nuclear fusion, embodied AI, and 6G. The plan is not just to fund labs, but to subsidize the entire manufacturing base to aim for total supply chain dominance by 2030. Today, China already holds 56% of all BCI-specific patents globally, compared to 18% for the US.57
中国最近在 BCI 上尤其深思熟虑，把它从一项科学雄心变成了一份可操作的产业计划。在“十四五”规划中，BCI 大多仍埋在脑科学、类脑计算和脑机融合的更宽泛表述之中。在“十五五”规划中，它上升了一个层次：BCI 被明确列为中国的战略性未来产业之一，与量子技术、生物制造、氢能与核聚变、具身 AI 和 6G 并列。这项计划不只是资助实验室，而是补贴整个制造业基础，目标是到 2030 年实现供应链的全面主导。今天，中国已持有全球 56% 的 BCI 专项专利，而美国为 18%。⟦57⟧
The West is losing ground; the US BRAIN Initiative has been contracting its funding while China’s Brain Project doubles down.
西方正在失地；美国的 BRAIN 计划一直在收缩资助，而中国的脑计划则加倍投入。
China is also actively forcing the demand side of the market into existence before perfect consumer products even ship. By establishing national health insurance billing codes for BCI procedures in 2025, they laid the groundwork for the world’s first commercially approved implantable BCI (Neuracle NEO) to hit the market in early 2026, beating Neuralink to the regulatory milestone.
中国也在完美的消费级产品尚未出货之前，就积极迫使市场的需求侧进入存在。通过在 2025 年为 BCI 手术设立国家医保结算编码，他们为全球首款获商业批准的植入式 BCI（Neuracle NEO，博睿康）于 2026 年初上市奠定了基础，在监管里程碑上领先于 Neuralink。
China has shown the same pattern before in electric vehicles, batteries, drones, solar, and advanced manufacturing: once Beijing decides a technology is strategic, it can align capital, factories, standards, suppliers, and customers with unusual speed. The US still leads the BCI race today, especially through its frontier startups and AI ecosystem. But China is now moving from catching up to mobilizing the whole country.
中国以前在电动汽车、电池、无人机、太阳能和先进制造上展现过同样的模式：一旦北京认定一项技术具有战略意义，它就能以不寻常的速度对齐资本、工厂、标准、供应商和客户。美国今天仍然领先 BCI 竞赛，尤其是通过其前沿初创公司和 AI 生态系统。但中国现在正从追赶转向举国动员。
Europe, by contrast, is playing the role of the regulatory superpower. The EU possesses strong neuroscience institutions, robust public ethics, and deep institutional trust, but a chronic inability to build dominant consumer tech platforms. Through comprehensive frameworks like the AI Act and global pushes for constitutional neurorights, Europe is actively setting the global ethical baseline for mental privacy, cognitive liberty, and freedom of thought.58 This gives Europe tremendous normative legitimacy, but not necessarily hard power. Europe is positioning itself to write the rules for neurotechnology, while inevitably importing the American and Chinese platforms those rules govern.
相比之下，欧洲扮演的是监管超级大国的角色。欧盟拥有强大的神经科学机构、稳健的公共伦理和深厚的制度信任，却长期无力打造主导性的消费科技平台。通过《人工智能法案》（AI Act）等全面框架，以及在全球推动宪法层面的神经权利（neurorights），欧洲正在积极为精神隐私、认知自由和思想自由设定全球伦理基线。⟦58⟧ 这赋予欧洲巨大的规范合法性，但不一定赋予硬实力。欧洲正把自己定位为神经技术规则的撰写者，同时不可避免地进口这些规则所管辖的美国和中国平台。
56. Commerce Department’s Bureau of Industry and Security requested public comment on export controls for BCI as an emerging technology.
56. 商务部工业与安全局就将 BCI 作为新兴技术实施出口管制征求了公众意见。
57. Source: JMIR Rehabilitation and Assistive Technologies.
57. 来源：《JMIR 康复与辅助技术》（JMIR Rehabilitation and Assistive Technologies）。
Defense and Dual-Use Matter
国防与两用问题
BCI is a dual-use technology.
BCI 是一项两用技术。
And the US and Chinese militaries are well aware of this; they have been subsidizing the field for years in the hope that it would become mature enough to be used in warfare.
美国和中国军方对此心知肚明；他们多年来一直在补贴这个领域，希望它成熟到足以用于战争。
The same headset that lets a designer move faster through a spatial interface could let a drone operator control a swarm without touching a joystick. The same neural decoder that helps a paralyzed patient speak could let a soldier communicate silently in a warzone.
同一款让设计师更快穿越空间界面的头戴设备，也可以让无人机操作员在不碰操纵杆的情况下控制一个机群。同一个帮助瘫痪患者说话的神经解码器，也可以让士兵在战区无声通信。
58. The EU AI Act’s Article 5 bans manipulative and subliminal systems, the closest existing regulation for mental privacy. Although, it is far from enough.
58. 欧盟《人工智能法案》第 5 条禁止操纵性和阈下系统，这是目前最接近精神隐私的现有监管。不过，这还远远不够。
This is not just speculation; defense agencies have been funding neurotechnology for decades, with particular interest in human-machine teaming, soldier monitoring, rehabilitation, training, and command latency. In war, shaving seconds off the observe-orient-decide-act loop59 matters a lot.
这不只是推测；国防机构数十年来一直在资助神经技术，尤其关注人机协同、士兵监测、康复、训练和指挥延迟。在战争中，把“观察—定向—决策—行动”回路⟦59⟧缩短几秒，意义重大。
There is already serious policy attention here. A 2025 GPPi report argues that military actors are deeply involved in US and Chinese neurotechnology ecosystems.60 The ICRC also flagged military BCI as a new international humanitarian law issue in 2025.
这里已经有严肃的政策关注。一份 2025 年全球公共政策研究所（GPPi）报告认为，军方行为体深度参与了美国和中国的神经技术生态系统。⟦60⟧ 红十字国际委员会（ICRC）也在 2025 年将军用 BCI 列为一项新的国际人道法议题。
One of the clearest tells is the cap tables. Synchron’s 2025 round brought together traditional tech investors with the Qatar Investment Authority, Australia’s National Reconstruction Fund, and In-Q- Tel, the CIA’s venture arm. Spy agencies and sovereign wealth funds investing so intently in frontier technology usually tells a clear story which goes beyond the medical device.
最清晰的信号之一是股东名单。Synchron 2025 年的融资轮汇集了传统科技投资者与卡塔尔投资局、澳大利亚国家重建基金，以及中央情报局的创投机构 In-Q-Tel。间谍机构和主权财富基金如此热切地投资前沿技术，通常讲述的是一个超出医疗器械本身的清晰故事。
Over the next few years, neurotechnology will move beyond healthcare and consumer electronics to turn into cognitive infrastructure. And just as with AI and semiconductors, any critical infrastructure technology eventually becomes a national security priority.
在未来几年里，神经技术将走出医疗和消费电子，变成认知基础设施。正如 AI 和半导体一样，任何关键基础设施技术最终都会成为国家安全优先事项。
This is where the ethical questions become impossible to avoid. As we start altering perception, intention, attention, memory, agency, and choice; we are touching human identity at its core; and when the fundamental substrate of a technology is the human mind itself, one can easily conjure a dozen dystopian scenarios for the future. So what if things go wrong?
正是在这里，伦理问题变得无法回避。当我们开始改变感知、意图、注意力、记忆、能动性和选择时，我们触及的是人类身份的核心；而当一项技术的根本基质就是人类心智本身时，人们很容易构想出十几种反乌托邦的未来情景。那么，如果事情出了问题呢？
59. The observe-orient-decide-act (OODA) is a rapid decision-making framework developed by military strategist John Boyd; the side that completes it faster seizes the initiative. An interface at the speed of thought can compress the delay significantly.
59. 观察—定向—决策—行动（OODA）是军事战略家约翰·博伊德（John Boyd）提出的快速决策框架；完成它更快的一方就能夺取主动权。一个以思想速度运作的界面可以显著压缩延迟。
60. See “Action Potentials: Neurotechnology, Brain-Computer Interfaces and the Future of Defense”.
60. 见《动作电位：神经技术、脑机接口与国防的未来》（Action Potentials: Neurotechnology, Brain-Computer Interfaces and the Future of Defense）。
22 Mind Reading at Scale: What Could Possibly Go Wrong
22 规模化读心：还能出什么问题
“Technology is neither good nor bad; nor is it neutral.” — Melvin Kranzberg
“技术既非善也非恶；它也不是中性的。”——梅尔文·克兰茨伯格（Melvin Kranzberg）
The fall of Icarus: Icarus flew too close to the sun and the wax melted, the wings worked but he aimed them wrong. A technology can work beautifully and still be pointed in the wrong direction.
伊卡洛斯的坠落：伊卡洛斯飞得离太阳太近，蜡熔化了——翅膀是有效的，但他把它们指向了错误的方向。一项技术可以运作得非常出色，却仍然被指向错误的方向。
For as long as humans have existed, the skull has been the only truly private room in the universe. This may soon be a concept of the past.
自有人类以来，颅骨一直是宇宙中唯一真正私密的房间。这或许很快就会成为过去的概念。
Every technology eventually crosses a line where the question stops being “Can we build it?” and becomes “What happens if it works?”
每一项技术最终都会越过一条线，问题从“我们能不能造出来？”变成“如果它奏效了会发生什么？”
BCI is approaching that line.
BCI 正在接近这条线。
And with neurotechnology, it does not take much imagination to see how it could go wrong. BCI is not another app, another platform, or another productivity tool; it reaches into the most intimate layer of human life: the mind itself.
而对于神经技术，要看出它可能如何出错，并不需要太多想象力。BCI 不是又一个应用、又一个平台或又一个生产力工具；它伸入人类生活最私密的一层：心智本身。
That is exactly what makes both the upside and the risks so enormous.
这正是其上行空间和风险都如此巨大的原因。
Like AI risk, the risks of neurotechnology are poorly understood, and for most people the best mental models they have come from science fiction. Given the breadth of the topic, a serious treatment would be a manifesto of its own,61 we will therefore only be scratching the surface here.
与 AI 风险一样，神经技术的风险被理解得很差，对大多数人来说，他们最好的思维模型来自科幻小说。鉴于这一话题的广度，严肃的处理本身就可以写成一部宣言，⟦61⟧ 因此我们在这里只会浅尝辄止。
The Neurotech Risk Surface
神经技术的风险面
The risks below are ordered by expanding blast radius; from what neural interfaces can reveal and manipulate within an individual mind, to their effects on identity, society, and ultimately the trajectory of AI:
以下风险按不断扩大的冲击半径排列：从神经接口能在个体心智内部揭示和操纵什么，到它们对身份、社会，以及最终对 AI 轨迹的影响：
Mental privacy. Neural data is a leaky signal: it can reveal more than the user intends. As decoders improve, a neural signal that today tells you whether someone is thirsty can later be used to infer depression risk, political leanings, attraction, addiction vulnerability, or job performance. Harmless data today can become intimate data tomorrow, and once collected, it is hard to take back.
精神隐私。神经数据是一种会泄漏的信号：它能揭示的，往往比用户打算分享的更多。随着解码器改进，一条今天告诉你某人是否口渴的神经信号，日后可以被用来推断抑郁风险、政治倾向、吸引力、成瘾易感性或工作表现。今天无害的数据，明天可能变成私密数据；而一旦采集，就很难收回。
61. Further reading: Yuste et al., Ienca and Andorno, and Nita Farahany’s The Battle for Your Brain.
61. 延伸阅读：Yuste 等人、Ienca 与 Andorno，以及妮塔·法拉哈尼（Nita Farahany）的《争夺你的大脑》（The Battle for Your Brain）。
Thirst neurons in a mouse brain. This is what thirst looks like before it becomes a sentence. Source: NIH.
小鼠大脑中的口渴神经元。这就是口渴在变成一句话之前的样子。来源：NIH。
Manipulation. Once BCIs mature, they will stop being passive sensors and writing will become possible. They will be able to recommend, nudge, stimulate, suppress, or amplify thoughts and emotions. The main failure mode becomes not only malicious actors hacking your thoughts, but also your attention, mood, and choices being gradually mediated by a system optimizing for something you did not choose, with access to your raw cognitive machinery. We have run a version of this experiment before, with recommendation algorithms on social media restructuring public discourse while holding nothing but your click history. Give those same algorithms the error signal straight from your limbic system, and the result could be dangerously superhuman persuasion.
操纵。一旦 BCI 成熟，它们将不再是被动的传感器，写入将成为可能。它们将能够推荐、轻推、刺激、抑制或放大思想和情绪。主要的失败模式将不只是恶意行为体黑客入侵你的思想，还包括你的注意力、情绪和选择被一个为你并未选择的目标而优化的系统逐渐中介，而这个系统能够接触到你原始的认知机制。我们以前已经做过这个实验的一个版本：社交媒体上的推荐算法在只掌握你点击历史的情况下，就重构了公共话语。把同样的算法接到直接来自你边缘系统的错误信号上，结果可能是危险的超人类说服。
Identity drift. A BCI that augments someone can also create dependency, dissociation, and over-trust in machine output. With a closed-loop AI operating this close to the mind, identity itself blurs. Did I choose that, or did the model predict and complete me? Did the stimulation improve my mood, or alter my preferences? Am I becoming more myself, or more optimized? These questions have no clean answers, and the drift they describe will be hard to control.
身份漂移。一个增强某人的 BCI，也可以造成依赖、解离，以及对机器输出的过度信任。当一个闭环 AI 如此贴近心智运作时，身份本身就会变得模糊。那是我选择的，还是模型预测并补全了我？刺激改善了我的情绪，还是改变了我的偏好？我是在成为更真实的自己，还是在被优化？这些问题没有干净的答案，而它们所描述的漂移将很难控制。
Regulatory lag. Regulation is nowhere near ready, and the ceiling problem makes it worse: when you do not know what a technology will eventually decode, the rules have to cover the extreme futures, not just the current demos. If a few minutes of EEG could one day reveal whether someone committed tax fraud, is it acceptable for that data to sit in a training corpus today? Consent has a time-machine problem: you find out what you gave away only after the models improve. Genomics already screened this movie, 23andMe’s bankruptcy turned the DNA of 15 million people into an asset for sale.62 Consumer neurotech is currently worse, in an audit of 30 companies, 29 granted themselves effectively unrestricted access to neural data, nearly all reserved the right to transfer it, only one in five even mentions encryption, and exactly one rules out sharing it altogether.63 Meanwhile the deepest questions have no case law at all. For instance: if a BCI logs your P30064 recognition response to a crime scene photo, can it be subpoenaed? Does the Fifth Amendment cover a brainwave?65
监管滞后。监管远未就绪，而上限问题使情况更糟：当你不知道一项技术最终将解码出什么时，规则必须覆盖极端的未来，而不仅仅是当前的演示。如果几分钟的 EEG 有朝一日能揭示某人是否逃了税，今天把这些数据放进一个训练语料库是否可以接受？同意有一个“时间机器”问题：只有在模型改进之后，你才会发现自己给出了什么。基因组学已经预演过这部电影：23andMe 的破产把 1,500 万人的 DNA 变成了一项待售资产。⟦62⟧ 消费级神经技术目前更糟：在对 30 家公司的审计中，29 家赋予自己对神经数据实际上不受限制的访问权，几乎全部保留了转让权，只有五分之一甚至提到加密，而明确排除分享的恰好只有一家。⟦63⟧ 与此同时，最深层的问题完全没有判例法。例如：如果一台 BCI 记录下你对犯罪现场照片的 P300⟦64⟧ 识别反应，它可以被传票调取吗？美国宪法第五修正案是否覆盖脑电波？⟦65⟧
62. When 23andMe went bankrupt in March 2025, the DNA of its ~15 million customers became an asset for sale, going for $305 million.
62. 当 23andMe 于 2025 年 3 月破产时，其约 1,500 万客户的 DNA 成了一项待售资产，成交价为 3.05 亿美元。
63. Source: Safeguarding Brain Data.
63. 来源：《守护脑数据》（Safeguarding Brain Data）。
64. The P300 is an involuntary brainwave that peaks about 300 milliseconds after a stimulus and spikes when a person recognizes something familiar. It is the basis of concealed-information tests: the brain flags recognition before the person decides to reveal anything.
64. P300 是一种不由自主的脑电波，在刺激后约 300 毫秒达到峰值，并在一个人识别出熟悉之物时出现尖峰。它是隐蔽信息测试的基础：大脑在当事人决定是否透露任何信息之前，就会标出识别。
65. The Fifth Amendment protects you from being forced to testify against yourself. Whether a decoded brain signal counts as testimony, or as physical evidence like a fingerprint, is a question no court has faced.
65. 第五修正案保护你不被强迫作出对自己不利的证词。一段被解码的脑信号究竟算证词，还是像指纹一样的物证，是任何法院都尚未面对过的问题。
Structural tension in society. Human society has run for millennia on the assumption that thoughts are private. Courts, marriages, negotiations, elections: all of them depend on the fact that no one can check what you really think. AI already challenged our monopoly on intelligence, sending ripples and fractures through society. BCI is about to challenge the privacy of the mind. If that shift arrives faster than society can rebuild itself around the new reality, the damage could be severe. There is also the risk of cognitive inequality and concentration of power: whoever controls the brain-and-AI stack controls the interface to intelligence itself, which could widen the gap between social classes, age groups, and countries.
社会中的结构性张力。人类社会几千年来一直运行在“思想是私密的”这一假设之上。法庭、婚姻、谈判、选举：它们全都依赖于没有人能核验你真正在想什么。AI 已经挑战了我们对智能的垄断，在社会中激起涟漪和裂痕。BCI 即将挑战心智的隐私。如果这一转变到来的速度，快于社会围绕新现实重建自身的速度，损害可能是严重的。还有认知不平等和权力集中的风险：谁控制脑与 AI 的技术栈，谁就控制通向智能本身的界面，这可能扩大社会阶层、年龄群体和国家之间的差距。
Brain hacking. As strange as it sounds, everything connected can be attacked, and a read-and-write brain device would be the most intimate attack surface ever shipped. The threat model is ordinary cybersecurity with new stakes: signal interception, spoofed inputs, malicious neuromodulation, denial of service. A ransomware attack would feel different if what it encrypted were your ability to think clearly. Every security failure of the last 30 years can be replayed here, against a piece of hardware you cannot uninstall.
大脑黑客攻击。尽管听起来很奇怪，凡是联网的东西都可以被攻击，而一台可读可写的脑设备将是有史以来出货过的最私密的攻击面。威胁模型就是普通的网络安全，只是赌注更新了：信号截获、伪造输入、恶意神经调控、拒绝服务。如果勒索软件加密的是你清晰思考的能力，那种攻击会感觉完全不同。过去 30 年的每一次安全失败都可以在这里重演，而对象是一件你无法卸载的硬件。
The surveillance state. Every surveillance technology has followed the same trajectory: smaller, cheaper, easier to hide, every year. Cameras and microphones did it; neural sensors will too. In the wrong hands that means emotional surveillance, dissident profiling, interrogation, and eventually thought decoded at population scale. The polygraph66 was junk science and still got a century of use; now imagine a version that actually works, deployed at scale by an authoritarian state.
监视国家。每一项监视技术都遵循同一条轨迹：一年比一年更小、更便宜、更容易隐藏。摄像头和麦克风如此；神经传感器也将如此。落入错误的人手中，这意味着情绪监视、异议者画像、审讯，以及最终在人群尺度上解码思想。测谎仪⟦66⟧ 本是垃圾科学，却仍被使用了一个世纪；现在想象一个真正奏效的版本，被一个威权国家大规模部署。
The AI-safety backfire. On the AI side, the sharpest risk is that neurotechnology does not help with alignment at all and only augments AI capabilities. Humanity’s inner life becomes one more pretraining corpus while the technology grows ever harder to control. And even if BCI does help steer and align AI, there is a risk that we align it on a subset of minds not representative of humanity. Lastly, if neural data becomes as valuable as we expect, predatory behaviors may emerge to collect it at scale.
AI 安全的反噬。在 AI 一侧，最尖锐的风险是神经技术根本无助于对齐，而只是增强了 AI 的能力。人类的内在生活变成又一份预训练语料，而这项技术变得越来越难以控制。即便 BCI 确实有助于引导和对齐 AI，也存在我们把它对齐到一小部分不能代表人类的心智上的风险。最后，如果神经数据变得如我们预期的那样宝贵，可能会出现掠夺性行为来大规模采集它。
The meta-risk. Underneath all of these sits something larger: the risk that humanity is simply not ready for this shift of its reality. We may end up governing twenty-first-century access to the mind with twentieth-century categories, because those are the only ones we have. AI was the first blow to human identity; BCI is the second, and it raises the question directly. What is a human being when the boundaries of the mind are negotiable? What is the self? What is our role in the world that follows? None of these questions will be easy to answer.
元风险。在所有这些之下，潜藏着某种更大的东西：人类根本还没有为这场现实转变做好准备的风险。我们可能最终用二十世纪的范畴去治理二十一世纪对心智的获取，因为那是我们仅有的范畴。AI 是对人类身份的第一次打击；BCI 是第二次，而且它直接提出了这个问题。当心智的边界变得可协商时，人是什么？自我是什么？我们在随后那个世界中的角色是什么？这些问题没有一个会容易回答。
Neurotechnology will force some of the most profound questions humanity has ever had to answer, on the timeline of a product cycle.
神经技术将迫使人类去回答它所面对过的一些最深刻的问题，而且是以一个产品周期的时间尺度。
How This Goes Right
这件事怎样才会走对
So if the risk surface is this large, why build BCI so fast?
那么，如果风险面如此之大，为什么还要如此快速地建造 BCI？
66. The polygraph, or “lie detector”, measures stress as a proxy for lying. Its scientific basis is shaky at best, yet it was used on millions anyway.
66. 测谎仪（polygraph，或称“测谎器”）把压力测量作为说谎的替代指标。其科学基础充其量是不稳固的，却仍然被用在了数百万人身上。
The honest answer is that, were AI capabilities not growing exponentially, I would advocate for slowing down the development of neurotechnology to give us more time to get it right. But as the world stands, building BCI has now become a necessity. The age of machines is upon us, and BCI is one of the few technologies with a real chance of making that transition survivable for humanity, one of the few chances we have to do alignment-by-integration. At this point it is not just an aspirational techno-utopian dream, it is becoming existential.
诚实的答案是：如果 AI 能力没有在指数增长，我会主张放缓神经技术的发展，以便给我们更多时间把它做对。但就世界目前的状况而言，建造 BCI 现在已经成为一种必要。机器的时代已经来临，而 BCI 是少数有真正机会让这场转变对人类而言可以生存下去的技术之一，是我们实现融合式对齐的少数机会之一。到了这个时候，它不再只是一个抱负满满的技术乌托邦梦想，它正在变得关乎存亡。
Building it right will be extraordinarily hard, as every scenario above should have made clear. But it is doable, good outcomes are possible; they simply have to be treated as an active design problem rather than the default outcome. And because we still only half understand the technology, the frameworks we build around it have to evolve dynamically.
把它做对将极其困难，正如上面每一个情景都已表明的。但这是可以做到的，好的结果是可能的；它们只是必须被当作一个主动的设计问题来对待，而不是默认的结果。而且，因为我们仍然只理解这项技术的一半，围绕它建立的框架必须动态演化。
We should start with architecture, because the strongest guardrails are not legal but technical. Raw neural data should leave the device as rarely as possible: process locally, transmit intent rather than brainwaves, export features rather than raw traces, train through federated learning and differential privacy so models improve without the signal ever pooling on anyone’s server. Where large corpora are genuinely needed, they can live in independent data trusts that audit and license access, rather than letting any single platform accumulate humanity’s inner life. A law can be repealed by the next administration; but an architecture has to be re-engineered. Privacy enforced by physics is the only kind that survives a bad decade.
我们应该从架构开始，因为最强的护栏不是法律的，而是技术的。原始神经数据应尽可能少地离开设备：在本地处理，传输意图而非脑电波，导出特征而非原始轨迹，通过联邦学习和差分隐私来训练，使模型改进而信号从不在任何人的服务器上汇集。在确实需要大型语料库的地方，它们可以存放在独立的数据信托中，由信托审计并授权访问，而不是让任何一个平台积累人类的内在生活。一部法律可以被下一届政府废除；但一种架构必须被重新工程化。由物理学强制执行的隐私，是唯一能在糟糕的十年中幸存下来的那种隐私。
We have already worked miracles with the internet and with biotechnology, we can pull it off once more.
我们已经在互联网和生物技术上创造过奇迹，我们可以再做成一次。
The same logic applies to business models. Social media did not become an outrage machine because its engineers were malicious; it became one because advertising was the revenue model and attention was the product. The cheapest way to avoid replaying that mistake at the neural layer is a bright line: brain data and advertising must never meet.
同样的逻辑也适用于商业模式。社交媒体之所以变成一台愤怒机器，并不是因为其工程师心怀恶意；而是因为广告是收入模式，注意力是产品。避免在神经层重演这一错误，最便宜的方式是划一条鲜明的界线：脑数据与广告永远不得相遇。
Consent has to grow up too. A checkbox at install cannot govern a data stream whose meaning changes as models improve. Neural consent needs to be granular, auditable, revocable, and, above all, perishable: it should expire unless renewed, because next year’s models will read what this year’s cannot. That is the answer to the time-machine problem. If you cannot take back what you streamed, then streaming has to become a running conversation rather than a one-time signature.
同意也必须长大。安装时的一个复选框，无法治理一条其含义会随模型改进而变化的数据流。神经同意需要是精细的、可审计的、可撤销的，而且最重要的是，是会过期的：除非续期，否则它应当失效，因为明年的模型将读出今年的模型读不出的东西。这就是对“时间机器”问题的回答。如果你无法收回自己曾经传输的东西，那么传输就必须变成一场持续的对话，而不是一次签名。
The law, for its part, needs one conceptual upgrade more than it needs a thousand new pages: neural data is not app data, not wearable data, not even ordinary biometric data. It deserves its own category at the very top of the sensitivity hierarchy, with strict purpose limitation, no resale, no secondary inference (the focus tracker that covertly scores your politics should be illegal, not just creepy), no employer or insurer access by default, robust deletion rights, and hard limits on what can be trained from it. Above all, cognitive liberty, the right to mental self-determination, must be recognized as a fundamental right rather than a nice idea in an ethics paper.
法律就其本身而言，需要的与其说是一千页新条文，不如说是一次概念升级：神经数据不是应用数据，不是可穿戴设备数据，甚至不是普通的生物识别数据。它理应在敏感度层级的最顶端拥有自己的类别，并带有严格的目的限制、禁止转售、禁止二次推断（那个暗中给你的政治立场打分的专注追踪器应当是非法的，而不仅仅是令人不适的）、默认禁止雇主或保险公司访问、强有力的删除权，以及对可以从中训练什么的硬性限制。最重要的是，认知自由——精神上的自我决定权——必须被承认为一项基本权利，而不是伦理论文里的一个漂亮想法。
Sidenote · 旁注
And here, unusually, there is good news. Chile wrote neurorights into its constitution in 2021. Colorado, California, Montana, and Connecticut have extended their data-privacy laws to neural data, and their definitions are becoming the de facto American standard. UNESCO’s Recommendation on the Ethics of Neurotechnology, adopted by its member states in November 2025, is the first global normative instrument for the field: soft law, but a shared vocabulary. The EU AI Act’s guidance already cites brain- computer interfaces by name in its rules on prohibited manipulation. In roughly two years, neural data has gone from no specific protection anywhere to binding or near-binding coverage for some 50 million people. Regulation usually arrives years after the scandal, for once, it has a chance of arriving before.
而在这里，不同寻常的是，有好消息。智利于 2021 年将神经权利写入宪法。科罗拉多、加利福尼亚、蒙大拿和康涅狄格已将数据隐私法扩展到神经数据，它们的定义正在成为事实上的美国标准。联合国教科文组织（UNESCO）于 2025 年 11 月由其成员国通过的《神经技术伦理建议书》，是该领域第一份全球规范性文书：虽是软法，却是一份共享的词汇。欧盟《人工智能法案》的指导已经在关于禁止操纵的规则中点名提到脑机接口。大约两年之内，神经数据从在任何地方都没有专门保护，变成了覆盖约 5,000 万人的具有约束力或接近约束力的保护。监管通常在丑闻发生多年后才到来；这一次，它有机会在丑闻之前到来。
Two lines deserve to be drawn thicker than all the others. First, the write channel is sacred. Reading the brain is dangerous; writing to it is civilization-level dangerous, and the standards should treat it accordingly: the closer a system comes to changing a mental state, the higher its burden of proof should be. Second, the brain must be treated as critical infrastructure: threat-modeled, red-teamed, secure by default, and protected internationally by red lines drawn before escalation rather than after. No neural interrogation. No compulsory brain monitoring. No covert neuromodulation. And no neuroweapons. Humanity eventually built such norms for biological and chemical weapons; this time we could build them before the first use instead of after it.
有两条线应当画得比其他所有线更粗。第一，写入通道是神圣的。读取大脑是危险的；向大脑写入则是文明级别的危险，标准应当相应对待：一个系统越接近改变一种心理状态，其证明负担就应当越高。第二，大脑必须被当作关键基础设施来对待：进行威胁建模、红队测试、默认安全，并在国际上由升级之前而非之后划出的红线加以保护。禁止神经审讯。禁止强制性脑监测。禁止隐蔽的神经调控。禁止神经武器。人类最终为生物和化学武器建立了这样的规范；这一次，我们可以在首次使用之前、而不是之后建立它们。
There is also a failure mode on the other side though, if we regulate this field into the ground, we will not get more safety; we will freeze BCI precisely during the decades when it matters most for navigating the transition to advanced AI. And in addition this hands the frontier to whoever does not ask permission. The goal is not to be slow, it is to be deliberate about how we build the technology. We have to move fast but intentionally: humanity’s future is at stake, we cannot afford to pause here, and we cannot afford to get it wrong.
不过，另一侧也有一种失败模式：如果我们把这个领域监管得走投无路，我们得到的将不是更多安全；我们将恰好在 BCI 对驾驭向高级 AI 的过渡最为重要的那几十年里把它冻结。此外，这还会把前沿拱手让给那些不问许可的人。目标不是要慢，而是要在如何建造这项技术上审慎。我们必须快速但有意地行动：人类的未来岌岌可危，我们负担不起在这里停下来，也负担不起把它做错。
Alongside all of this, we need to raise public literacy. Just as AI did, neurotechnology is about to enter the global conversation, and it should be something people actually understand. A society with an accurate mental model of what these devices can and cannot do is better equipped to choose well, it also becomes harder to panic, and harder to lull.
与此同时，我们需要提高公众素养。正如 AI 所做的那样，神经技术即将进入全球对话，它应当成为人们真正理解的东西。一个对这些设备能做什么、不能做什么拥有准确思维模型的社会，更能做出好的选择，也更难恐慌，更难被安抚入睡。
Kranzberg’s law67 is usually quoted as a warning. This technology will not be good on its own, and it does not have to be bad. It will be exactly as good as the standards we set while it is still young enough to accept them, and that work is needed right now.
克兰茨伯格定律⟦67⟧ 通常被当作一条警告来引用。这项技术不会自行变好，它也不必变坏。它将恰好与我们在它还年轻到足以接受这些标准时所设定的标准一样好，而这项工作现在就需要做。
Ultimately, if neurotech treats neural data the way ad-tech treats browsing data, it deserves to fail. Every guardrail in this section, the architecture, the consent, the law, the red lines, should exist to keep the technology on the right side of history.
归根结底，如果神经技术像广告技术对待浏览数据那样对待神经数据，它就应当失败。本节中的每一道护栏——架构、同意、法律、红线——都应当存在，以使这项技术站在历史正确的一边。
And beneath all the policy sits a choice no regulator can make for us: deciding what we want to preserve about being human. Is it agency? Authenticity? Intelligence? Mental self-determination? The privacy of an inner life? We should name those things deliberately and design toward them, or they will be defined for us.
而在所有政策之下，有一个监管者无法替我们做出的选择：决定我们希望保全关于作为人的哪些东西。是能动性？真实性？智能？精神上的自我决定？内在生活的隐私？我们应当有意地为这些东西命名，并朝它们去设计，否则它们将被替我们定义。
BCI has the potential to help humanity keep its hands on the wheel as AI grows ever more powerful, while unlocking countless other capabilities along the way, all through a technology we could barely dream of a decade ago. But it is also one of the most dangerous technologies humanity has ever brought within reach, and it should be treated accordingly.
BCI 有潜力帮助人类在 AI 日益强大时把手放在方向盘上，并沿途解锁无数其他能力，而这一切都通过一项十年前我们几乎无法想象的技术来实现。但它也是人类曾经触及过的最危险的技术之一，应当被相应对待。
So far, I have worn the hat of the cautiously optimistic futurist, mapping the most plausible path forward, but any serious thesis must survive its strongest objections. So let us swap hats: if this vision hits a wall, where will it happen, and what could derail it?
到目前为止，我戴着谨慎乐观的未来主义者的帽子，描绘最可信的前进路径，但任何严肃的论点都必须经受住其最强的反驳。那么让我们换一顶帽子：如果这一愿景撞上墙壁，它会在哪里发生，又有什么可能使它脱轨？
23 The Case Against the Case
23 对这一论点的反驳
“I am not entitled to have an opinion unless I can state the arguments against my position better than the people who are in the opposition.” — Charlie Munger
“除非我能比反对者更好地陈述反对我立场的论据，否则我没有资格持有一个观点。”——查理·芒格
There is no shortage of reasonable objections to this thesis, ranging from the physics of the human skull to the information theory of foundation models and the basic economics of hardware scaling. To earn the right to be taken seriously, I cannot just swat at strawmen; I have to face the strongest objections head-on.
对这一论点并不缺乏合理的反驳，从人类颅骨的物理学，到基础模型的信息论，再到硬件规模化的基本经济学。要赢得被认真对待的权利，我不能只去拍打稻草人；我必须正面面对最强的反驳。
Here are the 13 objections that should be addressed.
以下是应当予以回应的 13 条反驳。
01. Non-invasive may have a fundamental signal ceiling that scaling cannot break.
01. 非侵入式可能存在规模化无法突破的根本信号上限。
The skull blurs and attenuates neural activity, leaving a noisy, underdetermined signal. Scaling can extract hidden structure, but it cannot recover information that physics has erased.
颅骨会模糊并衰减神经活动，留下一个嘈杂、欠定的信号。规模化可以提取隐藏的结构，但它无法恢复物理学已经抹去的信息。
67. Melvin Kranzberg’s first law of technology, and the epigraph of this section: “Technology is neither good nor bad; nor is it neutral.”
67. 梅尔文·克兰茨伯格的技术第一定律，也是本节的题记：“技术既非善也非恶；它也不是中性的。”
RESPONSE We do not know where that ceiling sits. This is ultimately an empirical question, and the field has not yet scaled hardware, data, and models far enough to answer it.
回应 我们不知道那个上限在哪里。这归根结底是一个经验问题，而这个领域尚未把硬件、数据和模型规模化到足以回答它的程度。
02. Non-invasive research may divert resources from the higher-potential invasive path.
02. 非侵入式研究可能把资源从潜力更高的侵入式路径上转移走。
Capital, talent, and institutional attention are finite. If invasive systems have a much higher capability ceiling, directing the field toward easier-to-market headsets could delay the path with the greatest long-term potential.
资本、人才和机构注意力是有限的。如果侵入式系统有高得多的能力上限，把整个领域导向更容易市场化的头戴设备，可能推迟那条长期潜力最大的路径。
RESPONSE That argument made more sense in 2015 than it does in 2026. Hardware advances, data standards, decoder architectures, and clinical infrastructure increasingly transfer across modalities. Capital and talent drawn into non-invasive BCI strengthen the broader field, even if invasive ultimately wins. More importantly, for the foreseeable future, non-invasive is the only path with a realistic chance of escaping the lab and reaching millions of people.
回应 这一论点在 2015 年比在 2026 年更说得通。硬件进步、数据标准、解码器架构和临床基础设施日益跨模态迁移。被吸入非侵入式 BCI 的资本和人才会强化更广泛的领域，即便最终获胜的是侵入式。更重要的是，在可预见的未来，非侵入式是唯一有现实机会走出实验室、触达数百万人的路径。
03. The brain may not be the same kind of scaling problem as the internet.
03. 大脑可能并不是与互联网同类的规模化问题。
Brain data is noisier, more individual, and structured across many time scales; its labels are also far weaker. The Bitter Lesson may not transfer cleanly.
脑数据噪声更大、更具个体差异，并在多个时间尺度上结构化；其标签也弱得多。“苦涩的教训”可能无法干净地迁移过来。
RESPONSE As with the signal ceiling, this is a question only scale can settle. So far, the evidence points in the right direction: ZuCo, DEAP, AllJoined, BrainLM, and Banville et al. all show performance improving with more neural data across multiple modalities. ENIGMA reached state-of-the-art EEG image decoding on both consumer and research-grade hardware, using under 1% of the parameters of prior systems and only 15 minutes of calibration for a new subject. There is early evidence, not just aspirational hopes.
回应 与信号上限一样，这是一个只有规模才能解决的问题。到目前为止，证据指向正确的方向：ZuCo、DEAP、AllJoined、BrainLM 以及 Banville 等人的研究都表明，在多种模态上，性能随着更多神经数据而提升。ENIGMA 在消费级和研究级硬件上都达到了 EEG 图像解码的最先进水平，所用参数不足先前系统的 1%，对新受试者只需 15 分钟校准。这是早期证据，而不只是抱负性的希望。
04. BCI may lack the universal self-supervised68 objective that made AI scaling work.
04. BCI 可能缺乏使 AI 规模化奏效的那种普遍的自监督⟦68⟧目标。
Language modeling scaled because predict-the-next-token is stupidly universal: every paragraph on the internet is a labeled training example for free. Brain decoding has no such clean objective; most current approaches train task-specific decoders that do not transfer.
语言建模之所以能够规模化，是因为“预测下一个词元”愚蠢地普遍：互联网上的每一段文字都免费成为带标签的训练样本。脑解码没有如此干净的目标；目前大多数方法训练的是不能迁移的任务专用解码器。
RESPONSE The shape of the emerging objective is starting to look like multimodal world-brain prediction. Given a neural signal plus context (eye movement, audio, behavior, task state, screen content, body pose), learn the latent representation that predicts the missing channel. Less elegant than next-token prediction, but not obviously less scalable; indeed, AI has since scaled successfully on messier objectives, from masked-image modeling to contrastive learning across images and text. And the unit of training data, “brain signal plus context”, is exactly what consumer hardware will produce by default.
回应 正在涌现的目标形态，开始看起来像多模态的“世界—大脑”预测。给定一段神经信号加上上下文（眼动、音频、行为、任务状态、屏幕内容、身体姿态），学习能够预测缺失通道的潜在表征。它不如预测下一个词元那么优雅，但并不明显更不可规模化；事实上，AI 此后已经在更杂乱的目标上成功规模化，从掩码图像建模到跨图像和文本的对比学习。而训练数据的单元——“脑信号加上下文”——恰恰是消费级硬件默认就会产生的东西。
68. Self-supervised learning (SSL) trains models using targets generated by the data itself rather than human labels. The canonical example is predict-the-next-token in language modeling; every paragraph on the internet becomes a labeled example for free. SSL is the reason modern AI scaled so cleanly.
68. 自监督学习（SSL）用数据自身生成的目标来训练模型，而不是用人类标签。典型例子是语言建模中的“预测下一个词元”；互联网上的每一段文字都免费成为一个带标签的样本。SSL 是现代 AI 能够如此干净地规模化的原因。
05. Subject variability may kill foundation-model economics.
05. 受试者差异可能扼杀基础模型的经济逻辑。
Brains differ in anatomy, plasticity, and biochemistry. A decoder trained on one person rarely transfers cleanly to another.
大脑在解剖、可塑性和生物化学上各不相同。在一个人身上训练的解码器很少能干净地迁移到另一个人身上。
RESPONSE This is the core bet Section 10 traces in detail: that pretraining across many brains collapses the marginal cost of decoding a new one. If pretraining works for brains the way it works for language and vision, personalization becomes a short calibration step rather than a bespoke training process.
回应 这正是第 10 节详细追溯的核心押注：跨越许多大脑的预训练，将使解码一个新大脑的边际成本骤降。如果预训练对大脑的作用就像它对语言和视觉的作用一样，个性化就会变成一个短暂的校准步骤，而不是一个定制化的训练过程。
06. BCI has no parallel consumer market subsidizing its manufacturing iteration.
06. BCI 没有一个平行的消费市场来补贴其制造迭代。
Modern AI scaling was carried for over a decade by adjacent multi-billion-dollar markets that drove the underlying silicon, such as video games and crypto. They paid for the Nvidia GPU R&D that AI eventually needed.
现代 AI 的规模化，被相邻的、价值数十亿美元的市场（如电子游戏和加密货币）推动底层硅片发展了十多年。它们为英伟达的 GPU 研发买单，而 AI 最终需要这些研发。
RESPONSE The world is a densely connected graph. As a result, much of the hardware BCI will require is already being subsidized by adjacent industries: smartphone sensors, consumer-health wearables, mature CMOS, cheap optics, AR/VR, and decades of declining sensor costs. This is also going to be greatly amplified by the incoming rise of robotics. Some genuinely new technology will still be required, but most of the stack can be assembled from increasingly capable off-the-shelf hardware.
回应 世界是一张密集连接的图。因此，BCI 将需要的许多硬件，已经被相邻产业所补贴：智能手机传感器、消费健康可穿戴设备、成熟的 CMOS、廉价光学、AR/VR，以及数十年不断下降的传感器成本。即将到来的机器人崛起也将极大地放大这一点。仍将需要一些真正新的技术，但技术栈的大部分可以从日益强大的现成硬件中组装出来。
07. The consumer-EEG graveyard.
07. 消费级 EEG 的坟场。
Muse, Emotiv, NextMind, CTRL-labs, a handful of others: the last consumer non-invasive wave produced demos, acquisitions, and shutdowns, not a sustained product category.
Muse、Emotiv、NextMind、CTRL-labs，以及另外几家：上一波消费级非侵入浪潮产出的是演示、收购和关停，而不是一个可持续的产品品类。
RESPONSE The earlier wave tried to build products before the underlying stack was ready. There were no foundation models for the brain, no multi-modal sensor stacks, no shared protocols, no AI labs hungry for data, no cheap consumer-grade sensors. Just like most technologies, it does not work until it does; it is all about timing. The case today is not simply that “this time is different”, but that several missing pieces now exist.
回应 上一波试图在底层技术栈就绪之前就打造产品。当时没有大脑的基础模型，没有多模态传感器栈，没有共享协议，没有渴求数据的 AI 实验室，没有廉价的消费级传感器。就像大多数技术一样，它在奏效之前都不奏效；关键在于时机。今天的论点并不只是“这一次不同”，而是若干此前缺失的拼图现在已经存在。
08. Neural data may not be valuable enough to frontier AI labs.
08. 神经数据对前沿 AI 实验室可能不够有价值。
Labs already have enormous text and video corpora and are increasingly relying on synthetic data, reinforcement learning, and simulated environments. Noisy, expensive, consent-sensitive neural data may add too little marginal information to justify significant investment.
实验室已经拥有庞大的文本和视频语料库，并日益依赖合成数据、强化学习和模拟环境。嘈杂、昂贵、对同意敏感的神经数据，可能只增加太少的边际信息，不足以证明大量投资是合理的。
RESPONSE Neural data is potentially valuable because it captures aspects of cognition that text, video, and synthetic data can only expose indirectly: raw information processing from the brain. That could improve both capability and alignment (Section 17, Section 19). And as frontier labs approach the data wall and alignment becomes increasingly central, genuinely new modalities with a chance to address those issues become more valuable. But this remains an empirical bet: labs will need to test whether neural data produces measurable gains on capability and alignment.
回应 神经数据之所以可能有价值，是因为它捕捉到了文本、视频和合成数据只能间接暴露的认知层面：来自大脑的原始信息处理。这可能同时提升能力和对齐（第 17 节、第 19 节）。而当前沿实验室接近数据之墙、对齐日益成为核心时，有机会解决这些问题的真正新模态就会变得更有价值。但这仍然是一个经验性的押注：实验室需要检验神经数据是否能在能力和对齐上产生可测量的收益。
09. Neural data may be too private, too regulated, or too socially radioactive to become a frontier training modality.
09. 神经数据可能过于私密、监管过严，或在社会上过于“放射性”，而无法成为前沿训练模态。
People accept watches that log sleep and heart rate. They may feel differently about a device tracking attention, recognition, or emotional state. One sufficiently ugly scandal could set the field back years.
人们接受记录睡眠和心率的手表。他们对一台追踪注意力、识别或情绪状态的设备，可能会有不同的感受。一次足够难看的丑闻，就可能使该领域倒退数年。
RESPONSE Exactly why on-device preprocessing, differential privacy, and institutional legitimacy have to be designed into the technology from the start. The wrong version of this future would be extractive, opaque, and dangerous; the right one would be opt-in, auditable, and clearly valuable to the person generating the data. Once again, if the field treats neural data like ad-tech data, it deserves to fail.
回应 这正是为什么设备端预处理、差分隐私和制度合法性必须从一开始就被设计进这项技术。这一未来的错误版本将是榨取性的、不透明的、危险的；正确的版本将是自愿加入的、可审计的，并且对产生数据的人明显有价值。再一次，如果这个领域像对待广告技术数据那样对待神经数据，它就应当失败。
10. The real human bottleneck may be comprehension, not communication bandwidth.
10. 真正的人类瓶颈可能是理解，而不是通信带宽。
A faster pipe does not make the biological brain faster at understanding, judging, or verifying superhuman reasoning. A thousand-fold increase in input bandwidth could simply become a firehose into a bathtub. If comprehension is the binding constraint, BCI tweaks the wrong knob.
一根更快的管道并不能让生物大脑更快地理解、判断或核验超人类的推理。输入带宽提高一千倍，可能只是变成向一个浴缸里喷入的消防水龙。如果理解才是约束性瓶颈，BCI 拧的就是错误的旋钮。
RESPONSE In the near term, BCIs will increase the practical bandwidth between humans and machines by: reducing the meaning lost in translation, enabling AI to adapt explanations to a user’s understanding in real time, and operating at the right level of abstraction rather than dumping raw reasoning into the mind. However, fundamentally, communication bandwidth will not increase human thinking and processing speed; and as AI’s thinking speed accelerates, BCI alone will not allow the biological brain to keep pace with AI. This manifesto’s claim is that BCI will only be the first step towards closing this gap, it will catalyze advances across neurotechnology that will then collectively help close the human-machine gap. As the mind’s bottleneck illustrates, communication bandwidth is only one of two bottlenecks, and it will be the first and most tractable one to address. Nevertheless, over a longer time horizon, neurotechnology will also offer a path to addressing the second bottleneck: cognitive speed; through neural augmentation and brain emulation, we will have a path to overcome the biological limits of cognition itself.
回应 在近期，BCI 将通过以下方式提高人与机器之间的实际带宽：减少翻译中流失的意义，使 AI 能够实时根据用户的理解调整解释，以及在正确的抽象层次上运作，而不是把原始推理倾倒进心智。然而，从根本上说，通信带宽不会提高人类的思维和处理速度；随着 AI 思维速度加快，仅靠 BCI 也无法让生物大脑跟上 AI。本宣言的主张是：BCI 将只是弥合这一差距的第一步，它将催化整个神经技术的进步，而这些进步随后将共同帮助弥合人机差距。正如心智瓶颈所表明的，通信带宽只是两个瓶颈中的一个，而且将是第一个、也是最容易处理的一个。不过，在更长的时间视野上，神经技术也将提供一条应对第二个瓶颈——认知速度——的路径：通过神经增强和脑仿真，我们将拥有一条克服认知本身生物学极限的道路。
11. A better human-machine interface does not solve alignment on its own.
11. 更好的人机界面并不能独自解决对齐问题。
At best, it improves how clearly humans can express intent, preference, and correction. But alignment is ultimately about what the system is optimizing for, and a more capable interface may be only tangential to that problem. A misaligned system does not become safe simply because it can understand us better.
充其量，它改善了人类表达意图、偏好和纠正的清晰程度。但对齐归根结底关乎系统在优化什么，一个更有能力的界面可能只与这个问题相切。一个未对齐的系统，并不会仅仅因为它能更好地理解我们就变得安全。
RESPONSE A better interface is not the complete alignment solution, but it is nonetheless a significant part of it. Neurotechnology could improve reinforcement learning, provide richer signals of human intent and preference, surface confusion or disagreement, and overall help train models that better understand conflicting and complex human values (see Section 19). BCI, and neurotechnology more generally, is one of the only technologies with a real shot at enabling alignment-by-integration before the Phase Lock Window closes, its development should therefore be prioritized accordingly.
回应 更好的界面并不是完整的对齐解决方案，但它仍然是其中重要的一部分。神经技术可以改进强化学习，提供关于人类意图和偏好的更丰富信号，显现困惑或分歧，并总体上帮助训练出更好地理解相互冲突而复杂的人类价值观的模型（见第 19 节）。BCI 以及更广义的神经技术，是少数有真正机会在相位锁定窗口关闭之前实现融合式对齐的技术之一，因此其发展应当被相应优先考虑。
12. A neural interface may strengthen the machine more than the human.
12. 神经接口可能强化机器的程度超过强化人类。
The AI could use access to attention, emotion, uncertainty, and preference to model us with extraordinary precision, while our ability to understand or supervise it improves only marginally. The same channel meant to preserve human control could instead become a powerful tool for persuasion and manipulation.
人工智能可以利用对注意力、情绪、不确定性和偏好的获取，以非凡的精度为我们建模，而我们理解或监督它的能力却只得到边际提升。同一条本意在保全人类控制的通道，反而可能成为强大的说服和操纵工具。
RESPONSE This is a real danger. Better access to the human mind can improve oversight, but it can also make AI persuasion far more potent. The solution is to not assume neurotechnology will naturally favor the human; but instead to be intentional in our development and design of the technology to mitigate those risks.
回应 这是一种真正的危险。更好地获取人类心智可以改善监督，但也可以使 AI 的说服力强大得多。解决方案不是假定神经技术会自然地有利于人类；而是要在这项技术的开发与设计中有意识地减轻这些风险。
13. Neural feedback may optimize for impulses rather than values.
13. 神经反馈可能优化的是冲动，而不是价值观。
Fast neural reactions are not the same as reflective preferences. They contain novelty, fear, arousal, bias, addiction, and immediate reward. Treating them as biological ground truth could turn RLHF’s existing failure modes into something much stronger: models optimized not for what humans endorse, but for what most effectively lights up the human reward system.
快速的神经反应并不等同于反思性的偏好。它们包含新奇、恐惧、唤醒、偏见、成瘾和即时奖励。把它们当作生物地面实况，可能把 RLHF 现有的失败模式变成某种更强的东西：模型所优化的不是人类所认可的，而是最有效地点亮人类奖励系统的东西。
RESPONSE Neural signals should indeed not be treated as unquestionable ground truth for training, their value is as one signal among many. Used carefully, they may help detect confusion, error, or hidden disagreement; used naively as a reward target, they become another Goodhart’s failure69, optimizing for stimulation rather than human values. The solution here is to remain mindful of the way we make AI ingest this new signal.
回应 神经信号确实不应当被当作无可置疑的训练地面实况，它们的价值在于作为诸多信号中的一种。审慎使用时，它们可能有助于检测困惑、错误或隐藏的分歧；若被天真地当作奖励目标，它们就会变成又一次古德哈特失败⟦69⟧——优化的是刺激，而不是人类价值观。这里的解决方案是，对我们让 AI 摄取这一新信号的方式保持清醒。
We are currently navigating treacherous waters. Blindly following a roadmap drafted with information from 2026 is a recipe for disaster. The map is not the territory, and any attempt to project decades of technological evolution is fundamentally an exercise in probabilistic estimation, offering a set of strong priors that must be ruthlessly updated as empirical reality delivers new data. As the neurotechnology landscape matures and the fog clears, we must remain relentlessly willing to re- assess our trajectory and critically question whether neurotechnology remains a net-positive technology to continue developing.
我们目前正在穿越险恶的水域。盲目遵循一份用 2026 年信息起草的路线图，是灾难的配方。地图不是疆域，任何试图投射数十年技术演化的尝试，从根本上都是一次概率估算练习，它提供的是一组必须在经验现实交付新数据时被无情更新的强先验。随着神经技术版图成熟、迷雾散去，我们必须始终愿意重新评估我们的轨迹，并批判性地追问：神经技术是否仍然是一项值得继续发展的净正面技术。
69. Goodhart’s law: when a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure; optimize too hard for a proxy and it stops tracking the thing you cared about.
69. 古德哈特定律（Goodhart’s law）：当一项指标变成目标时，它就不再是一项好的指标；对一个替代指标优化得太狠，它就会停止追踪你真正在乎的东西。
Neurotechnologies are not a panacea. Even a flawless, zero-latency system capable of reading and writing a human mind with perfect fidelity still leaves us with an immense, daunting mountain of alignment, ethical, and architectural problems to solve. It is only one piece of a vastly larger puzzle.
神经技术不是万能药。即便是一个毫无瑕疵、零延迟、能够以完美保真度读取和写入人类心智的系统，仍然留给我们一座需要解决的、巨大而令人生畏的对齐、伦理和架构问题之山。它只是一幅巨大得多的拼图中的一块。
However, if we ground ourselves in first principles and project out into the long-term horizon, say the year 2060, BCI will be a prerequisite for humanity to remain consequential. Once machines operate at a scale a million times more capable than biological humanity, able to execute, reason, and iterate better than us in every conceivable domain, establishing a robust, high-bandwidth control and interaction surface will be an absolute necessity.
然而，如果我们立足于第一性原理，并向长期视野投射——比如说 2060 年——BCI 将成为人类保持其重要性的先决条件。一旦机器以比生物人类强大一百万倍的规模运作，能够在每一个可以想象的领域比我们更好地执行、推理和迭代，建立一个稳健的高带宽控制与交互面，将成为绝对的必要。
Let us now turn to what the coming decade could look like if we make the right choices.
现在让我们转向：如果我们做出正确的选择，未来十年可能是什么样子。
24 The Decade Ahead
24 未来十年
“The future is already here; it’s just not very evenly distributed.” — William Gibson
“未来已经到来；它只是分布得还不太均匀。”——威廉·吉布森（William Gibson）
We tend to look at humanity’s greatest achievements as singular, explosive events: the day the bomb dropped, the day the internet turned on, the day the first LLM went viral. But hindsight is a terrible historian, always eager to dramatize and compress events. In reality, deep technological shifts happen more like water freezing: a long, invisible drop in temperature, then the first sudden slush, and then a thousand small crystals locking into place until, seemingly all at once, the ground beneath you is solid ice.
我们往往把人类最伟大的成就看作单一的、爆炸性的事件：炸弹落下的那天，互联网接通的那天，第一个大语言模型走红的那天。但事后之见是一个糟糕的历史学家，总是急于戏剧化和压缩事件。实际上，深层的技术转变更像水结冰：一段漫长、看不见的降温，然后是第一场突然的雪泥，然后是一千个小晶体锁定到位，直到似乎在一瞬间，你脚下的地面变成了坚实的冰。
The next ten years of neurotech will follow the same narrative, the same one we just witnessed with language models: a long quiet under-the-radar phase, then sudden visibility, followed by ubiquity, and finally the strangeness that comes with a new world order.
神经技术的未来十年将遵循同样的叙事，就是我们刚刚在语言模型上见证过的那一套：一段漫长而安静的、不为人注意的阶段，然后突然可见，随后无处不在，最终是新世界秩序所带来的那种奇异。
We now turn to the future. The next ten years will likely be the most transformative in human history. AI will give machines intelligence, robotics will give them bodies, and BCI will begin connecting them directly to the human mind. The Old World is still standing, but the scaffolding of the new one is already everywhere.
我们现在转向未来。未来十年很可能是人类历史上最具变革性的十年。AI 将赋予机器智能，机器人技术将赋予它们身体，而 BCI 将开始把它们直接连接到人类心智。旧世界仍在，但新世界的脚手架已经无处不在。
2026–2028: First slowly
2026–2028：起初缓慢
The next 24 months will look the way 2018 to 2020 looked in AI from the inside. Calm, but accelerating.
接下来的 24 个月，将看起来像 2018 到 2020 年在 AI 内部看起来的样子。平静，但在加速。
The first usable foundation models for brain decoding will ship publicly, trained on EEG, ultrasound or MEG. Frontier AI labs will start exploring the use of neural data. At the same time, the first generation of non-invasive consumer devices a healthy adult would actually choose to wear will begin shipping at scale, somewhere between sub-vocal text decoding and continuous attention monitoring.
第一批可用的脑解码基础模型将公开发布，在 EEG、超声或 MEG 上训练。前沿 AI 实验室将开始探索神经数据的使用。与此同时，第一代健康成年人实际上会选择佩戴的非侵入式消费级设备将开始大规模出货，其能力介于默读文本解码和持续注意力监测之间。
BCI will leave its purely academic, medical niche and start bleeding into the broader culture. You hear friends bring it up at dinner, a high-production documentary drops, a YouTube deep-dive goes viral. It will stop feeling like a sci-fi trope and start feeling more like an impending reality.
BCI 将离开其纯粹学术、医疗的小众地带，开始渗入更广泛的文化。你会听到朋友在晚餐时提起它，一部高制作水准的纪录片上线，一段 YouTube 深度解析走红。它将不再感觉像一个科幻套路，而开始感觉更像一个即将到来的现实。
As the tech matures, impressive lab results begin to stack up. Teams demonstrate the ability to decode complex language, reconstruct visual images from the visual cortex, and map fine-grained user preferences directly from neural signals. This compounding momentum changes how capital and talent move, top-tier software engineers and AI researchers who were entirely focused on LLMs start pivoting their careers toward the human machine interface.
随着技术成熟，令人印象深刻的实验室结果开始堆积。团队展示解码复杂语言、从视觉皮层重建视觉图像、以及直接从神经信号测绘细粒度用户偏好的能力。这种不断累积的势头改变了资本和人才的流向，那些原本完全专注于大语言模型的顶尖软件工程师和 AI 研究者，开始把职业生涯转向人机界面。
The gravitational pull of BCI is growing.
BCI 的引力正在增长。
Crucially, this technology accelerates a shift that AI already started, the human role continues to drift away from being an operator, and toward being a source of judgment, taste, priority, and intent.
至关重要的是，这项技术加速了 AI 已经开始的一场转变：人类的角色继续从操作者漂移，转向成为判断、品味、优先级和意图的来源。
By early 2028, a frontier AI lab will announce a neural-data partnership, explicitly framing it as a training modality or alignment investment. Shortly after, the first landmark peer-reviewed paper will drop, showing that brain-tuning data directly improves a flagship model’s reasoning or alignment metrics.
到 2028 年初，一家前沿 AI 实验室将宣布一项神经数据合作，并明确将其定位为一种训练模态或对齐投资。此后不久，第一篇具有里程碑意义的同行评审论文将发表，表明脑调控数据直接改善了某款旗舰模型的推理或对齐指标。
By the end of 2028, the calm phase ends. It will become blindingly obvious to anyone paying attention that BCI is moving much faster than the public consensus expected. The dam will begin to break.
到 2028 年底，平静阶段结束。对任何关注的人来说，都将变得刺眼地明显：BCI 的前进速度远快于公众共识所预期的。大坝将开始决口。
2028–2031: And then suddenly
2028–2031：然后突然
The late 2020s and early 2030s will look the way 2022 to 2025 looked in AI. Loud.
2020 年代后期和 2030 年代初期，将看起来像 2022 到 2025 年在 AI 领域看起来的样子。喧嚣。
The first BCI primitive will hit 10 million active users. Sub-vocal text will begin displacing voice commands and physical typing for short interactions. Some people will start reading mail, sending messages, and dictating notes entirely in their heads, the same way mobile typing displaced laptop typing a decade earlier. BCI will also become a more central tool for alignment, and the first peer- reviewed demonstrations that brain-tuning improves frontier-model metrics on standardized benchmarks will happen around 2028 or 2029. This is the era where people stop calling neurotechnology an “emerging” field. By 2031, BCI will hit its Tesla moment: a flagship consumer product that the general public can name will appear, making the technology mainstream.
第一种 BCI 原语将达到 1,000 万活跃用户。默读文本将开始在短交互中取代语音指令和物理打字。有些人将开始完全在头脑中阅读邮件、发送消息和口授笔记，就像十年前手机打字取代笔记本电脑打字一样。BCI 也将成为对齐更核心的工具，第一批证明脑调控能改善前沿模型在标准化基准上的指标的同行评审演示，将发生在 2028 或 2029 年左右。这是人们不再把神经技术称为“新兴”领域的时代。到 2031 年，BCI 将迎来它的“特斯拉时刻”：一款普通公众能叫得出名字的旗舰消费级产品将出现，使这项技术进入主流。
As this transition happens, the hardware will iteratively vanish from daily life. The form factor will shrink into earbuds, a beanie, the temples of AR glasses, a hat or a simple woven headband. It becomes wearable all day, practically invisible, and socially acceptable.
随着这一转变发生，硬件将迭代地从日常生活中消失。形态将缩小到耳塞、一顶毛线帽、AR 眼镜的镜腿、一顶帽子，或一条简单的编织头带。它变得可以全天佩戴，几乎看不见，并且在社交上可被接受。
BCI will start embedding itself deeper into the social fabric. People begin using it more regularly at work and in social settings. It shows up more often in movies, comes up more often in politics, and public figures start talking about it.
BCI 将开始更深地嵌入社会结构。人们开始更经常地在工作中和社交场合使用它。它更频繁地出现在电影里，更频繁地出现在政治中，公众人物开始谈论它。
As BCI improves, it shifts from decoding coarse commands to decoding fine-grained intent. You no longer have to formulate a sentence to trigger an action, it becomes possible to interact with AI using only your thoughts, and starts to become more effective than typing or speaking. Thought-to-text and thought-to-speech become familiar concepts inside AI labs, and “how do we make LLMs easier to interface with through the brain?” becomes a hot topic.
随着 BCI 改进，它从解码粗粒度指令转向解码细粒度意图。你不再需要组织一个句子来触发一个行动，只用思想与 AI 交互成为可能，并开始变得比打字或说话更有效。“思想转文字”和“思想转语音”成为 AI 实验室里熟悉的概念，“我们如何通过大脑让大语言模型更易于交互？”成为一个热门话题。
This unlocks capabilities that sound genuinely absurd by today’s standards. You think of a query, and your earbuds whisper the answer back. You beam a raw concept or a melody directly to a friend’s headset. You record memories, dreams or thoughts and play them back as images or videos. And with the rise of embodied AI and robotics you can even look at a domestic robot, think about a task, and watch the machine execute it with near zero friction.
这解锁了以今天的标准听起来真正荒唐的能力。你想到一个查询，耳塞就把答案轻声回给你。你把一个原始概念或一段旋律直接“传送”到朋友的头戴设备。你记录记忆、梦境或想法，并把它们作为图像或视频回放。而随着具身 AI 和机器人技术的兴起，你甚至可以看着一台家用机器人，想一项任务，然后看着机器以近乎零摩擦的方式执行它。
At this stage, a reasonable number of knowledge workers will have tried a non-invasive headset, much like VR today, except this time the technology actually works. Once people can touch it, wear it, and feel the interaction for themselves, the technology becomes tangible, shifting the public conversation from metaphysical speculation to an immediate reality. The public conversation will get more vivid overnight.
到了这个阶段，相当数量的知识工作者将已经试用过非侵入式头戴设备，很像今天的 VR，只是这一次技术真正奏效了。一旦人们能够触摸它、佩戴它，并亲自感受到这种交互，这项技术就变得可感，使公众对话从形而上学的推测转向眼前的现实。公众讨论将在一夜之间变得更加生动。
Simultaneously, the invasive side of the market will mature rapidly. Implants will scale from hundreds of clinical-trial patients to tens of thousands of medical users, nearing a hundred thousand by 2031.
与此同时，市场的侵入式一侧将迅速成熟。植入物将从数百名临床试验患者扩展到数万名医疗用户，到 2031 年接近十万。
Crucially, for invasive BCI, the delivery mechanisms will become far less barbaric as the field begins moving beyond the “skull drill”70. The industry will start moving away from open-brain surgery toward minimally invasive delivery. Companies like Synchron will deploy stents up the jugular vein71 to read the motor cortex from the inside of a blood vessel, avoiding the skull entirely. Competitors like Neuralink will advance techniques that avoid piercing the dura mater72, while others push toward flexible bioelectronics and injectable neural dust: tiny implanted sensors designed to record neural activity with less surgical trauma.73
至关重要的是，对于侵入式 BCI，递送机制将变得远不那么“野蛮”，因为该领域开始走出“颅骨钻孔”⟦70⟧。产业将开始从开颅手术转向微创递送。像 Synchron 这样的公司将通过颈静脉部署支架⟦71⟧，从血管内部读取运动皮层，从而完全避开颅骨。像 Neuralink 这样的竞争者将推进避免穿透硬脑膜⟦72⟧的技术，而其他公司则推向柔性生物电子和可注射的“神经尘埃”：设计用于以更少手术创伤记录神经活动的微型植入传感器。⟦73⟧
These years won’t just be about surreal technology; they will also be ruthlessly competitive. BCI will transition from a fun gadget to an economically necessary tool, and governments, massive corporations, and institutional capital will race to secure position. As the stakes rise, mental privacy will become a mainstream political fight, showing up on social media, in regulation, and in arguments at home. Neurorights will become normal policy language.
这些年将不只是关于超现实的技术；它们也将是无情地竞争的。BCI 将从一件有趣的小玩意转变为一种经济上必要的工具，政府、大型公司和机构资本将竞相抢占位置。随着赌注升高，精神隐私将成为主流的政治斗争，出现在社交媒体上、监管中，以及家庭争论里。神经权利将成为正常的政策语言。
70. For most of its history, serious invasive BCI meant opening the skull and placing electrodes on or in the brain. That may still produce the highest-fidelity signals, but it is a brutal onboarding flow for a technology that hopes to scale.
70. 在其历史的大部分时间里，严肃的侵入式 BCI 意味着打开颅骨并把电极放在大脑上或大脑内。这或许仍能产生最高保真的信号，但对于一项希望规模化的技术来说，这是一种残酷的接入流程。
71. As much as this sounds like a medieval torture technique, compared with opening the skull, going up the jugular vein is the civilized option: well understood and low risk. (Out of context, this is probably the kind of sentence which might sound slightly awkward in court)
71. 尽管这听起来像一种中世纪酷刑技术，但与打开颅骨相比，沿颈静脉进入是文明的选项：被充分理解，而且风险较低。（脱离语境来看，这句话大概是那种在法庭上听起来可能略显尴尬的句子。）
72. Neuralink’s architectural updates focus on deploying flexible threads without requiring a full breach of the protective dura mater, radically reducing surgical risk and recovery time.
72. Neuralink 的架构更新聚焦于在不需要完全突破起保护作用的硬脑膜的情况下部署柔性细丝，从而大幅降低手术风险和恢复时间。
73. Neural dust: millimeter- and eventually micrometer-scale wireless sensors placed near neurons, powered and read out by ultrasound. The appeal is to be able to record from inside the brain without wires or bulky implants.
73. 神经尘埃（neural dust）：毫米级、最终是微米级的无线传感器，放置在神经元附近，由超声供能和读出。其吸引力在于能够在没有导线或笨重植入物的情况下从大脑内部记录。
By 2029, cumulative venture funding in BCI will cross the $8 to $10 billion mark. Sub-vocal text will become a viable input for AI glasses, and the first wave of non-invasive consumer products will begin shipping at scale.
到 2029 年，BCI 的累计风险投资将跨越 80 到 100 亿美元的关口。默读文本将成为 AI 眼镜的一种可行输入，第一波非侵入式消费级产品将开始大规模出货。
By 2030, the industry will cross the 10-million shipped units threshold. This milestone triggers the arrival of a US federal neural-data framework with actual enforcement teeth, alongside the first frontier AI model cards explicitly disclosing neural data in their training mix.
到 2030 年，该产业将跨越出货 1,000 万台的门槛。这一里程碑将触发一个真正具有执行力的美国联邦神经数据框架的到来，同时第一批前沿 AI 模型卡将明确披露其训练混合中的神经数据。
That same year, the medical sector will hit 10,000 active invasive BCI implants globally, demonstrating the safety and scalability of surgical deployments.
同年，医疗部门将在全球达到 10,000 例活跃的侵入式 BCI 植入，证明手术部署的安全性和可规模化。
By 2031, the true Tesla moment arrives. Cognitive-state-aware AI assistants are firmly on a path to become the dominant, default interface to machines, permanently changing how humans interact with digital infrastructure, setting humanity on a path toward a full merge by the 2040s.
到 2031 年，真正的“特斯拉时刻”到来。感知认知状态的 AI 助手牢牢走上成为与机器交互的主导、默认界面的道路，永久改变人类与数字基础设施交互的方式，把人类置于一条通往 2040 年代全面融合的路径上。
2036, a Tuesday morning.
2036 年，一个星期二的早晨。
2031–2036: Ubiquity
2031–2036：无处不在
By the early 2030s, BCI crosses the threshold into true ubiquity, it is no longer a technology you have to explain or justify. Everyone has an opinion on it, everyone sees its footprint on society, and the initial debate over whether it is real dissolves entirely.
到 2030 年代初期，BCI 跨过真正无处不在的门槛，它不再是一种你必须解释或辩护的技术。每个人都对它有看法，每个人都看到它在社会上的足迹，关于它是否真实的最初辩论彻底消散。
The most profound shift of this era is the evolution from prompting an AI to thinking with an AI. Collaboration turns into symbiosis, the days of pulling out a phone to type a query start to look ancient. Instead, the AI runs as a continuous background process alongside your mind. The boundary between your biological memory and the machine’s data retrieval begins to blur.
这个时代最深刻的转变，是从向 AI 发出提示，演化为与 AI 一起思考。协作变成共生，掏出手机去输入一个查询的日子开始显得古老。相反，AI 作为与你的心智并行的连续后台过程运行。你的生物记忆与机器的数据检索之间的边界开始模糊。
This creates a true cognitive exoskeleton. Just as the smartphone turned humanity into a primitive, high-latency cyborg network in the 2010s, AI and BCI create a low-latency, always-on exocortex. It amplifies human capability at a deeper, more structural level than any screen ever could.
这创造出一具真正的认知外骨骼。正如智能手机在 2010 年代把人类变成一个原始的、高延迟的赛博格网络一样，AI 和 BCI 创造出一个低延迟、始终在线的外皮层。它在比任何屏幕都更深、更结构性的层面上放大人类能力。
This transformation does not hit everyone at once. The future remains unevenly distributed, and a large portion of the population continues to work and live using the baseline tools of the mid-2020s. But for an ever-increasing, hyper-productive segment of the global workforce, operating without this cognitive layer feels like trying to run a modern business without electricity.
这一转变并不会一次性击中所有人。未来仍然分布不均，很大一部分人口继续使用 2020 年代中期的基线工具工作和生活。但对于全球劳动力中一个不断增大、生产力极高的群体来说，没有这一认知层去运作，感觉就像试图在没有电的情况下经营一家现代企业。
At the same time, computing transitions from episodic to ambient. The headset or implant reads your cognitive state continuously, not just when you consciously issue a command. AI agents monitor your cognitive load, attention levels, and mental fatigue in real time. If a complex technical task spikes your stress, the system can automatically dial back distractions, curate your digital layout, and surface the exact reference material you need before you even realize you are stuck. Prompting becomes a relic of the early AI era; ambient computing becomes the new default.
与此同时，计算从片段式转向环境式。头戴设备或植入物持续读取你的认知状态，而不仅仅是在你有意识地发出指令时。AI 智能体实时监测你的认知负荷、注意力水平和精神疲劳。如果一项复杂的技术任务使你的压力飙升，系统可以自动减少干扰、整理你的数字布局，并在你甚至意识到自己卡住之前，就把你需要的精确参考资料呈现出来。发出提示成为早期 AI 时代的遗物；环境计算成为新的默认。
This is also the moment where the “write” side of neurotech starts picking up real commercial momentum. Up until now, the field was heavily dominated by high-fidelity reading. By the middle of the decade, precision neuromodulation procedures begin moving into the consumer mainstream, becoming as harmless as LASIK surgery. It is now possible to write precise thoughts and feelings to the brain. By 2037, the first augmented adults will think measurably faster than their own unaugmented selves.
这也是神经技术“写入”一侧开始获得真正商业动能的时刻。直到那时，该领域在很大程度上由高保真读取所主导。到这个十年中期，精确的神经调控手术开始进入消费主流，变得像 LASIK 手术一样无害。现在已经可以向大脑写入精确的想法和感受。到 2037 年，第一批增强后的成年人将比未经增强的自己思考得可测量地更快。
Ultimately, the early 2030s is the point where four massive, independent technologies collide. High- bandwidth non-invasive BCI, frontier artificial intelligence, large-scale humanoid robotics, and early brain-writing capabilities. All at once, they stop being separate industries. They fuse into a single, compounding tech stack. Mirroring the way smartphones, the cloud, mobile broadband, and app stores combined in the early 2010s to create the modern digital world.
归根结底，2030 年代初期是四项巨大、彼此独立的技术相撞的时刻。高带宽非侵入式 BCI、前沿人工智能、大规模人形机器人，以及早期的脑写入能力。它们一下子不再是分离的产业。它们融合成一个单一的、不断复利的技术栈。这映照了智能手机、云、移动宽带和应用商店在 2010 年代初期结合、创造出当今数字世界的方式。
Over time, read becomes read-and-write, industrial and humanoid robotics systems adopt BCI as their natural control interface, the bandwidth bottleneck between human intent and machine execution closes in earnest, and the broader AI alignment problem makes significant progress, in large part thanks to advances in neurotechnology.
随着时间推移，读取变成“读取并写入”，工业和人形机器人系统把 BCI 采纳为它们的自然控制界面，人类意图与机器执行之间的带宽瓶颈真正关闭，而更广泛的 AI 对齐问题取得重大进展，这在很大程度上要归功于神经技术的进步。
By 2032, direct robotic control through BCI becomes a highly credible, multi-billion-dollar enterprise category. The same year, the first heavily capitalized neurotech conglomerate begins offering fully bidirectional, consumer-grade read-and-write systems designed specifically for healthy adults.
到 2032 年，通过 BCI 直接控制机器人成为一个高度可信、价值数十亿美元的企业品类。同年，第一家资本雄厚的神经技术集团开始提供专为健康成年人设计的、完全双向的消费级读写系统。
The gravitational pull of BCI is now second only to a few technologies; alongside AI and robotics, it redefines how the world operates.
BCI 的引力现在仅次于少数几项技术；与 AI 和机器人技术一道，它重新定义了世界的运作方式。
2036 onwards: The strange phase
2036 年以降：奇异阶段
After 2036, the timeline loses its linear feel. We cross a civilizational horizon where the boundary between human and machine disappears.
2036 年之后，时间线失去了线性的感觉。我们跨过一条文明的地平线，人与机器之间的边界消失了。
The catalyst is the Write revolution. For 20 years, neurotech was nearly exclusively about listening to the brain. Now, we talk back. The industry figures out how to insert rich sensory data, structured concepts, and motor skills directly into the cortex. The Matrix skill upload74 transitions from a sci-fi trope to an emerging feature of reality.
催化剂是“写入革命”。二十年来，神经技术几乎完全是在倾听大脑。现在，我们回话了。产业弄清了如何把丰富的感官数据、结构化的概念和运动技能直接插入皮层。《黑客帝国》式的技能上传⟦74⟧ 从科幻套路转变为现实中一项正在涌现的功能。
On a daily basis, this manifests as a fully programmable state of mind. You gain granular control over your own neurochemistry. You tap a screen, or just issue an internal intent, to induce instant, restorative sleep, mute an adrenaline spike before a presentation, or lock into a flawless four-hour hyper-focus state without pharmaceuticals.
在日常层面，这表现为一种完全可编程的心智状态。你获得对自己神经化学的精细控制。你点一下屏幕，或只是发出一个内部意图，就能诱发即时的、恢复性的睡眠，在演讲前消除肾上腺素飙升，或在没有药物的情况下锁定进入一个完美的四小时超专注状态。
But as the technical barriers fall, the philosophical ones become existential. When you can rewrite a mental state at will, the concept of the self fractures. If a machine can dial down your anxiety or inject a memory, where do the boundaries of your original identity end? Humanity is forced to invent entirely new legal and moral categories as the mind is no longer an unmappable black box.
但随着技术障碍倒下，哲学障碍变得关乎存亡。当你能够随意改写一种心理状态时，自我的概念就会碎裂。如果一台机器可以调低你的焦虑或注入一段记忆，你原初身份的边界止于何处？随着心智不再是一个无法测绘的黑箱，人类被迫发明全新的法律和道德范畴。
We are now navigating dangerous, uncharted territories.
我们现在正在穿越危险的、未经测绘的领地。
This convergence enables true proto-telepathy. For millennia, human collaboration has been bottlenecked by language, an incredibly low-bandwidth, lossy compression format for thought.
这种汇合使真正的“原初心灵感应”（proto-telepathy）成为可能。几千年来，人类协作一直受限于语言——一种带宽极低、对思想有损压缩的格式。
74. The Matrix skill upload: the scene where Neo has kung fu loaded straight into his brain and wakes up knowing it. Great movie; do me a favor and go check out the clip.
74. 《黑客帝国》式的技能上传：尼奥（Neo）把功夫直接加载进大脑、醒来就已掌握的那一幕。很棒的电影；帮我一个忙，去看看这个片段。
Proto-telepathy: A whole idea transmitted without being translated into words.
原初心灵感应：一个完整的想法被传输，而无需被翻译成词语。
With bidirectional communication, we unlock conceptual sharing. Instead of spending an hour translating a complex spatial map or nuanced feeling into words, you transmit the raw, multi- dimensional concept directly to a collaborator’s mind. They do not just hear your explanation; they grasp the mental model exactly as it exists in your head.
有了双向通信，我们解锁了概念共享。你不必花一个小时把一幅复杂的空间地图或一种细腻的感受翻译成词语，而是把原始的、多维的概念直接传输到合作者的心智中。他们不只是听到你的解释；他们把握的是这个心智模型在你头脑中存在的精确形态。
For better or worse, emotions themselves become transmissible, and real-time transmission of emotional states challenges the very nature of human isolation. When you can feel the precise texture of another person’s grief or fear, human conflict and relationship dynamics undergo a brutal structural rewrite.
无论是好是坏，情绪本身变得可传输，情绪状态的实时传输挑战着人类孤立的本质本身。当你能够感受到另一个人悲伤或恐惧的精确质地时，人类的冲突和关系动态将经历一次残酷的结构性改写。
As the years roll forward, the loop between mind and machine tightens into a complete symbiosis. The baseline definition of the impossible keeps shifting, leaving the twentieth-century world feeling like a distant memory.
随着岁月向前滚动，心智与机器之间的回路收紧成完整的共生。对不可能的基线定义不断移动，使二十世纪的世界感觉像一段遥远的记忆。
Maya in 2038
2038 年的玛雅
Maya wakes up. The subtle neural node behind her ear already knows she slept poorly; her morning agent has rescheduled her 9:00 AM meeting before she has even opened her eyes.
玛雅醒来。她耳后那枚不起眼的神经节点已经知道她睡得很差；她的晨间助手在她甚至还没睁开眼睛之前，就已经改期了上午 9:00 的会议。
She forms the internal intent for coffee, and the kitchen downstairs hums to life.
她形成要咖啡的内部意图，楼下的厨房随之嗡嗡启动。
The first hour of work used to be emails. Now it is an unvoiced conversation. She thinks toward her assistant, the assistant thinks back, and the rhythm of the exchange runs at the speed of inner thought. The decoding model does not read the words she would have typed, but the raw, uncompressed shape of what she thinks.
工作的第一个小时曾经是处理邮件。现在，它是一场不出声的对话。她朝助手思考，助手思考回来，交换的节奏以内心思维的速度运行。解码模型读取的不是她本来会打出的词语，而是她所想之物原始、未经压缩的形态。
When her attention drifts toward a lingering problem from yesterday, the assistant seamlessly surfaces the relevant context without being asked. When a drafted response from the AI is slightly off, Maya’s recognition of the wrongness reaches the feedback loop milliseconds before her physical hands could ever move. She corrects the model in real time, without ever articulating the correction.
当她的注意力飘向昨天一个挥之不去的问题时，助手在未被要求的情况下无缝呈现相关上下文。当 AI 起草的一份回复略有偏差时，玛雅对这种“不对劲”的识别，比她的双手可能移动的速度快了若干毫秒，就到达了反馈回路。她实时纠正模型，却从未把纠正说出口。
When she needs to recall a specific metric or look up a complex financial projection, she does not feel like she is searching an external database. She simply feels like she suddenly remembers the answer, it is seamlessly injected into her mind. The boundary between “a human thought” and “a machine thought” within any given reasoning has blurred.
当她需要回忆一个特定指标或查阅一份复杂的财务预测时，她并不觉得自己在搜索一个外部数据库。她只是觉得自己突然想起了答案，它被无缝注入她的心智。“一个人类想法”和“一个机器想法”在任何给定推理中的边界已经模糊。
By lunchtime, Maya has cleared a volume of creative and analytical work that, in 2026, would have taken her more than a week. But it does not feel special at all, it just feels like a normal Tuesday.
到午饭时，玛雅已经完成了在 2026 年会花她一个多星期的创造性和分析性工作量。但这完全不觉得特别，它只是感觉像一个普通的星期二。
The infrastructure that made all of this ordinary arrived in increments. For years, the typical knowledge worker wore a non-invasive headset every single day. By 2035, the surgical and regulatory floor for elective, low-risk neural modules dropped to where laser eye surgery sat a decade earlier. What began as medical restoration for late-stage ALS and Parkinson’s patients slowly became a standard surgical upgrade. Maya got hers last year, right after her close friend did.
使这一切变得平常的基础设施，是分阶段到来的。多年来，典型的知识工作者每天都佩戴非侵入式头戴设备。到 2035 年，选择性、低风险神经模块的手术和监管门槛，降到了十年前激光近视手术所处的位置。它起初是为晚期渐冻症和帕金森病患者所做的医疗重建，慢慢变成了标准的手术升级。玛雅去年装上了自己的那枚，就在她的一位密友装上之后。
Now, she no longer remembers what it felt like to have to compress every thought through her thumbs. The small, bidirectional module interfacing directly with her cortex is now an inherent part of her life and her self. It even helps with memory consolidation overnight; she sleeps deeper and dreams more vividly.
现在，她已不再记得把每一个想法通过拇指压缩出去是什么感觉。那个直接与她皮层交互的小型双向模块，现在是她生活和自我固有的一部分。它甚至在夜间帮助记忆巩固；她睡得更深，梦也更鲜明。
Late in the afternoon, Maya steps away from her digital flow to paint. She picked up the hobby recently, though the learning process looked nothing like it would have a decade ago. She did not spend months failing at perspective or color theory. Instead, she acquired the foundational muscle memory and spatial understanding directly through her neural node. As she waits for a layer of oil to dry, her mind drifts to a dense philosophy book on visual aesthetics. She never actually sat down to read it, she simply accepted the conceptual transfer over the weekend, and by morning, the arguments were entirely legible to her.
